# SYNAPSE review — 2026-09-18

*Produced by a 23-agent read-only review (`wf_20b505d1-443`, 4,209,772 subagent tokens, 1,530 tool
uses, 25 min). Eleven surveyors, one adversarial refuter per dimension, one completeness critic.
Read-only by construction: nothing here was fixed by the review itself. HEAD `b7592a9a`, v5.75.1.*

## The refuter scoreboard

Every load-bearing claim was re-run by an agent that did not gather it, and that could only
return CONFIRMED by personally reproducing the evidence. Of **170 claims re-run**:

- **CONFIRMED** — 103 (61%)
- **CORRECTED** — 62 (36%)
- **REFUTED** — 5 (3%)

**39% of load-bearing claims did not survive first
contact unchanged.** Read that as the cost of trusting a single unchecked pass, not as noise.

---

## Read this first — the single biggest risk

> **Nothing that runs automatically can see the runtime the product actually ships into. Every green signal in this project is measured in an environment no artist uses — stock CPython on Linux/macOS — while the product runs hython on Windows inside Houdini 22.0.400. There is no automatic backstop in the real environment, which is why skipping the human gates is fatal here rather than merely untidy.**

I picked this over the louder findings because it is the mechanism BEHIND most of them, and because each dimension found one instance of it and filed it as a local defect — nobody summed it, so it has the least attention relative to its consequence. The consequence is not one bug: it is that the project cannot detect a regression in its own product until a human remembers to look, and for the last two releases the human did not. Concretely, the agent-facing phantom gate is not merely stale but structurally pinned to the wrong build forever on that path, and it ships that way in the installer payload to every artist.

**Evidence.** (1) Scope: .github/workflows/ci.yml is the only workflow; line 109 is `python -m pytest tests/ -m "not needs_houdini"`; matrix is ubuntu/macos x 3.11/3.14 — no Windows leg, no Houdini leg, and `grep -nE 'schedule|cron|workflow_dispatch'` returns nothing. (2) The automatic hooks run no tests: `git config core.hooksPath` = C:/Users/User/SYNAPSE/harness/githooks, and `grep -cE 'pytest|installer' harness/githooks/pre-push harness/githooks/pre-commit` returns 0 and 0. (3) A verifier that reports failure and passes anyway: I ran `python harness/verify/version_agreement.py` — it prints `LOPCAT FAIL (catalog lop_solaris_knowledge_22.json is stamped 22.0.368 but the introspected runtime authority h22_symbol_table.json is stamped 22.0.400)` and `echo $?` returns 0; `grep -rn 'version_agreement' .github/` returns nothing. (4) The suite ratchet and phantom scanner in harness/verify/checks.py have no CI invoker — every hit outside the file itself is an ad-hoc harness script (harness/notes/h22/postmerge_verify.ps1). (5) The install qualification is human: docs/RELEASE_CARD.md:36-53 steps 6-12 including `pytest installer\tests` at step 12, and `grep -n 'release_ci_gate' docs/RELEASE_CARD.md` exits 1 — the CI gate is absent from the checklist an operator follows. (6) What that permits, measured first-hand: v5.75.0 (43ef30ca) shipped with `gh api .../check-runs` returning `test (macos-latest, 3.11) failure` and the other three `cancelled`. (7) The permanent version of the same disease: scout.py:139 hardcodes the H21 table and :652-656 documents 'No major obtainable (headless stock python, no host) -> the H21 table, unchanged', while scout is deliberately zero-hou — so on the MCP path the gate answers `exists_in_runtime: false` for hou.undos.group with `gate_armed: true`, which I reproduced live. (8) installer/build_payload.py:16-17 ships that table and the 74 MB H21-majority corpus to every artist.

**Weighed against:** Recall serving confidently-wrong project context (memory-substrate). Worse per-incident consequence — a superseded Solaris v3 recipe returned at 0.70 for a Karma question, a pure-stopword query at 0.90, and the keyword scorer untouched since 2026-05-31 while six waves of memory work went past it. I ranked it second on two grounds. First, attention: it got a full dimension, a detailed refuter pass and a 30-test incident fix (ae34ed96), so it is the opposite of unattended. Second, exposure: its own refuter attributed all 54 'Vector recall:' log lines and found only two are production, both on 2026-09-16, both BEFORE the wiring fix landed at 2026-09-17 18:04:36 — zero production recalls since. It is a loaded gun that has not been fired. My pick is a gun that fires on every release. · Release-ritual collapse (release-state). Loudest finding in the set — three 'blocker' claims, a CI-red tag, a bypassed tag gate, no cut directory, no CHANGELOG entry. I ranked it third because it is a symptom of my pick, not a peer of it. The refuter's own correction proves this: the suite was RED on that exact commit, on those exact files, and shipped anyway because release_ci_gate.py — the one thing that reads that signal — was never invoked. The tooling saw it; nothing automatic was holding the door. Fix the ritual and the next lapse still ships, because the only backstop is memory.

## Trajectory — **ACCUMULATING**

The product converges; the project accumulates, and the accumulating half is the half that decides production-readiness. Weight: I measured the tracked tree at 210.0 MB across 5,378 files — harness/ 86.8 MB (41.4%) and rag/ 74.4 MB (35.4%) against python/ at 27.5 MB (13.1%). 77% of this repository by weight is evidence and corpus. Flow: over six weeks (`git log --since=2026-08-07 --numstat`, 835 commits) the additions are rag +2,516,706, harness +659,508, tests +56,592, python +43,944, docs +21,226 — harness and corpus outgrew product code by ~72x, with zero directory deletions under harness/. Verification did not move at all in the same window: the shipping/hython leg is stale by 34 days, 659 commits AND a different Houdini build (baseline records 22.0.368; the seat pins 22.0.400; the hython lane is 22.0.417); no Houdini-dependent test runs automatically anywhere (one workflow, no cron, hooks run zero tests); harness/verify/version_agreement.py prints LOPCAT FAIL and exits 0 with no CI reference; and the release receipts stopped entirely — `ls harness/notes/release-5.75*` does not exist while release-5.74.0 holds 23 files. The net: SYNAPSE got more capable while its ability to check itself in the runtime it ships into stayed exactly where it was in mid-August.

**Counter-evidence, stated against my own answer.** Real, and it argues the other way on the axis I am not weighting. (1) Tests track product code almost 1:1 every single week, which is the strongest convergence signal in the repo — by ISO week, product/test added lines are W32 5,054/5,795; W33 10,210/13,208; W34 1,164/2,713; W36 17,824/16,215; W37 7,705/7,101; W38 9,758/11,560. Nobody is shipping code without tests. (2) Defects genuinely close: ae34ed96 (2026-09-17) fixed the memory duplicate-deposit at source with 30 pinning tests, and I independently confirmed the current tag is clean — `gh api .../commits/53e4f9cb/check-runs` returns success on all four required contexts. (3) The 2026-09-15 merge train discharged 24 PRs in one day. (4) CI red rate on master fell over the window on any bucketing. (5) The installer engine — the code with the worst blast radius — is frozen since v5.68.0 and is the best-written code I read (safety.py rejects junctions, path escape, zip-slip and Windows reserved names). A reasonable reader could call this 'converging on code, accumulating on artifacts' and land on 'flat'. I do not, because production-readiness is a claim about verified behaviour in the shipping runtime, and that specific measurement is older than the last 659 commits.

## Every blocker, by dimension

**release-state**

- v5.75.0 was published from a commit whose CI concluded failure, five seconds after that CI run started, and with zero release assets. This is the third instance of the exact failure class scripts/release_ci_gate.py was written to prevent.
- scripts/tag_release.py — the repo's version/dirty-tree/conformance gate — was not used for EITHER v5.75.0 or v5.75.1. The published v5.75.1 notes disclose only that release_ci_gate.py was skipped for v5.75.0; they never say the tag gate was skipped for v5.75.1 itself.
- The drift that reddened v5.75.0 is verifiable in the tagged tree, and it is exactly what tag_release.py's preflight checks — so the tag was created on a tree its own gate would have refused.

**architecture-truth**

- The `/mcp` transport that is actually configured never touches LosslessExecutionBridge. `.mcp.json` wires `python mcp_server.py` (stdio), and that process forwards every tool call over WebSocket to the live `/synapse` server — the same handler path `/synapse` uses. CLAUDE.md Identity and §1 call `/mcp` "the audited path"; for the transport in use that is false.
- Consent never blocks anywhere in the process — bridge path included. The only production constructor of a bridge is `get_process_bridge()`, which unconditionally builds with an auto-approve callback and then nulls the gate; `bridge_adapter.get_bridge()` re-asserts that. §1.2.1 tier 1 ("HumanGate ... APPROVE/CRITICAL block and poll at 250ms") and §11 rule 5 ("Consent gates are real — on the bridge path only") describe a path that does not exist.
- The one code path that would route an MCP client through the bridge — the in-Houdini hwebserver `/mcp` HTTP endpoint — is armed only by importing `synapse.mcp.server`, and no production startup path imports that module.

**memory-substrate**

- Recall returns confidently wrong answers. The keyword scorer that ranks every result uses substring matching over unfiltered query words (`1 for w in words if w in content_lower or w in summary_lower`), so a query of pure stopwords outscores a real one. Measured on a read-only copy of the live 1151-row store: `do I a to in on it up at` -> 0.90 against the Solaris v3 decision; `how do I set up a karma render` -> 0.70 against the same three Solaris decisions at ranks 1-3; `what is the best recipe for banana bread` -> 0.40. `_match_memory` admits anything >= 0.4 (knowledge.py:839) and the tier floor is DENSE_MATCH_FLOOR = 0.5 (knowledge.py:35,586), so 0.6+ becomes a chat answer at `confidence=min(0.8, score)` (knowledge.py:855). This path is newly LIVE because ae34ed96 fixed the wiring.
- The decision that supersedes what recall returns is absent from the primary. `mem_e2e9749cb3c3` (2026-09-06) binds "Create a Solaris Network" AND "Basic Studio Lighting Setup" to the v4 recipe. It exists only in the JSONL mirror, and the mirror has ZERO read sites at runtime. So `search("Create a Solaris Network")` returns `mem_9e0826890122` ("now = v3 recipe") at score 1.0 — the superseded answer, served as the top hit with no signal that it was overridden.
- A second real project store is still dead from the same 2026-09-15 defect and nobody noticed. `D:\MODEL_COLLECTION\Astra_Saucer_Sept2026\...\.synapse\memory.jsonl` carries `mem_043ce93ca4e5` twice (lines 5 and 6, both created_at 2026-09-15T15:24:47Z) and has not been written since Sep 15 11:40. Its Moneta primary carries the same duplicate (12 rows / 11 distinct), so `primary_repair.py` was not run on it either. It degraded three more times TODAY.
- Retrieval is dominated by the agent's own telemetry, not the artist's records. 533 of 1151 rows carry protected_floor 0.9; 530 of those are `feedback` rows whose content begins `{"attempt":{"context_sha256"` — RSI handler receipts. Only 3 protected rows are decisions. Ranking is `cos_sim * utility` with a 6h half-life, so of 612 unprotected rows only 57 (all <24h old) still carry utility >= 0.1; 411 sit below 1e-6, median 6.3e-8.

**test-ci-health**

- The panel surface — the thing an artist actually touches — has almost no automated coverage. 296 of the 485 per-leg CI skips (61%) are "no real Qt/PySide". The only lane that runs real Qt is the hand-run seat suite, which runs `tests/panel` ONLY: 207 items, of which 6 are RED and tolerated by a named baseline. 169 Qt-gated tests per leg live OUTSIDE tests/panel (90 in tests/test_panel_rhythm_docking.py, 30 in test_panel_sweep_a.py, 17 in test_chat_panel.py) and are therefore run by NOTHING — they skip on CI and they are not in the seat selector.
- The shipping interpreter — Houdini's hython with the vendored wheel tree ACTIVE, i.e. what artists actually run — was last measured 2026-08-15 at 6,193 passed / 119 failed / 5 collection errors, and the ratchet explicitly does NOT re-run it. That is 34 days and 659 commits ago. The current state of the shipping leg is UNKNOWN.
- The suite ratchet floor is ~3,070 passes behind reality, so it cannot catch a regression that deletes up to three thousand tests. The enforced gate leg says passed=6389/failed=0; the suite now passes 9,461 on CI and 9,588 locally. The ratchet's pass condition is `failed_now <= failed_base and passed_now >= passed_base` — a run that dropped 3,000 tests would still satisfy it.

**harness-inventory**

- The harness's own human-gate aggregator reports 649 open decisions, 544 of them older than 30 days, and it prints the verdict itself: "the queue has stopped moving." The aging gate exits 6 but nothing consumes that exit code.
- That 649 is an undercount: decisions.py reads only receipts, flywheel_queue.json and design_review/<date>/RULINGS-OPEN.md. It does not read the CTO backlog (27 open, 18 gate=joe), RANKED.md's Round-1 and Round-3 rulings, rope's blocked tasks, the conductor boards' own human_gates, or tasks.json. The "everything waiting on a human, in one place" claim in its own docstring is false.
- The flagship harness engine (harness/run.ts + tasks.json, 81 tasks) is being maintained but not run. Its MODE B trigger has been armed since 2026-08-31, its completion ledger file does not exist, and its progress log's last entry is 2026-07-16 — yet it received a code change on 2026-09-15.
- In six weeks the harness tree grew by roughly 1,400 tracked files with zero directory deletions. 274 of 835 commits since 2026-08-07 (33%) touch only harness/ — no product file, no test file.
- The SYNAPSE V2 blueprint — the forward product plan — is excluded from git by .gitignore and exists only on this disk. It would not survive a clean clone.
- Backup-Branches still pushes every non-master worktree branch to the public origin with no content gate and swallows push output — the higher of the two blockers the 2026-09-15 self-review raised (FENCE-2) is unfixed, and so are review rows 1a, 1c, 1d and 1i.
- The Solaris v3 acceptance harness was built and has never been run: all 19 gates are NOT_RUN and the promotion rule's bindings map is empty. CTO backlog H4 (gate=joe) flagged this on 2026-09-05 and it has not moved.

**h22-build-posture**

- The live `/mcp` scout — the tool CLAUDE.md Safety Rule 15 mandates before writing any unfamiliar `hou.*` call — is serving the H21.0.671 symbol table on this H22-only host, with `gate_armed: true` and `stale: false`, and it returns `exists_in_runtime: false` for `hou.undos.group`, which is real on 22.0.400 and called in 16 files under `python/synapse/server/`.
- `docs/studio/UPGRADE.md` promises the phantom gate is fail-closed on any build change ("scout refuses ... raises rather than serve unverified verdicts"). That promise does not cover this host's actual failure mode: the no-running-Houdini path deliberately does not set `stale`, so the gate stays armed and answers.
- The rulebook that CLAUDE.md declares to be law is an empty shell pinned to H21: `surfaces/` holds only `.gitkeep`, `phantoms.json` is `{"phantoms": []}`, `manifest.json` has `sections: []` and a `runtime_baseline` of graphical 21.0.671 / hython 21.0.631 / python 3.11, and neither `scripts/rulebook_harvest.py` nor `scripts/rulebook_diff.py` exists.
- The phantom lint does not run in CI. CI runs pytest only; `check_phantom_clean` is a harness sprint guardrail, and the tests that touch it only scan synthetic strings, never the repository's own source.

**panel-ux**

- The panel's one visible status sentence is derived only from Houdini reachability, so it says "Ready" (GROW green) while memory is degraded, no model is connected, or the bridge is down. This is the exact false green `health_strip.py` was written to prevent, reintroduced one layer up.
- The health strip is no longer a strip. Its own module docstring promises "always-visible" cells where "Degraded is loud ... no click required to notice"; at HEAD the `HealthStrip` widget is never instantiated and the four cells are built only when the overflow menu is opened.
- Two panels ship on disk and only one is registered. The 2026-09-15 "firstrun" artist audit and its merged fix both targeted the unregistered one, so the FR-1 send guard is not in the code path Houdini loads.

**open-work**

- Six `worktree-wf_*` branches from 2026-09-14 are LOCAL-ONLY (never pushed), absent from the 2026-09-15 closeout triage, and three of them carry work that exists nowhere else.
- `worktree-wf_8a870870-1d5-3` holds a complete agent.usd schema-migration path that master does not have at all: `_upgrade_agent_usd()` in scene_memory.py plus a 5-test `TestLegacyAgentUsdUpgrade` class.
- `worktree-wf_51b5f8aa-295-1` holds a 25-test, 448-line suite pinning panel state-sentence honesty (`tests/test_panel_header_sentence_floor.py`) that is absent from master with no equivalent.

**known-defects**

- Defect (2) PDG dirtyAllTasks signature mismatch is STILL PRESENT at HEAD, and the harness check built to catch it is a FALSE GREEN — its literal fingerprint matches zero lines of the file it inspects.
- Defect (5) "no API cancels an in-flight ROP render" is PARTLY REFUTED and partly still true, and the README describes the WRONG half of the gap. rps/rkill works for background renders; foreground renders are unreachable by any control. README's stop table names only the recoverable gap.
- UNRECORDED AS A DEFECT: the panel's live system prompt instructs the model to set soho_foreground=1 — the exact render mode that freezes the GUI unbounded and is unreachable by every stop control — and the repo contains three mutually contradictory teachings on this parameter.
- There is NO canonical defect ledger. Every known defect I verified lives in a different artifact type, and none is indexed. This is the structural finding behind "documented where someone would find it."

**product-readiness**

- synapse_safe_render's advertised lockup guard is unreachable: the guard reads width/height from the caller's payload, but the registered tool schema exposes only rop_path and soho_foreground, so effective_w/effective_h are always 0 and the `> 512` branch can never fire through MCP.
- The panel's chat worker cannot start or stop a render in the shipped default mode. 40 of 137 tools are denied, and that set includes every render-initiating and render-stopping tool.
- synapse_scout — named in CLAUDE.md §11 rule 15 as "the front-line defense against SYNAPSE's #1 failure class (phantom APIs)" — returns a hard error on every call through the live MCP bridge.
- On the /mcp path scout resolves the H21 symbol table, not H22, so even when it works its existence verdicts are for the wrong build. In-Houdini the table is correctly H22 — this is path-specific, not global.
- No recipe in the 62-recipe registry matches "create a solaris network" in any casing, and every trigger is an anchored ^…$ regex that misses ordinary artist phrasing.
- The four "Create a Solaris Network" recipe versions are split across three memory substrates, and the newest one is invisible to semantic recall. v4 exists only in a markdown file; v1–v3 exist only in the Moneta vector store.
- synapse_knowledge_lookup returns uncapped raw parameter dumps — 91,373 bytes (~23k tokens) for the single query "karma xpu" — and this is a documented design choice, not a bug.
- The memory store is 99% machine telemetry. Of 1,144 rows, 11 are artist-authored content and 2 of those are empty strings.
- The store grows on read-only calls — roughly 2 rows per tool call — and at the observed rate will cross the 2,000-record migration budget in about two weeks, at which point the first save of an unsaved scene will silently fail to carry memory forward.
- A foreground render cannot be cancelled by any shipped control, because every stop path needs the main thread the render is holding.

**docs-truth**

- The 'Full setup guide' linked from README:32 and from docs/index.md:10 sends artists to download v5.74.0 — the release BEFORE the memory-store fix README:113 headlines. The link resolves; it does not 404. It silently delivers the pre-fix installer.
- docs/studio/DEPLOYMENT.md documents the full multi-user studio path — studio-lan/studio-vpn binding 0.0.0.0, firewall rules, TLS, role matrix — with zero mention that `execute_python`/`execute_vex` run ungated on the live path. README:188 and SECURITY.md:19 both state a handler-layer gate is a prerequisite that is 'tracked, not shipped'. The three documents disagree about whether studio deployment is a supported posture.
- docs/status.md — titled 'Current status and limits' and linked from README:143 as '[Current status]' — was last touched at v5.67.4 and describes v5.67.3 as 'the latest changes'. That is eight releases and nine days behind VERSION 5.75.1. No test binds it.

*47 blocker-severity findings across 11 dimensions.*

## What nobody looked at

The completeness critic's job was to find the gaps between the eleven dimensions, then go look
at the biggest ones itself.

### The shipped RAG corpus as a product artifact (rag/ — 74.4 MB, 35.4% of the repo by weight)

**Why it matters.** This is the material that grounds every answer the model gives an artist, it is the biggest single thing they download, and it is majority-H21. It fell between h22-build-posture (which audited the symbol table, not the corpus) and product-readiness (which audited tools, not payload).

**What it found.** No dimension owned rag/. h22-build-posture's refuter noted the corpus is H21-named but framed it as a scout-grounding issue, never as a shipped artifact. I measured it: `git ls-files` + os.path.getsize over the tracked tree gives 210.0 MB total, of which rag/ is 74.4 MB (rag/catalog 51.0 MB, rag/corpus 13.3 MB, rag/documentation 8.2 MB, rag/skills 1.7 MB). It grew +2,516,706 lines in six weeks (`git log --since=2026-08-07 --numstat`), dominated by 8682afef (W5-CATALOG live H22.0.400 dump). Decisively: C:/Users/User/SYNAPSE/installer/build_payload.py:16-17 sets RUNTIME_TREES = ("python/synapse/", "shared/", "houdini/", "rag/catalog/", "rag/corpus/", "rag/skills/", "rag/documentation/", "docs/help/") — so essentially the entire 74 MB corpus ships to every artist. `git ls-files rag/ | grep -oE 'h2[12][._][0-9]+|houdini2[12]'` returns 104 houdini21 against 23 h22.0.400. The single largest shipped grounding set is H21-named on an H22-only product.

### The phantom-API gate's resolution logic on the agent path — and why it is permanently H21 there

**Why it matters.** CLAUDE.md §11 rule 15 calls this the front-line defense against the project's documented #1 failure class. On the agent path it is structurally incapable of ever seeing the running build, and it answers 'does not exist' for a live API. It sits between architecture-truth (owns the path) and h22-build-posture (owns the table); neither connected the two.

**What it found.** I probed the live /mcp scout myself, twice. Call 1 returned {"error":"ProgrammingError","message":"SQLite objects created in a thread can only be used in that same thread. The object was created in thread id 42800 and this is thread id 73532."}. Call 2 succeeded and returned: table.houdini_version "21.0.671", path h21_symbol_table.json, gate_armed true, stale false, and symbols [{"symbol":"hou.undos.group","exists_in_runtime":false}] — a symbol that is real on 22.0.400 and called across the server. The loaded tool description itself opens "Scout the Houdini 21.0.671 documentation RAG". h22-build-posture found this symptom; nobody found the cause. C:/Users/User/SYNAPSE/python/synapse/cognitive/tools/scout.py:139 hardcodes _PKG_SYMBOL_TABLE to h21_symbol_table.json, and the docstring at :652-656 states the rule outright: "No major obtainable (headless stock python, no host) or no per-major file committed -> the H21 table, unchanged." Scout is deliberately zero-hou (CLAUDE.md rule 15), so on the stdio MCP path no major is EVER obtainable. This is not a stale artifact — it is a hardcoded default that fires 100% of the time on the path agents use, and both tables ship (ls python/synapse/cognitive/tools/data/*symbol_table*.json returns h21 and h22).

### The install/uninstall engine as executed, and who ever runs its tests

**Why it matters.** The one piece of code an artist runs before anything else has good internals and zero automatic verification, and its gate is a script that is re-authored per release and stopped existing two releases ago.

**What it found.** product-readiness's refuter flagged that nobody opened the installer. I did. It is the best-engineered code I looked at: C:/Users/User/SYNAPSE/installer/synapse_setup/safety.py rejects junctions/symlinks (no_links), blocks path escape (inside), blocks zip-slip and Windows reserved device names (member_path), and does atomic writes. Full lifecycle including uninstall exists (setup_cli.py:15 choices include uninstall, uninstall-check). 37 test functions across installer/tests/. Two correctives to the review set's framing: (1) `git diff --stat v5.74.0 HEAD -- installer/` is EMPTY — installer code is frozen since 8c23fccc (v5.68.0), so the 'unqualified installer' risk is about the payload, not the engine; (2) the only invokers of `installer/tests` are per-release copies of cut.py under harness/notes/release-5.7x.0/, and docs/RELEASE_CARD.md:36-53 carries the qualification as human steps 6-12 including 'pytest installer\tests' at step 12. CI never touches it (ci.yml:109 runs `pytest tests/` only).

### Scene-destructive disk surface (hou.hipFile.load/clear with suppress_save_prompt=True)

**Why it matters.** A correct, load-bearing safety property held by convention alone. One future refactor that imports this module into the host turns two lines into unsaved-work destruction, and nothing would go red.

**What it found.** Nobody asked what SYNAPSE can destroy on the artist's disk. C:/Users/User/SYNAPSE/python/synapse/farm/native_driver.py:461 calls hou.hipFile.load(str(frozen), suppress_save_prompt=True, ignore_load_warnings=True) and :500 calls hou.hipFile.clear(suppress_save_prompt=True) — both discard unsaved work without asking. Its docstring line 1 claims 'never imported by the artist host'. I tested rather than trusted: the only production reference is farm/backend.py:20, `self.driver = Path(__file__).with_name("native_driver.py")` — a subprocess path, not an import. It also operates on a frozen copy in a job dir (pkg.frozen_copy). So the isolation is real and the design is sound. But no test asserts it: the only importers are tests/test_farm_backend.py:17, test_farm_recovery.py:19, test_farm_usd_settings.py:10.

### Credential handling and egress (checked, and it is a genuine positive)

**Why it matters.** Worth stating precisely because an eleven-dimension review that never mentions credentials invites a reader to assume the worst. This surface is better guarded than several the review set graded 'blocker'.

**What it found.** No dimension covered secrets, on a repo whose own memory index records that origin is PUBLIC and auto-pushed. I checked: .gitignore:102-105 covers .env, .env.*, *.key; `git ls-files` shows only .env.example tracked; the local .env is untracked. A prefix scan for sk-ant-/AIza/nvapi- across the tree hits only test fixtures, and they are deliberate canaries (tests/test_install_verify.py:34 FAKE_KEY = "sk-ant-VERIFYLEAKCANARY0123456789"; tests/test_anthropic_errors.py:26 sentinel ' PRIVATE_SCENE_DATA sk-ant-never-display-this'). No logger call sites log api_key/Authorization. docs/studio/EGRESS.md names all three remote hosts and is conformance-pinned by tests/test_m3_egress_docs.py.

### Test trees outside tests/ (agent/, .synapse/tests, harness/bastion/tests) and turn-latency

**Why it matters.** Every headline test figure in the project (9,461 passing) silently excludes these trees, so the denominator in four dimensions is wrong in the same direction.

**What it found.** ci.yml:109 runs `pytest tests/` only. agent/ is a separate ~1,200-line agent runtime that grew in the window and whose tests have no invoker I could find. Nobody measured end-to-end turn latency either — the one axis an artist feels every single interaction.

---

## Where two dimensions disagreed

Each pair looked at the same underlying fact and reached different answers. The critic settled
each one first-hand.

### release-state — 'It was green throughout that incident'; the suite 'cannot tell you the download button points at a release that has no assets' vs test-ci-health — 'v5.75.0's red was three version-conformance tests (platform-independent), fixed forward by v5.75.1'

**The fact in dispute.** Whether the test suite was green or red on v5.75.0's commit (43ef30ca)

**Settled.** test-ci-health, decisively. I ran `gh api repos/:owner/:repo/commits/43ef30ca/check-runs`: test (macos-latest, 3.11) = failure, the other three = cancelled (fail-fast). v5.75.1 (53e4f9cb) returns success on all four. release-state's 39-passed run was executed at HEAD b7592a9a, a tree where the drift had already been repaired — an isolated green presented as proof a check is toothless. This matters more than a scoring correction: the two dimensions prescribe opposite remedies. release-state says build detection; test-ci-health says the detection fired and nothing read it. Only the second is actionable.

### architecture-truth — F3, severity blocker: 'no production startup path imports that module'; 'the audited path has no external client at all' vs h22-build-posture and product-readiness — both report live responses from synapse_scout 'through the live MCP bridge'

**The fact in dispute.** Whether the in-Houdini /mcp endpoint exists and answers

**Settled.** h22-build-posture and product-readiness. I settled it first-hand: two calls to the live scout, the second returning a full JSON payload with gate_armed, table path and symbol verdicts. architecture-truth reached a blocker verdict from a static import graph while explicitly declining the read-only probe that refutes it — and the task permitted read-only live queries. Its dependent claims inherit the error, including 'no other production bridge instances' reading very differently once you know python/synapse/mcp/tools.py:118-134 routes non-read-only tools through execute_through_bridge.

### known-defects — 'There is NO canonical defect ledger', filed as the report's one structural finding, severity blocker vs harness-inventory — cites harness/cto/BACKLOG.json throughout ('27 open, 18 gate=joe'), and reads its closure predicates

**The fact in dispute.** Whether a canonical machine-readable defect ledger exists in this repo

**Settled.** harness-inventory. I parsed the file: 35 items, 23 open / 8 closed / 4 unknown, 3 critical / 17 high / 15 medium, each with evidence, repro, gate and closure_predicate. known-defects' evidence was `ls docs/ | grep -iE 'defect|known|ledger'` — a docs-scoped search published as a repo-wide negative. The residual true finding is narrower and still worth acting on: none of its six named defects appears in that ledger. The remedy changes from 'build a ledger' to 'file six rows'.

### architecture-truth — headline: 'consent does not block on any path in the process, bridge or live'; no operation ever pauses vs product-readiness — '40 of 137 tools are denied' to the panel chat worker in shipped default mode, including every render-initiating and render-stopping tool

**The fact in dispute.** Whether SYNAPSE has any real, enforced, default-on authorization gate

**Settled.** product-readiness, on the narrow fact. worker_policy.py:64 _DENIED_GATES with _DEFAULT_MODE = standard, enforced at claude_worker.py:401-402, armed at synapse_panel.py:3422 — a default-on, fail-closed DENY layer that architecture-truth never opened. Both are right about HumanGate never firing. The synthesis neither reached: SYNAPSE has no CONSENT layer (nothing pauses to ask) but does have an AUTHORIZATION layer (things are refused outright), and conflating them is why one dimension calls the surface wide open while the other calls it too closed to render.

### product-readiness — 'returns a hard error on every call through the live MCP bridge', severity blocker vs h22-build-posture — a first call returned the SQLite error, 'the retry succeeded'

**The fact in dispute.** How often the live scout fails

**Settled.** h22-build-posture. I reproduced exactly that sequence: call 1 threw the cross-thread SQLite ProgrammingError naming thread 42800 vs 73532, call 2 returned a complete result. It is intermittent — first-touch-per-worker against the fixed 4-worker ThreadPoolExecutor at mcp_server.py:227-231 — not a dead tool. product-readiness also sourced the mechanism to a stale docstring (asyncio.to_thread) rather than the real _off_loop dispatcher, so a fix built on its diagnosis would target the wrong executor.

### h22-build-posture — 'nothing in CI or production calls it'; artist symptom is 'a scout that says no, not a phantom that ships' vs docs-truth — the same drift, with the loader ignoring the stamp entirely

**The fact in dispute.** Whether the lop-catalog stamp check runs, and what an artist experiences when it drifts

**Settled.** Neither as filed; docs-truth is closer and the corrected version is worse than both. It does run — version_agreement.py:685/:775 and harness/finalize.ps1:105 on every release cut — and I confirmed it prints LOPCAT FAIL while the script exits 0, deliberately wired out of the gate. And python/synapse/core/lop_knowledge.py validates schema at :105 and blake2b at :112 without ever reading houdini_version, so the fail-closed refusal h22-build-posture describes does not happen: a stale Solaris answer ships silently. Both dimensions filed this 'watch'; it is a check that runs, reports failure, is ignored by its own exit code, is pinned by a test that only sees synthetic fixtures, and feeds a loader that ignores the stamp.

### memory-substrate — 'the divergence is CLOSED, three surfaces agree to within exactly 4 records' (1151/1155/1151) vs product-readiness — reports the same store at 1144 rows, and separately spots MonetaBackedStore count 1161 against its JSONL shadow at 1165

**The fact in dispute.** Whether the memory substrate's three surfaces agree

**Settled.** memory-substrate on structure, and both are describing the same live store at different minutes — it grows ~1 row per tool call, so the absolute numbers were stale before either was written. The real contradiction is one neither owned: memory-substrate presents 'three surfaces agree' as a property of the SUBSTRATE when it holds for exactly ONE store. Its own refuter found a second live store (Houdini21_Alien_Spaceship_Saucer) whose primary is literally {"rows": []} with 39 records stranded in the mirror — the exact inverse of the audited outage, ten times the size of the 4-record gap, and unreachable by recall because recall reads the primary.

---

# The eleven dimensions

## release-state — MIXED

> The version ladder itself is clean — 92 tags, none orphaned, local and remote identical, VERSION conformant at HEAD — but the release *ritual* regressed sharply at v5.75.x: both 5.75 tags bypassed the repo's own tag-creation gate, v5.75.0 shipped from a CI-red commit with zero assets, and v5.75.1 shipped with no install qualification at all.

**Trajectory.** Instrumentation improving, adherence regressing, over the last ~6 weeks. Cadence is extreme: `git for-each-ref refs/tags --sort=creatordate` shows 44 version tags between 2026-08-02 (v5.42.0) and 2026-09-17 (v5.75.1) — roughly 0.9 releases/day, with 10 tags in the five days 09-13..09-17. The gates were built in direct response to failures: `scripts/tag_release.py` at 957a17be (2026-08-09, "release truth - VERSION canonical, six-surface sync, refuse-dirty-tag gate"), `scripts/release_ci_gate.py` at b1ec9a8d (2026-09-06, "cto(crank 3): the loop reads the tag, not the branch") after v5.64.0/v5.65.0 were found pointing at CI-red commits, RELEASE_CARD extended at 67625294 (2026-09-14, "teach RELEASE_CARD the half it was missing"), Gate C pre-commit fence at 1399abff (2026-09-15), composer repairs at 72cd76c0 (#105, 2026-09-15). Against that, adherence went the other way: v5.68.0–v5.74.0 all carry the gate's own annotated message and all shipped four assets including a real install qualification; v5.75.0 (43ef30ca, 2026-09-17) repeated the exact CI-red-tag failure the 09-06 gate was written to stop — the third instance of that class — and v5.75.1, the remediation release, itself skipped the tag gate and shipped without `installer-verification.json`. Receipt volume also dropped: `harness/notes/release-5.74.0/` holds 23 files; there is no `harness/notes/release-5.75.0/` or `release-5.75.1/` directory at all (`ls harness/notes/release-5.75*` → "No such file or directory").

**The refuter on where this report overstates itself.** Yes, in four specific places. (1) The headline's "92 tags, none orphaned" quietly drops the "version" qualifier their own finding carries, and is false of the repo's 119 tags: 15 non-version tags are off master and one of those, windows-installer-5.67.4-preview.1, is a published GitHub prerelease with 3 assets. The finding was scoped honestly; the headline was not. (2) "status: mixed" is more comfortable than their own evidence. The version *ladder* is clean; that is one sentence of the dimension. The rest is that today's Latest — carrying ~3,000 lines of new memory, write-plane and panel code — shipped with no install qualification, no cut directory, no CHANGELOG entry, no seat receipt, and a bypassed tag gate, and that its predecessor is still published with zero assets. Two of the eight tags in their own "adherence held" window (v5.75.0, v5.75.1) broke it, and the receipts stopped entirely. That reads worse than mixed. (3) Severity is inverted. Three process findings are rated "blocker" while the single finding with real, disclosed artist exposure — an unqualified installer that is the current download — is rated "watch." The v5.74.0 attestation they cite as the comparison is a 10-section probe (ci, compiled_installer, installed_houdini, installer_unit_checks, live_houdini, local_stock_suite, payload_audit, readme_receipt, readme_render_probe, remaining_qualification); none of it ran. Shipping that unqualified is the blocker; a skipped script is the cause. (4) Finding 10's artistImpact — "It was green throughout that incident" — inverts the record. The suite was red on that exact commit, on those exact files, and their 39-passed run was executed against a repaired tree. That sentence, and the "instrumentation improving, adherence regressing" trajectory built partly on it, both overstate how blind the tooling was. The tooling saw it. Nobody read it.

### Findings

#### [BLOCKER · load-bearing] v5.75.0 was published from a commit whose CI concluded failure, five seconds after that CI run started, and with zero release assets. This is the third instance of the exact failure class scripts/release_ci_gate.py was written to prevent.

**Evidence.** `python scripts/release_ci_gate.py v5.75.0 --check-only` → `tag v5.75.0 43ef30ca / ci completed failure REFUSE / run 35280263679 — a red commit must not become a release`, exit=1. Timing: `gh run list` gives run for 43ef30ca createdAt `2026-09-17T22:05:49Z`; `gh release list` gives v5.75.0 published `2026-09-17T22:05:54Z` — a 5-second gap. Assets: `gh release view v5.75.0 --json assets` → `assets=0` (v5.71.0–v5.74.0 each show 4). Prior instances named at scripts/release_ci_gate.py:3-6 (v5.64.0, v5.65.0 red; v5.65.2 tagged mid-run).

**For the artist.** For ~45 minutes v5.75.0 was the newest release on the page with nothing to download, while the README download button still pointed at v5.74.0's installer. The release is still published today (not draft, not prerelease) — an artist browsing /releases sees a normal-looking v5.75.0 with no assets. Its GitHub body now opens with a SUPERSEDED banner, so the exposure is mitigated but not removed.

#### [BLOCKER · load-bearing] scripts/tag_release.py — the repo's version/dirty-tree/conformance gate — was not used for EITHER v5.75.0 or v5.75.1. The published v5.75.1 notes disclose only that release_ci_gate.py was skipped for v5.75.0; they never say the tag gate was skipped for v5.75.1 itself.

**Evidence.** scripts/tag_release.py:143-144 hardcodes the annotated message `SYNAPSE {tag} -- release-truth gate passed`. `git for-each-ref refs/tags/<t> --format='%(subject)'` returns exactly that string for v5.68.0, v5.69.0, v5.70.0, v5.70.1, v5.71.0, v5.72.0, v5.73.0, v5.74.0 — and for v5.75.0 returns `v5.75.0 -- the second write, and the silence around it`, for v5.75.1 `v5.75.1 -- the tag that was checked`. Neither was produced by the script. docs/releases/v5.75.1.md names only `scripts/release_ci_gate.py` as the gate that "was not invoked".

**For the artist.** None directly. Indirectly it is why v5.75.0 shipped broken: the gate's sync_check (tag_release.py:92-102, which shells out to sync_version.py --check and prints `sync_check drift REFUSE`) would have caught the drift before the tag existed.

#### [BLOCKER · load-bearing] The drift that reddened v5.75.0 is verifiable in the tagged tree, and it is exactly what tag_release.py's preflight checks — so the tag was created on a tree its own gate would have refused.

**Evidence.** `git show 43ef30ca:VERSION` → `5.75.0`; `git show 43ef30ca:pyproject.toml | grep -m1 '^version'` → `version = "5.75.0"`; but `git show 43ef30ca:CLAUDE.md | grep -oE 'SYNAPSE v[0-9.]+'` → `SYNAPSE v5.74.0` and `git show 43ef30ca:README.md | sed -n '7p'` → `<sub>v5.74.0 · … tags: v5.74.0 is Latest</sub>`. Both are declared surfaces at scripts/sync_version.py (`claude_md`, `readme`), and tag_release.py:92-102 refuses on their drift.

**For the artist.** None once superseded. It is the mechanism finding, not a user-facing one.

#### [WATCH · load-bearing] v5.75.1 — the current Latest, the version an artist downloads today — was never install-qualified. No `installer-verification.json` was produced or shipped, and `qualification_report` in the public build report is null.

**Evidence.** `gh release view v5.75.1 --json assets` → 3 assets (SHA256SUMS.txt, SYNAPSE-5.75.1-Setup.exe, SYNAPSE-5.75.1-Setup.public-build.json); v5.71.0/v5.72.0/v5.73.0/v5.74.0 each show 4 including installer-verification.json. Fetched public build report: `qualification_report = None`, vs v5.74.0's `"installer-verification.json"`. Stated plainly at docs/releases/v5.75.1.md ("Assets, and the one that is missing") and in commit aa9624e5's body.

**For the artist.** Real. The installer an artist downloads today has not been run against a Houdini install. v5.74.0's report (fetched) proves what that qualification catches: `compiled_installer` checks for missing dependencies, write-denied destinations and fresh-install paths, plus a 207-test seat run against Houdini 22.0.400. None of that was executed for 5.75.1. The notes say so honestly — this is a disclosed gap, not a hidden one.

#### [WATCH · load-bearing] There is no internal release-cut record for v5.75.0 or v5.75.1 at all — no harness/notes/release-5.75.x directory, no RELEASE_v5.75.x.md, no suite/seat receipts. Every release from 5.70.0 through 5.74.0 has one.

**Evidence.** `ls harness/notes/release-5.75*` → `No such file or directory`. `ls harness/notes/RELEASE_v5.7*.md` → only RELEASE_v5.70.0.md and RELEASE_v5.70.1.md. By contrast `harness/notes/release-5.74.0/` contains build-prod.log, build-test.log, qual-stdout.txt, suite-5.74.0.txt, seat-5.74.0.txt, installer-tests-5.74.0.txt, payload-vs-tag-5.74.0.json, RELEASE_v5.74.0.md (23 files). docs/RELEASE_CARD.md step 4 requires `harness\notes\RELEASE_vX.Y.Z.md`; steps 10-12 require stock suite, seat suite and installer tests.

**For the artist.** None directly, but it is why the qualification gap cannot be closed retroactively: there is no record of what was run, so the only honest option was to declare it unqualified.

#### [WATCH] CHANGELOG.md has no entry for v5.75.0 or v5.75.1 — zero mentions of the string "5.75" — while README.md:205 links it as the deep record. The ritual's step 4 ("promote the CHANGELOG entry") was skipped for both.

**Evidence.** `grep -n "5\.75" CHANGELOG.md` → no output, exit 0 via head. `grep -nE '^## ' CHANGELOG.md` → newest version heading is `25:## v5.74.0 - The page, and the receipt that guards it`; line 5 is `## Unreleased — backfill: BLOCKS, deterministic scene setups`, which is a v5.43.0-era backfill, not 5.75 work. README.md:205 = `[Release notes](docs/releases/v5.75.1.md) · [Changelog](CHANGELOG.md)`.

**For the artist.** An artist who follows the README's Changelog link to understand what changed sees the history stop at v5.74.0 — two releases behind the version they just installed, including the memory-store outage fix.

#### [WATCH · load-bearing] docs/releases/v5.75.0.md carries no supersession notice. The GitHub release body was amended with one; the in-repo copy was not, so the two records of the same release now disagree.

**Evidence.** `gh release view v5.75.0 --json body` opens: `> **SUPERSEDED by [v5.75.1](...)** … This tag points at a commit whose CI concluded **failure** … **Use v5.75.1.**`. `grep -niE "supersed|ci concluded|not checked" docs/releases/v5.75.0.md` returns only one unrelated line (:83, about a test file); `grep -n "5.75.1" docs/releases/v5.75.0.md` returns nothing.

**For the artist.** Anyone reading the repo's own release notes directory — which the README points at — reads v5.75.0 as a live, sound release. The correction exists only on GitHub.

#### [WATCH] docs/releases/v5.72.1.md is a public release note for a version that was never tagged and never published, and the file itself says nothing about being abandoned.

**Evidence.** `git tag -l 'v5.72.1'` → no output. `gh release view v5.72.1` → `release not found`. `grep -niE "abandon|not released|never released|superseded" docs/releases/v5.72.1.md` → no hits; the file opens `# v5.72.1 - The receipts say the numbers now / A patch release that repairs this project's own evidence.` The abandonment is recorded only in commit 195afb1a ("docs(closeout): v5.72.1 abandoned, and why all three new guards were themselves wrong", 2026-09-15).

**For the artist.** A reader browsing docs/releases/ sees v5.72.1.md sitting between v5.72.0 and v5.73.0 and reasonably concludes it shipped. It did not.

#### [WATCH] Release evidence is largely uncommitted. The entire v5.72.1 cut directory is untracked, and v5.73.0's is 9 tracked files against 32 on disk — so a substantial part of the release record exists only on this machine.

**Evidence.** Per-directory `git ls-files <d> | wc -l` vs `find <d> -type f -not -path '*__pycache__*' | wc -l`: release-5.71.0 18/26, release-5.72.0 8/15, release-5.72.1 0/11, release-5.73.0 9/32, release-5.74.0 18/23. `git status --porcelain` shows 34 untracked paths including harness/notes/release-5.71.0/commit-msg.txt, release-5.72.1/ and eight release-5.73.0/probe_j5*.py files.

**For the artist.** None. It is a durability risk for the project's own audit trail — lose this disk and several releases' evidence goes with it.

#### [WATCH · load-bearing] Nothing in the test suite verifies that GitHub's actual Latest release matches the README's "is Latest" banner. The conformance test is repo-file-only and its own docstring says so.

**Evidence.** tests/test_phase0c_doc1_toolcount.py:156-161 — `_assert_release_tags(readme, canonical, notes)` with docstring "Older Latest ordering is not remote-state attestation; publication separately verifies GitHub's retained Latest against the approved release plan." Lines 176-181 compare the banner only against the local VERSION file. Ran `python -m pytest tests/test_phase0c_doc1_version_conformance.py tests/test_phase0c_doc1_toolcount.py -q` → `39 passed in 0.71s` — green regardless of remote state.

**For the artist.** This is the exact blind spot v5.75.0 fell through: the suite cannot tell you the download button points at a release that has no assets. It was green throughout that incident.

#### [INFO · load-bearing] POSITIVE, verified end to end: the v5.75.1 installer an artist downloads today hashes to exactly the digest the release notes, SHA256SUMS.txt and the public build report all claim, and the build report's source_revision is the v5.75.1 tag commit.

**Evidence.** `curl -sL <release URL>/SYNAPSE-5.75.1-Setup.exe | sha256sum` → `d90e9fefff062cb4f4c6fd6ddf6f807d5b559cde3ec2899057dd1f335da6c421`, size 31961483 bytes. Published SHA256SUMS.txt → `d90e9fef…5da6c421 *SYNAPSE-5.75.1-Setup.exe`. Public build report → `installer_sha256 = d90e9fef…5da6c421`, `installer_bytes = 31961483`, `source_revision = 53e4f9cb7359c83edb108841ef0615ea0c84a692` = `git rev-list -n1 v5.75.1`. README.md:13 download URL returns HTTP 200.

**For the artist.** The download works and the checksum an artist is told to verify against is genuinely correct. Ritual step 19 holds for this release even though steps 7-12 did not run.

#### [INFO · load-bearing] The tag ladder itself is structurally sound: all 92 version tags are ancestors of master, none points at an amended or rebased-away commit, and local and remote tag sets are identical.

**Evidence.** Loop over `git tag -l 'v*'` with `git merge-base --is-ancestor <sha> master` → `checked=92 orphaned=0`. `comm` of sorted local `git tag -l 'v*'` against `git ls-remote --tags origin` (peeled) → 92 vs 92, empty in both directions.

**For the artist.** None. It means every published version can still be checked out and diffed — the history behind the releases is intact.

#### [INFO · load-bearing] Master is ahead of origin by exactly one commit, and it contains no product code — so there is effectively nothing product-shaped sitting unreleased. Since v5.75.1 only two commits landed: one docs-only (pushed) and one tooling/docs (unpushed).

**Evidence.** `git rev-list --left-right --count origin/master...master` → `0  1`. `git log --oneline origin/master..master` → `b7592a9a chore(hython): land the verb contract, and close the dual-write handoff`; its --stat touches only .synapse/contracts/, docs/, harness/verify/ and tools/ (12 files, 2356 insertions, no python/synapse/). `git log --oneline --stat v5.75.1..HEAD` shows only b7592a9a and aa9624e5 (docs/releases/v5.75.1.md, +17/-1). origin/master head aa9624e5 is CI-green (`gh run list` → `aa9624e5 completed success`). `git status --porcelain | grep -v '^??' | wc -l` → 0 tracked modifications.

**For the artist.** None. v5.75.1 is genuinely current — the tree has not moved past the shipped product.

#### [INFO] Unmerged work is small and mostly stale: one open PR (draft, explicitly gated on a human decision), and 94 local branches of which 15 carry commits not on master, the largest at 15 commits.

**Evidence.** `gh pr list --state open` → a single row: `#93 rebase/m2-pgdrm-reworded draft=true 2026-09-15T22:09:40Z loop(pgdrm): M2 PG-DRM kernel rebased on master + one-shot-iterable REFUSAL (GATED on PLAN Stop 4 decision #1)`. Branch sweep with `git rev-list --count master..<b>`: rebase/cto-render 15, fix/cto-render-20260908 8, salvage/bp2-nits 7, feature/tops-renderfarm-20260907 7, feature/windows-installer-20260909 6; total local branches 94. `git worktree list` → 13 worktrees, three under OneDrive.

**For the artist.** None. No artist-visible feature is stuck behind a merge.

### Refuter verdicts

- **CONFIRMED** — v5.75.0 was published from a commit whose CI concluded failure, five seconds after that CI run started, and with zero release assets — the third instance of the class scripts/release_ci_gate.py was written to prevent.
  - *Evidence:* Reproduced every leg. `python scripts/release_ci_gate.py v5.75.0 --check-only` → `tag v5.75.0 43ef30ca / ci completed failure REFUSE / run 35280263679 - a red commit must not become a release`, EXIT=1. `gh api repos/:owner/:repo/actions/runs/35280263679` → `head_sha=43ef30cad45a... status=completed concl=failure created=2026-09-17T22:05:49Z updated=2026-09-17T22:11:17Z event=push`. `gh release view v5.75.0 --json assets,publishedAt` → `assets=0 pub=2026-09-17T22:05:54Z draft=false pre=false`; v5.71.0/v5.72.0/v5.73.0/v5.74.0 each `assets=4`. Publish − run-start = 5s; publish → v5.75.1 publish (22:50:15Z) = 44m21s, matching their "~45 minutes". Prior instances confirmed in source at scripts/release_ci_gate.py:3-5 (v5.64.0, v5.65.0 concluded failure; v5.65.2 tagged in_progress). Two precision notes they did not surface: (a) the run was still in_progress at publish — the conclusion landed 22:11:17Z, 5m23s AFTER the release went out, so this is shipping *unchecked*, not shipping *knowingly red*; (b) `gh api .../runs/35280263679/jobs` shows exactly one context failed — `test (macos-latest, 3.11) => failure` — with `ubuntu 3.11/3.14` and `macos 3.14` all `cancelled` by fail-fast.
- **CONFIRMED** — scripts/tag_release.py was not used for EITHER v5.75.0 or v5.75.1; the published v5.75.1 notes disclose only the release_ci_gate.py skip, never the tag-gate skip.
  - *Evidence:* `git for-each-ref refs/tags/<t> --format='%(subject)'` for v5.68.0..v5.74.0 returns `SYNAPSE <tag> -- release-truth gate passed` for all eight; v5.75.0 returns `v5.75.0 -- the second write, and the silence around it` and v5.75.1 returns `v5.75.1 -- the tag that was checked`. The signature string is hardcoded at scripts/tag_release.py:144 (`'-m', f'SYNAPSE {tag} -- release-truth gate passed'`), inside the `git tag -a` call beginning at :143 — their "143-144" range is correct. sync_check is at :92-102 exactly (`p = _run([sys.executable, ... 'sync_version.py'), '--check'])` at :92, `print('sync_check   drift              REFUSE')` at :98, `'all CONFORM        OK'` at :102). Disclosure gap confirmed: `grep -nE "tag_release|release_ci_gate" docs/releases/v5.75.1.md` returns only three release_ci_gate hits (:15, :17, :54) and zero tag_release hits. Both tags are annotated objects by Joseph Ibrahim (`%(objecttype)` = tag), 2026-09-17 18:05:16 and 18:49:51 -0400.
- **CONFIRMED** — The drift that reddened v5.75.0 is verifiable in the tagged tree and is exactly what tag_release.py's preflight checks, so the tag was created on a tree its own gate would have refused.
  - *Evidence:* Confirmed and strengthened — they inferred this; I executed it. `git show 43ef30ca:VERSION` → 5.75.0; `:pyproject.toml | grep -m1 '^version'` → `version = "5.75.0"`; `:CLAUDE.md | grep -oE 'SYNAPSE v[0-9.]+'` → `SYNAPSE v5.74.0`; `:README.md | sed -n '7p'` → `<p align="center"><sub>v5.74.0 · Houdini 22.0.400 · Python 3.13 runtime<br>tags: v5.74.0 is Latest</sub></p>` (they quoted this with an ellipsis, dropping the `<p align="center">` wrapper — cosmetic). Both are declared surfaces at scripts/sync_version.py:49 (`claude_md`) and :51 (`readme`). ### What the surveyor did not look at inside its own dimension

- docs/RELEASE_CARD.md — the operator's card they cite five times for steps 4, 10-12, 13 and 19 — never names scripts/release_ci_gate.py. `grep -n "release_ci_gate" docs/RELEASE_CARD.md` → no output, exit 1. The card's tag-and-push block goes 13 Gate C commit → 14 tag_release.py --check-only → 15 tag_release.py → 16 push → 17 compose assets → 18 gh release create. The gate written specifically to sit between step 16 and step 18 is absent from the checklist an operator actually follows. Their openQuestion asks "why did both releases bypass the gates? UNKNOWN. Nothing in the tree records the decision." For the CI gate, the tree records it plainly: the ritual document omits it. That is a findable structural cause, not an unknown.
- docs/RELEASE_CARD.md:68 — step 18 reads `gh release create vX.Y.Z --latest --notes-file <public body> + 4 assets`. v5.75.1 shipped 3. They cite steps 4, 10-12, 13 and 19 but never 18, so the asset shortfall is never stated as the card violation it is.
- What the 5.75 line actually shipped — the most important number in this dimension and it is absent. `git log --oneline v5.74.0..v5.75.1` → 5 commits; `git diff --stat v5.74.0 v5.75.1 -- python/` → 15 files, 2,998 insertions, 64 deletions, including two brand-new modules (python/synapse/memory/primary_repair.py +1,010, backfill_from_moneta.py +727) plus moneta_store.py +235, store.py +268, server/write_plane.py +268, server/handlers.py +84, panel/health_strip.py +208. That is the body of work that went out with no install qualification, no seat receipt and no cut record. The report characterises the *process* failure thoroughly and never sizes the *payload* that rode it.
- They never ran scripts/release_ci_gate.py against v5.75.1 — the one cheap check that would confirm the tag's own name ("the tag that was checked") is honest. I ran it: `python scripts/release_ci_gate.py v5.75.1 --check-only` → `tag v5.75.1 53e4f9cb / ci completed success OK`, EXIT=0. A verified positive left on the table in a report that otherwise reaches for positives.
- They never audited the 27 non-version tags (15 orphaned, one a published prerelease) or the 90-vs-92 gap between GitHub releases and v-tags. Both are squarely inside "whether any tag points at a commit that was later amended or rebased."
- They never tested the SUPERSEDED banner they quote approvingly. It asserts the drift "reds three conformance tests on every required context" — but only one required context completed; the other three were cancelled by fail-fast. The remediation notice itself contains an unmeasured claim, which is the exact pattern the report is auditing.
- docs/releases/ holds 16 .md files (`ls docs/releases/*.md | wc -l`) against 92 v-tags. They flag v5.72.1.md as the anomaly in that directory without establishing what the directory's convention is, which is what would let a reader judge whether an orphaned note is unusual.
- Nothing checked whether anything *enforces* RELEASE_CARD step 4's CHANGELOG promotion. They report the omission for 5.75.0/5.75.1 but never ask whether a test or hook was supposed to catch it — the same question they asked (and answered) for the banner.

### Open questions

- Does the v5.75.1 installer payload actually correspond to the v5.75.1 tag tree? UNKNOWN. The build report asserts source_revision=53e4f9cb, but v5.74.0 proved that assertion independently (harness/notes/release-5.74.0/payload_vs_tag.py + payload-vs-v5.74.0-5.74.0.json, 415 files byte-identical). No equivalent producer was run for 5.75.1 — there is no release-5.75.1 directory — so the claim currently rests on the builder's own stamp. Re-running payload_vs_tag.py against the published Setup would close it.
- Why did v5.75.0 and v5.75.1 both bypass scripts/tag_release.py? UNKNOWN. Possible readings: the gate refused (VERSION is a Gate C pre-commit path per RELEASE_CARD step 13, and a refused bump commit would cascade) and was worked around by hand, or it simply was not invoked. The tags were created by Joseph Ibrahim on 2026-09-17 at 18:05:16 and 18:49:51 — 44 minutes apart. Nothing in the tree records the decision.
- Is v5.75.0 intended to stay published as a normal (non-prerelease) release? It carries a SUPERSEDED banner in its body but is still isDraft=false, isPrerelease=false with zero assets. Marking it prerelease would demote it out of the ordinary listing without destroying the record.
- Should docs/releases/ hold notes for versions that were never released? v5.72.1.md is the current instance. An explicit ABANDONED/SUPERSEDED header convention in the file itself would close both that and the v5.75.0 divergence.
- Nothing checks that GitHub's retained Latest matches the README banner — the toolcount test explicitly disclaims remote attestation. Should publication-time verification (RELEASE_CARD step 19) become a script with a receipt, given it is the step whose absence made v5.75.0 invisible to the suite?
- Was a full stock-suite or seat-suite run performed for v5.75.1 at all? UNKNOWN — no suite-5.75.1.txt or seat-5.75.1.txt exists anywhere. CI on 53e4f9cb concluded success on the four required contexts, which covers the stock suite on Linux/macOS, but the Windows seat suite (RELEASE_CARD step 11) has no receipt.

## architecture-truth — DEGRADED

> The bridge is real, well-built and improving — but CLAUDE.md's load-bearing claim that "/mcp goes through the audited bridge" is false for the transport actually configured, and consent does not block on any path in the process, bridge or live.

**Trajectory.** Converging — but onto the LIVE handler path, not onto the bridge. `git grep -c "hou.undos.group" -- python/synapse/server/` summed: `@{6 weeks ago}`=54, `@{4 weeks ago}`=54, `@{2 weeks ago}`=54, HEAD=62. Every architecture commit in the window hardens the live path in place: c938ef0a (handlers_node create/connect/delete undo groups), b07e3a08 (W5-UNDOB set_parm + set_keyframe wrap, envelope + CLAUDE.md sync), a2af501b "feat(trust): the Undo Receipt -- tell the artist what one Ctrl+Z reverses", b86676b1 (insert_cache decision-ID handshake), ce05bab0 "fix(bridge): execute native history outside new undo groups". Zero commits in the last 6 weeks move traffic onto LosslessExecutionBridge or wire a real gate. Practically, the two paths are merging by the live path absorbing the bridge's properties one anchor at a time, while the doc still describes them as two peers. That is convergence on capability and divergence from the document.

**The refuter on where this report overstates itself.** Yes — on two counts, and one of them is serious.

(1) The headline's second clause — "consent does not block on any path in the process, bridge or live" — is stated as universal and is not. It is true of HumanGate. It is false as a statement about the system's authorization posture: worker_policy.py, default-on, hard-DENIES execute_python, execute_vex, delete_node, renders and exports for the autonomous panel worker (worker_policy.py:64 `_DENIED_GATES`, enforced at claude_worker.py:401-402, armed at synapse_panel.py:3422). They never opened that file. The artistImpact sentence "A delete_node, an export, a render submit and an arbitrary execute_python all proceed identically" is simply wrong for that path.

(2) Finding 3 is marked severity "blocker" and loadBearing:true on the strength of a grep that missed a package `__init__` import edge — and they wrote in their own openQuestion that they chose not to run the probe that would have settled it. The probe is a stateless GET returning 406. It took five seconds and refutes the finding. A blocker-severity claim that the author knew was unverified, published as a finding rather than as an open question, is the report's worst move. It also propagates: "the audited path has no external client at all" is false, and finding 14's "no other production bridge instances" reassurance reads very differently once you know the live /mcp endpoint routes through `execute_through_bridge`.

Status "degraded" is defensible — the doc genuinely overstates the configured transport, HumanGate genuinely never fires, and half the mutating surface genuinely lacks an undo group. But the headline sentence "CLAUDE.md's load-bearing claim that /mcp goes through the audited bridge is false" is over-broad: it is false for the stdio transport Joe has configured, and TRUE for the in-Houdini HTTP transport that is live right now. The honest headline is narrower and more useful: there are two /mcp transports, one audited and one not, and the one Joe's client uses is the unaudited one.

Two smaller confidence excesses: the 9-entry skip set (it is 13, verified by import) and the 6-week undo-group count (a reflog clamp, not a repo fact). Both are the "measured from the doc/from local state rather than from code" class they were sent to hunt for.

### Findings

#### [BLOCKER · load-bearing] The `/mcp` transport that is actually configured never touches LosslessExecutionBridge. `.mcp.json` wires `python mcp_server.py` (stdio), and that process forwards every tool call over WebSocket to the live `/synapse` server — the same handler path `/synapse` uses. CLAUDE.md Identity and §1 call `/mcp` "the audited path"; for the transport in use that is false.

**Evidence.** `cat .mcp.json` -> {"mcpServers":{"synapse":{"type":"stdio","command":"python","args":["mcp_server.py"]}}}. mcp_server.py:20 architecture comment: `Claude Desktop <-[stdio/JSON-RPC]-> mcp_server.py <-[WebSocket]-> Synapse (Houdini)`. mcp_server.py:1137 `@server.call_tool()` -> :1172 `data = await send_command(cmd_type, payload)`. `grep -n "dispatch_tool|bridge|execute_through_bridge|handler.handle" mcp_server.py` returns only two unrelated hits (:166 resolve_endpoint, :604 a comment) — no bridge reference anywhere in the file.

**For the artist.** Every tool call Joe makes from Claude Code or Claude Desktop today gets the live path's properties, not the bridge's: no consent gate, no USD composition validation, no rollback-on-violation. Undo behaviour is whatever the individual handler happens to wrap.

#### [BLOCKER · load-bearing] Consent never blocks anywhere in the process — bridge path included. The only production constructor of a bridge is `get_process_bridge()`, which unconditionally builds with an auto-approve callback and then nulls the gate; `bridge_adapter.get_bridge()` re-asserts that. §1.2.1 tier 1 ("HumanGate ... APPROVE/CRITICAL block and poll at 250ms") and §11 rule 5 ("Consent gates are real — on the bridge path only") describe a path that does not exist.

**Evidence.** shared/bridge.py:2949-2957 `bridge = LosslessExecutionBridge(consent_callback=lambda op: True)` then `bridge._gate = None`. python/synapse/panel/bridge_adapter.py:275-277 `_bridge = get_process_bridge()` ... `_bridge._consent_callback = _panel_consent` / `_bridge._gate = None`. shared/bridge.py:2448-2462 `_check_consent` prefers `self._gate`, falls through to the callback. `grep -rn "LosslessExecutionBridge("` excluding tests/harness/worktrees returns exactly three sites: scripts/bench_scale.py:242, scripts/probe_stage_hash_floor.py:123, shared/bridge.py:2954. The file states it itself at shared/bridge.py:2541-2547: "It is dead today ONLY because _check_consent prefers self._gate, and no production path ever sets one."

**For the artist.** No SYNAPSE operation ever pauses to ask. A delete_node, an export, a render submit and an arbitrary execute_python all proceed identically. The REVIEW/APPROVE/CRITICAL table in §1.2 is aspirational, not a runtime behaviour.

#### [BLOCKER · load-bearing] The one code path that would route an MCP client through the bridge — the in-Houdini hwebserver `/mcp` HTTP endpoint — is armed only by importing `synapse.mcp.server`, and no production startup path imports that module.

**Evidence.** python/synapse/mcp/server.py:949 `@hwebserver.urlHandler("/mcp")` sits inside a module-level `try: import hwebserver` block, so registration happens at import time. `grep -rn "synapse\.mcp|from \.\.mcp|from \.mcp" python/synapse/server/ python/synapse/panel/ python/synapse/__init__.py` returns only `..mcp.tool_impls.solaris` (handlers_solaris_tools.py:24) and `synapse.mcp._tool_registry` (panel/face_token.py:546, tool_bridge.py:11, tool_palette.py:147, worker_policy.py:29) — never `synapse.mcp.server`. `grep -rn mcp houdini/ packages/` returns nothing. python/synapse/server/hwebserver_adapter.py:328-400 (`start_hwebserver`) does not import it either.

**For the artist.** None directly — but it means the audited path has no external client at all. UNKNOWN whether the endpoint is live in the running process: I did not probe HTTP :9999/mcp, only the static import graph.

#### [WATCH · load-bearing] Live-path undo wrapping is real but covers roughly half the mutating surface. Measured at HEAD: 132 registered commands, 41 in `_READ_ONLY_COMMANDS`; of the 91 mutating ones, 47 wrap in `hou.undos.group`/`undo_context`, 45 do not. CLAUDE.md's scope caveat ("live mutating handlers beyond this tracked set are still not audited here") is honest but understates the size of the gap.

**Evidence.** Script run at C:/Users/User/AppData/Local/Temp/claude/C--Users-User-SYNAPSE/f1c652fa-7459-4560-81ac-291364e46ae1/scratchpad/undo_audit.py (regex over reg.register() targets + per-method body scan across python/synapse/server/). Output: `registered: 132 read_only_set: 41 / MUTATING with undo wrap: 47 / MUTATING WITHOUT undo wrap: 45 / unresolved targets: 0`. Caveat I verified by hand: the five `solaris_*` rows delegate through handlers_solaris_tools.py:56-78 to python/synapse/mcp/tool_impls/solaris/*, which DO wrap (component_builder.py:263, create_variants.py:171, scene_template.py:211, set_purpose.py:299, import_megascans.py:202), and several rows (doctor, memory_query, hda_list, query_prims, write_report, undo, redo) are correctly unwrapped. So the true unwrapped count is lower than 45 — I did not resolve it exactly.

**For the artist.** Ctrl+Z after a SYNAPSE operation is inconsistent. Some ops reverse in one step; others leave a pile of individual entries.

#### [WATCH · load-bearing] Two concrete, artist-visible undo holes confirmed by reading the handler bodies: `render_settings` and `tops_setup_wedge`. The entire `handlers_tops/` tree contains exactly one `hou.undos.group`.

**Evidence.** python/synapse/server/handlers_render.py:1190-1231 `_handle_render_settings` — `for k, v in sorted(overrides.items()): p = node.parm(k); if p: p.set(v)` with no undo group anywhere in the method. python/synapse/server/handlers_tops/wedge.py:85-190 `_handle_tops_setup_wedge` — `node.createNode("wedge", wedge_name)` then `multiparm.set(...)`, `name_parm.set(...)`, `type_parm.set(...)`, `range{idx}x/y.set(...)`, `steps_parm.set(...)` per attribute, no group. `grep -rn undos python/synapse/server/handlers_tops/` returns one line: render_sequence.py:182.

**For the artist.** One `synapse_render_settings` call with five overrides = five separate undo entries. One `tops_setup_wedge` with three attributes = a node creation plus ~19 separate parm entries to walk back. That is the exact failure the Undo Receipt feature (a2af501b) was built to make visible.

#### [WATCH] CLAUDE.md §1.1 and §1.5 name the wrong main-thread primitive. The bridge does NOT use `hdefereval.executeInMainThreadWithResult()` — it deliberately replaced it, and says why. The bridge's own module header carries the same stale claim.

**Evidence.** shared/bridge.py:2066-2071: "Was: hdefereval.executeInMainThreadWithResult(_sync_payload). That is the vendor's BLOCKING marshal, and it carries the second vendor defect: it reads its result back out of hdefereval MODULE GLOBALS (`result = _last_result`), so two concurrent blocking marshals ... can hand each other's results back." shared/bridge.py:2088 `_rom = _resolve_marshal()`. python/synapse/server/main_thread.py:5-6: "Uses hdefereval.executeDeferred() (non-blocking) + threading.Event instead of executeInMainThreadWithResult() (blocking, no timeout)." Stale internal claims at shared/bridge.py:10 and :16.

**For the artist.** None directly — but the doc points a future maintainer at the primitive the codebase spent effort removing, which is how the freeze class comes back.

#### [WATCH · load-bearing] Live ops do emit path-qualified observe-only IntegrityBlocks exactly as §1 and §1.3 describe — but with three coverage limits the doc does not state: a 9-command skip set, an env kill switch, and a silent `None` when the main thread is stalled.

**Evidence.** python/synapse/server/handlers.py:546 `_enveloped = _mutating and _envelope.envelope_active(cmd_type)`, :551-563 two `capture_scene_hash` calls bracketing `registry.invoke()` inside the C5 lock, :655 `_envelope.record_live_block(...)` (the single call site). Schema: shared/bridge.py:663-666 `execution_path` / `consent_applicable` / `composition_applicable` / `undo_applicable`; :697-700 `anchors_hold` honours the applicability flags. Undocumented limits: integrity_envelope.py:81-100 ENVELOPE_SKIP_COMMANDS (9 entries incl. add_memory, decide, write_report, doctor); :125-133 `SYNAPSE_LIVE_ENVELOPE=0` kill switch; :207-212 `if is_main_thread_stalled(): return None` and :233-236 RuntimeError -> None on capture timeout.

**For the artist.** None visible. The provenance trail is genuine but has honest holes during a busy cook — which the code records as `hash_unavailable` rather than faking.

#### [WATCH] All of CLAUDE.md §1.5 ("Async Execution Boundary (R2)") describes a method with zero production callers, and the FastMCP framing around it is also wrong.
