No caller in python/, scripts/, or mcp_server.py. mcp_server.py:42 `from mcp.server import Server` + `from mcp.server.stdio import stdio_server` — the low-level SDK server, not FastMCP. The only live entry is the sync `execute()` (shared/bridge.py:1728).

**For the artist.** None. It is a documented mechanism that runs only in tests — the kind of thing that reads as shipped capability in a review.

#### [WATCH] §2's live-path note is right that MOERouter is panel-side only — but on the running host neither router is even initialized for tool traffic.

**Evidence.** Live read-only query against the running Houdini (pid 32804): `synapse_router_stats` -> `{"error":"Router not initialized"}`. python/synapse/server/handlers.py:1760 `if not hasattr(self, "_router"):` — TieredRouter is constructed lazily inside `_handle_chat`, not at startup. MOERouter's only non-test caller was deleted: python/synapse/panel/tool_filter.py:5-11 documents the removal; shared/router.py:48 "To revive: a production caller of MOERouter.route() must exist first."

**For the artist.** None. Tool dispatch is a flat registry lookup (handlers.py:506 `self._registry.get(cmd_type)`); the 6-specialist MOE story in §2 is orchestration-persona framing, not a live mechanism.

#### [WATCH · load-bearing] The harness check meant to catch fail-open mutation has gone partly blind: its regex no longer matches `python/synapse/mcp/tools.py` even though the fallback it hunts is still there.

**Evidence.** harness/verify/checks.py:1922 regex `except ImportError:\s*\n\s*response = handler\.handle\(command\)`. Ran it against HEAD: tool_executor.py -> False, mcp/tools.py -> False, bridge_adapter.py -> True. But mcp/tools.py:129-134 still reads `except ImportError:` / `from synapse.core.farm_contract import is_farm_control` / `if is_farm_control(tool_name): return {...}` / `response = handler.handle(command)` — two farm-control lines were inserted between the `except` and the fallback, so the anchored regex stopped matching. The check still returns ok:False via bridge_adapter, so it is red, but it now names 1 of the 3 sites it was written to name.

**For the artist.** None directly. It means a future fix to bridge_adapter alone would turn this check green while two fail-open paths survive.

#### [INFO · load-bearing] Live-path `execute_python`/`execute_vex` are ungated exactly as §1.2's live-path note says — full `__builtins__`, no import filter, no consent, no length cap. This is the one place the doc is precisely right, and it is pinned.

**Evidence.** python/synapse/server/handlers.py:1316 `exec_globals = {"hou": hou, "__builtins__": __builtins__}`. No HumanGate / _check_consent / .propose( / GateProposal anywhere in the handler. Ran `python -m pytest tests/test_phase0b_consent_posture.py -q` -> `2 passed, 2 warnings in 0.54s` (narrow, 2 tests, no hou, no store access). The test asserts against live source via inspect.getsource.

**For the artist.** Arbitrary Python executes in the Houdini session on request with no prompt. Correct and intended for single-user localhost; it is the stated blocker for any studio deployment.

#### [INFO · load-bearing] `run_on_main` IS the marshal of record on the live path — the doc is right about this, and the envelope's own assertion of `main_thread_executed=True` rests on it.

**Evidence.** `grep -rl run_on_main --include=*.py python/synapse/ | grep -v __pycache__ | wc -l` -> 55 files, including every handlers_*.py. python/synapse/server/integrity_envelope.py:33-35: "main_thread_executed=True IS asserted: every hou-touching live handler marshals through server.main_thread.run_on_main (code-verified)." python/synapse/server/main_thread.py:441 `def run_on_main(fn, timeout=_DEFAULT_TIMEOUT, ...)`, :617-620 posts via `hdefereval.executeDeferred`. Timeouts: main_thread.py:21-22 `_DEFAULT_TIMEOUT = 10.0`, `_SLOW_TIMEOUT = 30.0` — matching the Identity section's "30s slow-op timeout".

**For the artist.** This is the anchor that keeps Houdini from crashing on off-thread hou calls. It holds.

#### [INFO] §1.7's PDG rollback citation points at the wrong lines. The substance (broken signature, failure recorded not rolled back) still holds; the line numbers drifted ~660 lines.

**Evidence.** CLAUDE.md §1.7 cites `shared/bridge.py:1718` for `dirtyAllTasks(remove_files=...)` and `:1723` for `rollback_note`. Actual: shared/bridge.py:2377 `lambda: top_node.dirtyAllTasks(remove_files=remove_files)`, :2382 `rollback_note = f" Rollback (dirtyAllTasks) failed: {_rb_exc}."`. Line 1718 at HEAD is inside `_undo_group_evidence`'s warning block.

#### [INFO] `bridge_adapter.py` contradicts itself in the same file about who HumanGate governs.

**Evidence.** python/synapse/panel/bridge_adapter.py:246-248: "HumanGate still governs AUTONOMOUS / MCP operations, which use their own bridge instances — not this panel singleton." But :253-277 `get_bridge()` returns `get_process_bridge()` (the process-wide singleton) and sets `_bridge._gate = None` on it, and :401-402 `execute_through_bridge` — the /mcp adapter entry — calls that same `get_bridge()`. There are no other production bridge instances (see finding 2 evidence).

**For the artist.** None. It is the kind of stale reassurance that makes a reviewer stop looking.

#### [INFO] The live path's newest safety work is a propose-then-commit handshake, not consent. `synapse_insert_cache`'s "Review gate" is a decision-ID round trip, not a HumanGate block.

**Evidence.** python/synapse/server/handlers_cache.py:923-939 `decision_id = resolve_param(payload, "decision_id")` / `entry = store.lookup(decision_id)`; :667-673 rejects with "decision_id ... is not in the issued-decision store -- it was never [issued]. Run synapse_assess_cache first and use the decision_id it returns."; :678-695 expiry and strategy-drift rejections. No HumanGate, no consent poll. Commit b86676b1.

**For the artist.** Positive — the artist (or the agent on their behalf) must assess before inserting, and a stale or drifted plan is refused. It is a real guard, just not the one §1.2 describes.

### Refuter verdicts

- **CONFIRMED** — F1 (loadBearing): The /mcp transport that is actually configured never touches LosslessExecutionBridge — .mcp.json wires `python mcp_server.py` (stdio), which forwards every tool call over WebSocket to the live /synapse server.
  - *Evidence:* Reproduced every cited line. `cat C:/Users/User/SYNAPSE/.mcp.json` -> {"mcpServers":{"synapse":{"type":"stdio","command":"python","args":["mcp_server.py"]}}}. mcp_server.py:20 architecture comment; mcp_server.py:1137 `@server.call_tool()`; mcp_server.py:1172 `data = await send_command(cmd_type, payload)`; mcp_server.py:371 `async def send_command` -> :401 `await ws.send(...)`. mcp_server.py:152 `SYNAPSE_PATH = os.environ.get("SYNAPSE_PATH", "/synapse")`. `grep -nE "dispatch_tool|LosslessExecutionBridge|execute_through_bridge|handler\.handle|bridge" mcp_server.py` -> only :166 (bridge_endpoint port discovery) and :604 (unrelated comment). No bridge in the file.
- **CORRECTED** — F2 (loadBearing): Consent never blocks anywhere in the process — bridge path included. get_process_bridge() builds auto-approve and nulls the gate; bridge_adapter re-asserts it.
  - *Correction:* HumanGate never blocks on any path — that part holds. But "consent never blocks anywhere in the process" and the artistImpact ("A delete_node, an export, a render submit and an arbitrary execute_python all proceed identically") are false for the autonomous panel worker, which has a real, enforced, default-on DENY layer they never opened. python/synapse/panel/worker_policy.py:138 `is_tool_allowed_for_worker`; default mode is `standard` (worker_policy.py:57 `_DEFAULT_MODE = _MODE_STANDARD`) and worker_policy.py:64 `_DENIED_GATES = frozenset({"review", "approve", "critical"})` — i.e. execute_python, execute_vex, delete_node, renders, exports are refused outright, plus unknown tools fail closed. Enforced at panel/claude_worker.py:401-402 `if self._enforce_worker_policy: allowed, reason = is_tool_allowed_for_worker(tool_name)` with `enforce_worker_policy=True` passed at panel/synapse_panel.py:3422. Also: cited line numbers drifted — the "It is dead today ONLY" docstring starts at shared/bridge.py:2540 (not 2541-2547), and the bridge_adapter block is :271-275 (not :275-277).
  - *Evidence:* The HumanGate half reproduces exactly: shared/bridge.py:2954 `bridge = LosslessExecutionBridge(consent_callback=lambda op: True)` / :2955 `bridge._gate = None`; bridge_adapter.py:271-275 `_bridge = get_process_bridge()` then `_consent_callback = _panel_consent` / `_bridge._gate = None`; _panel_consent is bridge_adapter.py:235-250 `return True`; _check_consent at shared/bridge.py:2448-2462 prefers `self._gate`. Constructor census reproduced exactly three prod sites: scripts/bench_scale.py:242, scripts/probe_stage_hash_floor.py:123, shared/bridge.py:2954. TieredRouter also takes `gate: Optional[HumanGate]` (routing/router.py:178) but handlers.py:1777 constructs it as `TieredRouter(config=config, memory=owner)` — no gate — and `self._gate` is never read anywhere in that file.
- **REFUTED** — F3 (loadBearing, severity blocker): The in-Houdini hwebserver /mcp HTTP endpoint is armed only by importing synapse.mcp.server, and no production startup path imports that module.
  - *Correction:* The /mcp HTTP endpoint IS registered and answering in the live process right now. This also refutes their own openQuestion 1 and materially changes the picture: python/synapse/mcp/tools.py:118-134 routes every non-read-only tool through `execute_through_bridge`, so the in-Houdini /mcp endpoint is genuinely bridge-routed. The correct statement is narrower than theirs: the audited path exists and is live; what is true is that the *stdio* client Joe actually has configured bypasses it. Raising this to a "blocker" on a static grep, when a 5-second read-only probe was available and the task explicitly permitted read-only live queries, is the single worst error in the report.
  - *Evidence:* Two independent disproofs. (a) Static: python/synapse/mcp/__init__.py:19 `from .server import MCPServer, SSEEventBus` — importing ANY submodule of the package initialises the package and therefore imports `.server`. The four sites the surveyor found (`panel/tool_bridge.py:11`, `panel/worker_policy.py:29`, `panel/tool_palette.py`, `panel/face_token.py:546`) all import `synapse.mcp._tool_registry`, which does exactly that. Reproduced in-process: `python -c "import synapse.mcp._tool_registry; print('synapse.mcp.server' in sys.modules)"` -> True, MCP_SERVER_AVAILABLE -> True. (b) Live: a stateless read-only GET against the running Houdini (pid 32804) — `curl -s -m 5 -w "%{http_code}" http://localhost:9999/mcp` -> **406** with body `{"error": "GET /mcp requires Accept: text/event-stream"}`, byte-identical to python/synapse/mcp/server.py:1016-1021. Controls: GET /synapse -> 404, GET /zzz_not_a_route -> 404.
- **CONFIRMED** — F4 (loadBearing): Live-path execute_python/execute_vex are ungated — full __builtins__, no consent, no import filter — and it is pinned by tests/test_phase0b_consent_posture.py.
  - *Evidence:* python/synapse/server/handlers.py:1316 `exec_globals = {"hou": hou, "__builtins__": __builtins__}` — exact line. Stronger than their evidence: `grep -nE "HumanGate|_check_consent|GateProposal|\.propose\(" python/synapse/server/handlers.py` returns ZERO hits for the entire 2000+ line file, and handlers.py:503-575 `handle()` dispatch does no consent check either. Ran `python -m pytest tests/test_phase0b_consent_posture.py -q` -> `2 passed, 2 warnings in 0.50s`. Caveat worth recording: that test is narrower than it reads — it only `inspect.getsource`s `_handle_execute_python` / `_handle_execute_vex` method bodies, so a gate added at the dispatch layer (where a real gate would go) would leave it green. It pins the posture, not the absence of gating.

One thing they missed that strengthens their own point: tool_executor.py is blind for a *second, independent* reason — python/synapse/panel/tool_executor.py:663 now reads `except ImportError as _marshal_exc:`, which the anchored `except ImportError:\s*\n` pattern cannot match at all. So two distinct drifts, not one, and fixing the farm-control insertion alone would still leave tool_executor.py unnamed.
- **CONFIRMED** — F14 (marked loadBearing FALSE): bridge_adapter.py contradicts itself about who HumanGate governs.
  - *Evidence:* Spot-check requested. python/synapse/panel/bridge_adapter.py:246-248 "HumanGate still governs AUTONOMOUS / MCP operations, which use their own bridge instances — not this panel singleton", against :271 `_bridge = get_process_bridge()` / :275 `_bridge._gate = None` and :400-403 `bridge = get_bridge()` inside `execute_through_bridge` (def at :379). Classification objection: given that I proved the in-Houdini /mcp endpoint is live and mcp/tools.py:126 calls `execute_through_bridge`, this stale docstring is the exact sentence that would stop a reviewer discovering the live /mcp path has no gate. That is load-bearing.
- **CONFIRMED** — F15 (marked loadBearing FALSE): synapse_insert_cache's "Review gate" is a decision-ID round trip, not a HumanGate block.
  - *Evidence:* Spot-check requested. python/synapse/server/handlers_cache.py:923 `decision_id = resolve_param(payload, "decision_id")`, :929 `entry = store.lookup(decision_id)`; rejection paths at :667-673 ("is not in the issued-decision store"), :675-682 (TTL expiry), :684-691 (strategy drift), :693-698 (unsupported strategy). No HumanGate, no consent poll anywhere in the file. Commit b86676b1 dated 2026-09-15, subject matches.
- **CORRECTED** — Trajectory: `git grep -c "hou.undos.group" -- python/synapse/server/` summed to 54 / 54 / 54 / 62 at 6wk / 4wk / 2wk / HEAD.
  - *Correction:* The real series is 46 -> 54 -> 54 -> 62, a steeper climb than they reported. Their conclusion (the live path is absorbing the bridge's properties one anchor at a time, while the doc still calls the two paths peers) survives and is in fact better supported — but the number they showed was a reflog artifact, which is precisely the failure class this project has been burned by. The cited commits all verify: c938ef0a (2026-08-15), b07e3a08 (2026-08-15), a2af501b (2026-09-15), b86676b1 (2026-09-15), ce05bab0 (2026-09-08). `git log --since="6 weeks ago" -- shared/bridge.py` returns only 2 commits (1d982426, ce05bab0), supporting "zero commits move traffic onto the bridge."
  - *Evidence:* `git rev-parse "@{6 weeks ago}"` emits `warning: log for 'master' only goes back to Mon, 17 Aug 2026 15:29:37 -0400` and silently clamps to the oldest reflog entry — roughly 4.5 weeks, not 6. The reflog is per-clone local state, so their 6-week datapoint is a property of this machine's checkout, not of the project. Re-measured against real commits by date: `git rev-list -1 --before="6 weeks ago" master` -> 1f18ab46 -> **46**; 4 weeks -> c179767b -> 54; 2 weeks -> 6e3dd963 -> 54; HEAD -> 62.
- **CORRECTED** — openQuestion 2: "What share of live traffic, if any, goes through the panel path to LosslessExecutionBridge? If it is zero in practice, the bridge is instrumentation-only."
  - *Correction:* LosslessExecutionBridge.execute() has run at least once in the live process this session. The bridge is NOT instrumentation-only — it is low-traffic but reachable and exercised. Combined with the live 406 on /mcp, the report's central framing ("the audited path has no external client at all") does not hold.
  - *Evidence:* This was answerable with a read-only live call they had available and did not make. `synapse_metrics` against pid 32804 reports `synapse_stage_touch_ms_count 1` (and `synapse_stage_touch_ms_sum 0.0027`). `_record_stage_touch_ms` has exactly one caller — shared/bridge.py:1496, inside `_infer_stage_touch` (:1481) — and `_infer_stage_touch` has exactly two callers, shared/bridge.py:1759 (inside `execute()`, def at :1728) and :1980 (inside `execute_async()`).

### What the surveyor did not look at inside its own dimension

- RBAC — the largest uncovered claim in their own dimension. CLAUDE.md Identity states the live path is "`/synapse` (live WS) → `server.handlers` directly: RBAC-gated". They never tested it, and it is false twice over on the running configuration. `check_permission` is enforced at exactly one site: python/synapse/server/websocket.py:679 — the LEGACY transport. The live transport is python/synapse/server/hwebserver_adapter.py:105 `@hwebserver.webSocket("/synapse")` (started by panel/synapse_panel.py:1165 `start_hwebserver(port=9999)`), and `grep -n "rbac|check_permission" hwebserver_adapter.py handlers.py` returns nothing. And even on the legacy path it is inert: rbac.py:202-203 `mode = os.environ.get("SYNAPSE_DEPLOY_MODE", "local"); return mode != "local"`. My live HTTP probe confirms hwebserver is the serving transport (it answers HTTP on :9999).
- execute_vex has NO undo group at all — and the doc says it does. python/synapse/server/handlers.py:1439-1443: `# F1 freeze-relief: execute_vex has no undo group of its own — sandwich wraps the whole create/set/cook payload` / `with cook_sandwich(label="execute_vex")`. A cook_sandwich is an update-mode manipulation, not an undo group. CLAUDE.md Identity claims "usd/material/cops/batch/**execute** handlers wrap in hou.undos.group", and python/synapse/server/integrity_envelope.py:22-24 repeats it. My registry scan lists execute_vex among the unwrapped. This is a live Ctrl+Z hole on a CRITICAL-gated tool, sitting inside the exact doc sentence they were auditing.
- The converse understatement, also missed: execute_python does MORE than the doc grants. handlers.py:1340-1356 wraps in `hou.undos.group("synapse_execute")` AND performs a real `hou.undos.performUndo()` rollback on coding-bug exceptions. CLAUDE.md's "grouping only, no rollback — wrapping is not reversing" is wrong in the safe direction here. A review that only hunts overstatement will keep missing this class.
- Anchor 4 (Scene Integrity) was never tested. §1.1 promises "USD composition validation after mutation — Stage traversal checks composition arcs. Rollback on violation." No finding touches `_verify_composition`, and the strongest anchor claim in the table went unexamined.
- §1.4 (R1 topological hashing: cookCount + sessionId + geo intrinsics) and §1.2's R4 disk-write auto-elevation to APPROVE were both left untested. R4 in particular is a structural claim that is cheap to verify.
- §1.2's gate-level table was never diffed against shared/constants.OPERATION_GATES. I did it: the table is accurate, with five undocumented additions (read_stage, history_undo, history_redo at inform; sleep_pass at approve). Worth stating — a section that checks out is a finding too, and they left the reader unable to tell "verified correct" from "not looked at".
- §1.8 Emergency Halt makes a precise, falsifiable artist-facing claim ("Partial operations remain artist-undoable ... Say 'undoable', never 'reversed'") and was not audited, despite python/synapse/server/emergency_live.py existing.
- The worker_policy authorization layer as a whole — not just as a correction to finding 2, but as a structural fact about the architecture. There IS a real, default-on, fail-closed gate in this system. A review whose headline is "consent does not block anywhere" and which never mentions it gives the reader a materially wrong model of the safety surface.
- Their own openQuestion 2 was answerable from the live process they were already connected to (synapse_metrics -> synapse_stage_touch_ms_count 1, a bridge-only counter). Leaving a resolvable question open while publishing a blocker-severity finding that the same class of probe would have refuted is an inconsistent evidence standard within one report.

### Open questions

- Is the in-Houdini hwebserver `/mcp` HTTP endpoint actually registered in the live process (pid 32804)? Static analysis says nothing imports `synapse.mcp.server`, but I did not probe HTTP :9999/mcp to confirm — that would have been a stateful POST. UNKNOWN.
- What share of live traffic, if any, goes through the panel path (panel/tool_executor.py:650 -> execute_through_bridge -> bridge.execute)? That is the only route still reaching LosslessExecutionBridge in production. If it is zero in practice, the bridge is instrumentation-only and §1 should say so.
- Exact count of genuinely-unwrapped mutating handlers after excluding delegating wrappers (solaris_*) and genuinely non-scene commands. My script said 45 of 91; hand-checking shows the real number is lower but I did not resolve it precisely.
- Was the checks.py:1922 regex going blind on mcp/tools.py noticed, or did it drift silently when farm-control admission was inserted? If unnoticed, the same anchored-regex pattern is likely blind elsewhere in harness/verify/checks.py.
- Is there a decision on record to retire `LosslessExecutionBridge.execute_async` and the FastMCP framing, or is it being held for a future transport? Six weeks of history show no work on it either way.

## memory-substrate — MIXED

> The substrate was genuinely repaired — three surfaces now agree to within 4 records, the poison producer is guarded and tested, and the ungated prune is disarmed — but recall itself is worse than broken: the wiring fix turned a dead memory tier into one that answers a karma-render question with a superseded Solaris recipe at 0.7 confidence, and a pure-stopword query scores 0.90.

**Trajectory.** Strongly improving on durability, detection and wiring; dead flat on retrieval quality. 29 commits touched python/synapse/memory/ in the last six weeks (`git log --oneline --since=2026-08-07 -- python/synapse/memory/ | wc -l` -> 29), covering the W3 wave (e8b691de W3-STORE typed cortex, a7112bd1 W3-VEC vector recall, a82492a3 W3-KIND, 0c87a835 W3-MIGRATE, 1b8d1e80 W3-HARDEN), then dd66b089/4f252ba5 (BP2 store-truth + health wiring), then ae34ed96 (2026-09-17, the incident fix: 24 files, +5936/-48, closing the producer, the prune, detection, the B4 wiring and 30 new tests). Against that, the keyword scorer that decides every returned result has not been touched since 2026-05-31: `git blame -L 143,148 -s python/synapse/memory/moneta_store.py` -> all six lines are 84fcd0710 ("feat(memory): Moneta backend - Miles 3-8", #14). Three and a half months and six waves of memory work went past the one function that determines whether a memory is returned.

**The refuter on where this report overstates itself.** Yes, in three specific places — though the core engineering judgment survives.

1. The headline's tense outruns its evidence. "The wiring fix turned a dead memory tier into one that answers a karma-render question with a superseded Solaris recipe at 0.7 confidence" is written as an observation of live behaviour. It is not. The code path is genuinely live (handlers.py:1777 `TieredRouter(config=config, memory=owner)`; knowledge.py:818 `search(query=..., limit=3)`), and their simulation on a store copy is methodologically correct — I reproduced every number. But I attributed all 54 "Vector recall:" log lines and found only TWO are production, both on 2026-09-16, both BEFORE ae34ed96 landed. Zero production recalls since the fix, zero today. The honest headline is "will answer", not "answers". They flagged this correctly in openQuestion #1 and then wrote the finding as if it were resolved.

2. Finding #5's "the disarm is observably firing in production" is flatly false and is the report's one real integrity failure. All 11 "Consolidation is due (1001 entities)" lines resolve to pytest-of-User temp stores running test_automatic_consolidation_prune_does_not_run_by_default against a _FakeECS with n=1001. They cited unit-test output as production evidence — in a report whose own finding #14 warns that pytest pollutes this exact log channel. The code guards are real; the production observation is not. And guard 2 (_mirror_gap_blocking_prune) has never executed anywhere: 0 "REFUSED" lines and no test references it.

3. "Three surfaces now agree to within 4 records" is true for ONE store and is presented as a property of the substrate. A second live store on this machine has a completely empty primary with 39 records stranded in the mirror (see "missed"). Scoped to untitled, the claim is exact and I confirmed it.

The status "mixed" is right, and "worse than broken" for retrieval is defensible — I reproduced the 0.90 stopword score, the 0.70 karma-on-Solaris result and the rank-1153-of-1155 collapse of the best semantic match. If anything the retrieval half is UNDERSTATED: 11 of 1155 rows are actual knowledge, not the 46% telemetry figure they reported. The durability half is slightly overstated by being scoped to one store and by one piece of misattributed evidence.

### Findings

#### [BLOCKER · load-bearing] Recall returns confidently wrong answers. The keyword scorer that ranks every result uses substring matching over unfiltered query words (`1 for w in words if w in content_lower or w in summary_lower`), so a query of pure stopwords outscores a real one. Measured on a read-only copy of the live 1151-row store: `do I a to in on it up at` -> 0.90 against the Solaris v3 decision; `how do I set up a karma render` -> 0.70 against the same three Solaris decisions at ranks 1-3; `what is the best recipe for banana bread` -> 0.40. `_match_memory` admits anything >= 0.4 (knowledge.py:839) and the tier floor is DENSE_MATCH_FLOOR = 0.5 (knowledge.py:35,586), so 0.6+ becomes a chat answer at `confidence=min(0.8, score)` (knowledge.py:855). This path is newly LIVE because ae34ed96 fixed the wiring.

**Evidence.** python/synapse/memory/moneta_store.py:143-148 (scorer), :154 (`if score > 0`); knowledge.py:35,586,839,855. Ran scratchpad/nullctl.py against a temp copy of snapshot.json: QUERY 'do I a to in on it up at' -> 0.90 DECISION ['9 word matches'] '**Decision:** "Create a Solaris Network" now = v3 recipe'; QUERY 'zzzqqqxx' -> 0 hits. Same scorer duplicated at sqlite_store.py:633 and store.py:958.

**For the artist.** He asks about a Karma render and the memory tier hands the model a Solaris network recipe as high-confidence project context. Wrong context is more expensive than no context, because it is acted on.

#### [BLOCKER · load-bearing] The decision that supersedes what recall returns is absent from the primary. `mem_e2e9749cb3c3` (2026-09-06) binds "Create a Solaris Network" AND "Basic Studio Lighting Setup" to the v4 recipe. It exists only in the JSONL mirror, and the mirror has ZERO read sites at runtime. So `search("Create a Solaris Network")` returns `mem_9e0826890122` ("now = v3 recipe") at score 1.0 — the superseded answer, served as the top hit with no signal that it was overridden.

**Evidence.** scratchpad/measure3.py: only-in-JSONL = 4, ['mem_1ed48dbc06ea','mem_3f27b6d52222','mem_cb73dd2c8061','mem_e2e9749cb3c3']; mem_e2e9749cb3c3 in Moneta: False / in JSONL: True. scratchpad/probe_index.py: st.search('Create a Solaris Network') -> rank 1 mem_9e0826890122 score 1.0, content '**Decision:** "Create a Solaris Network" now = v3 recipe'. Named as still-open in ae34ed96's own commit body ("Still open, named so it is not forgotten: four records exist only in the mirror").

**For the artist.** He types the exact trigger phrase he wrote the v4 decision for and gets v3. The correction he recorded is on disk, in a file nothing reads.

#### [BLOCKER · load-bearing] A second real project store is still dead from the same 2026-09-15 defect and nobody noticed. `D:\MODEL_COLLECTION\Astra_Saucer_Sept2026\...\.synapse\memory.jsonl` carries `mem_043ce93ca4e5` twice (lines 5 and 6, both created_at 2026-09-15T15:24:47Z) and has not been written since Sep 15 11:40. Its Moneta primary carries the same duplicate (12 rows / 11 distinct), so `primary_repair.py` was not run on it either. It degraded three more times TODAY.

**Evidence.** Decrypted read: 'Astra lines: 12 unreadable: 0 distinct: 11 dup ids: {mem_043ce93ca4e5: 2}'. `ls -la` shows memory.jsonl mtime Sep 15 11:40 next to memory.jsonl.degraded-load-{1789746695,1789749245,1789752304} (= 2026-09-18 11:51/12:34/13:25). synapse.log:15730,15740,15750 on 2026-09-18 13:25:04 — 'DEGRADED LOAD: 1 incomplete source record/read(s): line 6: conflicting duplicate memory identity'. Log census: `grep DEGRADED LOAD | sed | uniq -c` -> untitled 11, Astra 5, Alien_Spaceship_Saucer 2.

**For the artist.** That project has recorded zero memory for three days and is still silently refusing every write. The repair was scoped per-store; mirror_repair.py exists and was simply never pointed at this one.

#### [BLOCKER · load-bearing] Retrieval is dominated by the agent's own telemetry, not the artist's records. 533 of 1151 rows carry protected_floor 0.9; 530 of those are `feedback` rows whose content begins `{"attempt":{"context_sha256"` — RSI handler receipts. Only 3 protected rows are decisions. Ranking is `cos_sim * utility` with a 6h half-life, so of 612 unprotected rows only 57 (all <24h old) still carry utility >= 0.1; 411 sit below 1e-6, median 6.3e-8.

**Evidence.** Snapshot scan: protected rows 533, by memory_type {decision: 3, feedback: 530}, by source {ai: 3, auto: 530}; feedback content head Counter shows 529x '{"attempt":{"context_sha256"'. Moneta api.py:413 `ranked.append((cos_sim * memory.utility, memory))`; decay.py:31 DEFAULT_HALF_LIFE_SECONDS = 6*60*60. Age-vs-utility scan: unprotected {(<24h, u>=0.1): 57, (<7d, u<1e-3): 107, (>=7d, u<1e-3): 411}; max 1.0, p50 6.276e-08, min 5.479e-20.

**For the artist.** A note he wrote last week is ~7 orders of magnitude below a badly-matching loop receipt. The store remembers what the agent did, not what he decided.

#### [WATCH · load-bearing] Detection landed on three surfaces but does not cover the PRIMARY store. `MonetaBackedStore` — the substrate that actually serves — defines no `health()`, `is_degraded` or `degraded_reason`. write_plane reaches the contract only through `_HEALTH_CONTRACT_MEMBERS = ('_jsonl_net','primary','shadow')`, i.e. the MIRROR. An unanswered candidate goes into `silent`, and `silent` is discarded whenever any other candidate answered healthy, so the function returns status 'ok'. The exact inverse of the incident (broken mirror, healthy primary) is detected; a broken primary is not.

**Evidence.** AST scan of moneta_store.py: 'MonetaBackedStore -> NONE' for health/is_degraded/degraded_reason. write_plane.py:353 (_HEALTH_CONTRACT_MEMBERS), :498-503 (unanswered -> silent, `continue`), :528-536 (`if not healthy:` -> unknown, else -> {'status':'ok'}). No divergence fact was added anywhere: `grep -rn 'divergence|only_in_moneta|mirror_gap' write_plane.py doctor.py health_strip.py` -> no hits, despite VERDICT B6 recommending it as the second fact.

**For the artist.** The panel dot and synapse_health can still read green while the primary is sick. It would only catch the failure it has already seen.

#### [WATCH · load-bearing] The vector index is fully populated and genuinely read — the prior 'write-only' claim is re-refuted — but its output is then discarded. On a read-only copy: ecs.n 1151, len(vector_index) 1151, durability on, and `_handle.query(embedding, limit=60)` returns 60 candidates. Those 60 are then re-ranked by the substring scorer and anything with no literal substring overlap is dropped at `if score > 0`. The vector stage can therefore only REMOVE keyword-reachable memories; it can never add a semantic one.

**Evidence.** scratchpad/probe_index.py against a temp copy: 'ecs.n: 1151 / len(vector_index): 1151 / h.query(limit=60) returned: 60'. moneta_store.py:1135-1147 (query then score_memories), :154 (`if score > 0`), :1155-1157 (exception path scores ALL memories — strictly better recall than the success path). 54 'Vector recall:' INFO lines in the production log; the 1- and 4-candidate lines are small sibling stores, not this one.

**For the artist.** A 384-dim embedder loads on every store init and buys nothing. Paraphrases he does not word-match are unreachable.

#### [WATCH · load-bearing] The repaired record that recall most needs is also the one it ranks last. `mem_596cf755cfbf` — one of the five ids primary_repair deduped, keeping the metadata-rich twin with 4 tags — is the single best pure-cosine match for both 'Basic Studio Lighting Setup' (cos 0.611) and 'how do I set up a karma render' (cos 0.721). Under `cos*utility` it ranks 1142 of 1145. The repair saved a record that retrieval cannot reach.

**Evidence.** scratchpad/rank.py, simulating Moneta's exact ranking over the real semantic_vectors: 'best pure-cosine: mem_596cf755cfbf note cos=0.721 util=5.48e-20 -> rank 1142 of 1145'. Same script: the three protected decisions occupy ranks 1-3 for ALL five test queries regardless of topic.

**For the artist.** Deduping preserved his tags. Ranking then made them invisible. Both halves are true at once.

#### [WATCH · load-bearing] Tier 3 — the deep planning model — is memory-blind, while Tier 2 is not. `_try_tier2` calls `enrich_context(message, tier1_hint, memory=self._memory)`; `_try_tier3` calls `enrich_context(message, tier1_hint)` with no memory argument, so no `<memory>` block reaches the more expensive model.

**Evidence.** python/synapse/routing/router.py:679-683 (tier 2, passes memory=self._memory) vs :887-890 (tier 3, does not). enrich_context signature at context_enrichment.py:37-42; the memory block is built only `if memory is not None` (:80-89).

**For the artist.** The harder the question, the less project memory the model gets. Exactly backwards.

#### [WATCH] Three silent-failure sites survive on the recall path, in contrast to the one that was fixed loudly. `_match_memory` was correctly upgraded to a `logger.warning` naming the exception type and call shape (knowledge.py:818-831). But `enrich_context` still swallows a failed memory search at `logger.debug` — invisible at production INFO — and `_augment_with_knowledge` has a bare `except Exception: pass`. `_memory_binding_error`, which records a failed save-transition, is written at two sites and read at ZERO.

**Evidence.** context_enrichment.py:92-93 `except Exception: logger.debug('Memory search failed during context enrichment')`. handlers_memory.py:165-166 `except Exception: pass`. `grep -rn '_memory_binding_error' python/` -> exactly two hits, memory_lifecycle.py:117 (write) and :387 (reset to None). `grep -c 'Memory search failed' ~/.synapse/logs/synapse.log` -> 0.

**For the artist.** If enrichment or knowledge augmentation breaks, the answer just gets quietly thinner. Nothing tells him.

#### [WATCH · load-bearing] The save-transition window is real and closing. `_MAX_RECORDS = 2000` gates migration; the live store is at 1145 (57.2%). The byte cap is not the binding constraint (1.39 MB of 8 MB, 17.3%). Measured daily writes over the last ten days range 33-242 with a median near 110, so roughly 8 days of headroom. The duplicate-identity blocker at :174-175 is cleared for this store (0 duplicates) but NOT for Astra.

**Evidence.** python/synapse/host/memory_lifecycle.py:25-26 (caps), :171-172 (enforcement), :174-175 (duplicate check). Measured: 'records: 1145 / 2000 cap = 57.2% | payload bytes: 1454326 (1.39 MB) / 8MB cap = 17.3%'. Per-day counts from the snapshot: 2026-09-08..09-17 = 111,149,97,120,242,84,33,167,92.

**For the artist.** When he finally saves this untitled scene, memory either follows or it does not — and past the cap it silently does not, logged at ERROR into an attribute nothing reads.

#### [WATCH] The store is polluted by its own instrumentation, and the pollution is protected while the artist's notes are not. Every MCP client connection deposits a `note` memory and every tool call deposits an `action` memory: seven rows landed on 2026-09-18 alone, five of them 'AI session started (client: client_4.0.0_0000N)', written by this audit's sibling agents. Meanwhile pytest writes real-looking `DEGRADED LOAD` ERROR lines into the production log from ~40 tmp test stores, making the one log channel that would have caught the outage unreadable by eye.

**Evidence.** Snapshot newest rows: mem_7c5e3a85de4a / mem_1ef24c9c0fe5 / mem_a8a1d0529eb0 / mem_aaeef45ae7fe all 'AI session started (client: ...)' at 2026-09-18T17:52-17:53; mem_744960d45611 'Executed: memory_status'. synapse.log:15781-15791 shows 11 'Client connected' lines in 70 seconds. The DEGRADED LOAD path census lists ~40 distinct C:\...\pytest-of-User\pytest-NNNN\test_*\memory.jsonl entries alongside the three real stores.

**For the artist.** His signal-to-noise ratio degrades every time anything connects, and the 8-day record cap fills faster than his own writing rate implies.

#### [WATCH] Two smaller defects the incident work did not reach. (1) Moneta's real-USD sublayer path fails on every init with 'A layer already exists with identifier .../.moneta/usd/cortex_root.usda' and silently retries without sublayers, which is why both files under `.moneta/usd/` are 11 bytes; SYNAPSE's own `.moneta/cortex_root.usda` (1.7 MB, 1151 full-payload prims) is a separate and healthy surface. (2) `_degraded_load` is still assigned only inside `if degraded_reason:`, so the VERDICT's one-character fix (`= bool(degraded_reason)`) was not applied — a store object that degraded once cannot clear the flag by re-loading a repaired file.

**Evidence.** synapse.log:15734 2026-09-18 13:25:04 'Moneta init with use_real_usd=True failed (ErrorException: ... SdfLayer::_CreateNew ... A layer already exists with identifier ...); retrying without USD sublayers'. `ls .moneta/usd/` -> cortex_protected.usda 11 bytes, cortex_root.usda 11 bytes. store.py:450-453 (`if degraded_reason: self._degraded_load = True`).

**For the artist.** None today — the third copy exists via a different writer. The uncleared flag means recovery from a degrade still requires a process restart, not a repair-and-reload.

#### [INFO · load-bearing] The divergence the VERDICT measured is CLOSED. Three surfaces now agree to within exactly 4 records, all named. One-instant census of the live untitled store: Moneta snapshot 1151 ids, JSONL mirror 1155 ids, cortex_root.usda 1151 prims all carrying a non-empty payload. only-in-Moneta = 0 (was 253), only-in-JSONL = 4 (unchanged), in-Moneta-not-cortex = 0. Both repair tools demonstrably ran, in the correct order: primary dedupe at 2026-09-17T17:49:39, backfill at 17:51:45.

**Evidence.** scratchpad three-surface census script output: 'Moneta snapshot ids: 1151 / JSONL mirror ids: 1155 / cortex_root.usda ids: 1151 (payload non-empty: 1151, empty: 0) / only Moneta: 0  only JSONL: 4'. Backup filenames prove the runs: snapshot.json.pre-primary-repair-1789681779 and memory.jsonl.pre-backfill-1789681905; naming pinned at primary_repair.py:745 and backfill_from_moneta.py:470. Snapshot carries 0 duplicate mem ids and 0 duplicate entity_ids across 1151 rows.

**For the artist.** Every record he has written since 2026-09-01 now exists in three independent copies. A single-file loss is survivable again.

#### [INFO · load-bearing] The ungated automatic prune is genuinely disarmed, with two independent guards, and the disarm is observably firing in production. `add()` still counts to 100, but now calls `_maybe_auto_consolidate()`, which refuses unless (1) `$SYNAPSE_AUTO_CONSOLIDATE` is explicitly truthy — absent/empty/unparseable all read OFF — and (2) every live record is provably mirrored. The env var is set nowhere outside documentation.

### What the surveyor did not look at inside its own dimension

- A live store whose PRIMARY IS EMPTY — bigger than the 4-record gap they led with. D:\HOUDINI_PROJECTS_2025\Houdini21_Alien_Spaceship_Saucer\.synapse: its mirror was repaired on 2026-09-17 (memory.jsonl.pre-mirror-repair-1789682248 = 17:57:28) and now holds 39 clean, distinct records — but .moneta/snapshot.json is literally {"snapshot_version": 1, "snapshot_created_at": 1789580732.5, "rows": []} (78 bytes) and .moneta/cortex_root.usda is 121 bytes. only-in-mirror = 39, only-in-primary = 0. Recall reads the primary, so that project's entire memory is unreachable. It is the exact inverse of the untitled outage, ten times the size of the 4-record gap they called a blocker, and it sits inside their dimension untouched.
- They never re-measured the VERDICT's 15-row ledger, which the brief explicitly asked for. I did: CLOSED — mirror parses/accepts (1159/1159, 0 unreadable), dual-write resumed (only-in-Moneta 0), 5 primary dups removed (0 dup mem ids, 0 dup entity_ids), 253 backfilled, producer fixed, auto-prune disarmed, test coverage for the cause (both files now hit `conflicting duplicate memory identity`), vector-index-write-only still REFUTED, Storage Sense still REFUTED. STILL OPEN — 4 mirror-only records, detection of a degraded PRIMARY, retrieval returns the artist's memory, INTENT §6 provenance (0 files under the live .synapse/provenance). NEWLY OPEN — the two out-of-lane rows (second degraded store, machine-wide census), which are now answered below and produced the Alien finding.
- The 253 'JSONL dual-write failed (isolated): Refusing to write: the store loaded in DEGRADED mode' WARNING lines in the production log — the precise mechanism that produced the 253 Moneta-only rows, with a clean window from 2026-09-15 17:14:24 to 2026-09-17 15:53:57 and nothing after. It is the single best piece of evidence that the outage is closed and that the mirror gap had one cause, and it appears nowhere in their report.
- The machine-wide census they listed as out-of-lane and never run. I ran it (2,569 candidate .synapse dirs under C:\...\houdini_temp, D:\ and Documents, test paths excluded). Result: Astra\newProject\scripts is the ONLY live project store carrying a duplicate identity. Everything else with content is clean — LinkedinGraphic 11/11, SYNAPSE_TESTS newProject 8/8 and scripts 8/8, Astra newProject 3/3, flow_trail 28/28, nscr_COPS 7/7. A single archived backup tree (D:\Backups\SYNAPSE\tree\20260830_082425) shows 19 lines / 7 distinct with six ids triplicated — historical, not live. That bounds the blast radius to exactly two stores needing action.
- Moneta's own answer to their openQuestion #3 (attended_count = 0 everywhere), which is sitting in a docstring: api.py:421-428 — signal_attention appends to the attention log and 'the ECS update happens when the sleep-pass reducer drains the log.' No sleep pass has run on the live store, so attended_count = 0 is expected behaviour, not a dropped signal. Their framing ('either the ECS reduce only lands on a sleep pass, or signals are being dropped') had the right disjunction and never opened the file that settles it. The consequence they drew — attended_count >= 3 is unreachable, so nothing can earn protection through use — stands.
- That Moneta's 'vector pre-filter' is not a pre-filter. api.py:406 `over_fetch_k = max(len(self.vector_index), 1)` fetches EVERY row, ranks by cos*utility, then truncates to the caller's limit. So utility gates the candidate set before the keyword scorer ever runs. I confirmed the consequence they missed: mem_596cf755cfbf — their own finding #10 — is not in h.query(limit=60) at all. Their #8 and #10 are one mechanism, not two, and stating it that way makes the fix obvious (utility must leave the candidate stage, not just the rerank).
- The artist-facing recall tool. They traced KnowledgeIndex._match_memory and MonetaBackedStore.search but never opened synapse_recall -> handlers_memory.py:190-202 -> tracker.py:627 handle_memory_recall, which defaults to DECISION scope only. That is the one recall path with production log evidence (the two limit=20 lines on 2026-09-16), and its default scope means the artist's recall tool can only ever return the 6 decisions in a 1155-row store — three of which are the superseded Solaris ones.
- Repair is entirely manual and nothing detects the need for it. `grep -rn 'primary_repair|backfill_from_moneta' python/ scripts/` outside the modules themselves returns ZERO hits; both are argparse __main__ CLIs (primary_repair.py:954). Combined with their own finding #7 (no health() on the primary, no divergence fact in write_plane/doctor/health_strip), nothing on this machine would ever tell anyone that Astra or Alien needs repairing. That is the actual open loop behind their #3.
- Their own openQuestion #5 is answerable from the tests they already ran. tests/test_store_incident_2026_09_17.py:495 and :527 pin the recall path, and both query the exact stored phrase ('review link posted before lunch'), so a whole-word + stopword-filtered scorer would still pass them. The _STOPWORDS set they point at already exists at knowledge.py:615-619 and is used by _tokenize at :626 for every OTHER strategy — the memory strategy is the only one that bypasses it. The fix is smaller than they implied and the pinned-test risk they feared is not in the two tests that matter.

### Open questions

- Has the chat cascade (`_handle_chat` -> TieredRouter.route) actually executed once since ae34ed96 wired memory into it? UNKNOWN: router.py logs only on failure (:336,:342,:756,:938,:973), there is no routing-log file under ~/.synapse, and 'Memory search failed' count is 0 — which proves no exception, not that the path ran. A single live chat turn would settle it, and would also be the acceptance test for finding #1.
- Were the 4 mirror-only records pruned out of the primary or never deposited? Still UNKNOWN on disk: all snapshot rows carry state=0 and consolidated_into=null, and no PruneAudit is persisted anywhere. The answer changes the fix — never-deposited means a write path skipped the primary, which would still be live.
- Why is `attended_count` 0 on all 1151 rows when `signal_attention` IS called (moneta_store.py:1168-1176) and durability IS on in a fresh open? Either the ECS reduce only lands on a sleep pass (which has never run), or signals are being dropped. This matters because attended_count >= 3 is the only escape from the prune predicate, so today no record can ever earn protection through use.
- How many other project stores on this machine carry the duplicate or have diverged? The log names three real ones, but the log only shows stores that were OPENED. A machine-wide census of `**/.synapse/memory.jsonl` + `.moneta/snapshot.json` was out of lane here and has never been run.
- Is the substring scorer safe to replace, or does something depend on its looseness? Ten test files reference memory search; none was read for this question. Tightening it to whole-word + stopword-filtered matching (KnowledgeIndex._tokenize already has _STOPWORDS at knowledge.py:615-619) would drop many currently-passing results to zero hits, which may be correct and may break pinned tests.

## test-ci-health — MIXED

> The stock-Python suite is large, genuinely green, and improving fast (9,461 passing on all four required CI legs, master failure rate down from 12/16 to 1/49 over six weeks) — but everything that touches the artist's actual runtime is outside that green: the Qt panel, the hython/vendored interpreter, and the .synapse contract gates are all hand-run, partly red, and in one case 34 days unmeasured.

**Trajectory.** Improving, and sharply, on the axis CI actually covers. Over the last six weeks of master runs (`gh run list --workflow=ci.yml --branch master --limit 200`, 200 runs spanning 2026-08-09 to 2026-09-17: 165 success / 35 failure), the weekly failure rate goes 12/16 red (Aug w2) -> 5/59 (Aug w3) -> 6/10 (Aug w5) -> 9/41 (Sep w1) -> 2/23 (Sep w2) -> 1/49 (Sep w3). The one red in the most recent week was 43ef30ca (v5.75.0), a version-conformance drift fixed forward within 40 minutes by 53e4f9cb. The CI infrastructure itself got materially better in that window: ci.yml's `fetch-depth: 0` plus the `git branch master origin/master` step retired two guards that were reporting an environment gap on every PR leg, and the scoped `SYNAPSE_MEMORY_BACKEND=moneta` step (ci.yml, B5 2026-09-05) closed a structural blind spot where a real data-safety bug (destination store mirroring deposits back into the source memory.jsonl) was red on any workstation and green in CI. Pass count grew from the 6,389 baseline (b2412b6b, 2026-08-15) to 9,461 on CI today. The dimension is flat-to-regressing on everything CI does not cover: the shipping/hython leg has not been measured at full scope since 2026-08-15 (659 commits), the 6 seat reds have persisted across at least v5.72.0, v5.73.0 and v5.74.0 (cut.py:44-51 records them being mis-counted across all three), and the last two releases (v5.75.0, v5.75.1) dropped the release-ritual artifacts entirely — no cut.py, no suite receipt, no seat receipt, no install qualification.

**The refuter on where this report overstates itself.** Partly, but less than I expected -- this is a well-evidenced report and most of its hard numbers reproduce to the digit. The status "mixed" is right, and I would not soften it.

Three places where confidence outruns evidence:

1. **"genuinely green" in the headline rests on a gate that is not wired.** They write that master's failure rate is "down from 12/16 to 1/49," then treat the ratchet as the mechanism that would catch a coverage collapse. But CI never runs `harness/verify/checks.py` -- its only invoker is the human-armed `harness/run.ts:249,366`, and `suite_baseline.json:8`'s claim that the gate leg is "What CI enforces" is simply false. They quoted that file's honesty docstring for the *shipping* leg and swallowed its self-description for the *gate* leg. In a dimension whose brief says docs are claims to test, that is the exact miss.

2. **"The panel ... has almost no automated coverage" is stronger than their own log supports.** On the ubuntu-3.11 leg `tests/panel` collects 201 items and 117 of them pass. What has no coverage is Qt-rendered behaviour, not the panel module. Their own Qt split was also wrong in the other direction -- 210 outside `tests/panel`, not 169 -- so the finding is simultaneously overstated in wording and understated in magnitude.

3. **"It has now failed to prevent the thing it exists to prevent three times"** is the one sentence in the report I would call plainly wrong rather than imprecise. `scripts/release_ci_gate.py` was created 2026-09-06; v5.64.0 and v5.65.0 are both dated 2026-09-05. A script cannot fail to prevent what predates it. It has failed once, and it was run and passed at v5.75.1.

The weekly trajectory series is presented as measurement and is a bucketing artifact. Everything else -- the 104/13/91/28/22 contract cross-join, the 512/23/182 skip analysis, the 7,889-function tautology scan, the 6,193/119/5 shipping figures, the four red-tag resolutions, the seat receipt -- I reproduced independently and it held.

### Findings

#### [BLOCKER · load-bearing] The panel surface — the thing an artist actually touches — has almost no automated coverage. 296 of the 485 per-leg CI skips (61%) are "no real Qt/PySide". The only lane that runs real Qt is the hand-run seat suite, which runs `tests/panel` ONLY: 207 items, of which 6 are RED and tolerated by a named baseline. 169 Qt-gated tests per leg live OUTSIDE tests/panel (90 in tests/test_panel_rhythm_docking.py, 30 in test_panel_sweep_a.py, 17 in test_chat_panel.py) and are therefore run by NOTHING — they skip on CI and they are not in the seat selector.

**Evidence.** Skip clustering over the last green master run (id 35284295063, sha aa9624e5): `gh run view 35284295063 --log | grep -oE 'SKIPPED \[[0-9]+\] .*'` → 1,940 skips across 4 legs = 485/leg; Qt/PySide-reasoned = 832/4 = 208/leg by strict reason match, 296/leg including all PySide variants. Split by path: panel 39/leg, OUTSIDE panel 169/leg. Seat scope: harness/notes/release-5.74.0/cut.py:214 `run([sys.executable, ".synapse/hytest.py", "tests/panel", "-q", ...])`. Seat result: harness/notes/release-5.74.0/seat-5.74.0.txt last line `6 failed, 201 passed, 43 warnings in 72.68s`. Baseline reds named at cut.py:52-58 (SEAT_KNOWN).

**For the artist.** The panel is the product's face. A Qt regression in chat layout, the token rail, the doctor button or the failure trail ships unnoticed unless someone runs hython by hand at release time — and six panel behaviours are known-broken right now and gated only on "no NEW reds".

#### [BLOCKER · load-bearing] The shipping interpreter — Houdini's hython with the vendored wheel tree ACTIVE, i.e. what artists actually run — was last measured 2026-08-15 at 6,193 passed / 119 failed / 5 collection errors, and the ratchet explicitly does NOT re-run it. That is 34 days and 659 commits ago. The current state of the shipping leg is UNKNOWN.

**Evidence.** harness/verify/suite_baseline.json "shipping" block: `"passed": 6193, "failed": 119, "skipped": 191, "errors": 5`, `"measured": "2026-08-15"`, `"commit": "b2412b6b"`, and its own `_what`: "RECORDED-NOT-RERUN by check_suite_baseline (shape-enforced, never re-run per sprint) ... Honestly BAD and recorded as measured". Staleness: `git rev-list --count b2412b6b..HEAD` → 659. checks.py:2570-2572 confirms the skip: "The SHIPPING leg is recorded and shape-enforced but NOT re-run here".

**For the artist.** 119 known failures in the interpreter artists run, last confirmed a month ago. Whatever green a release cites is a green from a different Python than the one on the seat.

#### [BLOCKER · load-bearing] The suite ratchet floor is ~3,070 passes behind reality, so it cannot catch a regression that deletes up to three thousand tests. The enforced gate leg says passed=6389/failed=0; the suite now passes 9,461 on CI and 9,588 locally. The ratchet's pass condition is `failed_now <= failed_base and passed_now >= passed_base` — a run that dropped 3,000 tests would still satisfy it.

**Evidence.** harness/verify/suite_baseline.json gate leg: `"passed": 6389, "failed": 0, "promoted": "2026-08-15"`. Pass condition at harness/verify/checks.py:2566 `ok = failed_now <= failed_base and passed_now >= passed_base`. Current counts: last green CI run 35284295063 summary line `9461 passed, 485 skipped, 44 deselected, 4 xfailed, 130 subtests passed`; local Windows receipt harness/notes/release-5.74.0/suite-5.74.0.txt last line `9588 passed, 433 skipped, 5 xfailed, 625 warnings in 456.85s`.

**For the artist.** none (internal), but it is the mechanism meant to stop a silent coverage collapse from reaching a release.

#### [WATCH · load-bearing] There is no Windows CI leg, for a product that ships into Windows Houdini seats. The matrix is ubuntu-latest + macos-latest x Python 3.11 + 3.14, and master requires exactly those four contexts. The only Windows evidence is a hand-run local suite at release time.

**Evidence.** .github/workflows/ci.yml: `matrix: os: [ubuntu-latest, macos-latest]`, `python-version: ["3.11", "3.14"]`. `gh api repos/JosephOIbrahim/Synapse/branches/master/protection` → required contexts exactly `test (ubuntu-latest, 3.11)`, `test (ubuntu-latest, 3.14)`, `test (macos-latest, 3.11)`, `test (macos-latest, 3.14)`; `enforce_admins: false`. Windows-only tests exist and are skipped on CI: skip reason "Vendored binaries require a vendored ABI (cp311/cp313) + Windows ... these resolution tests are Windows-only by design" (2/leg), "nt-only invariant" (1/leg), "Selector-loop forcing only applies on Windows" (1/leg).

**For the artist.** A Windows-specific break in path handling, the vendor ABI gate or the selector loop reaches a seat with no automated check having had a chance to see it.

#### [WATCH] HEAD is one commit ahead of origin/master, unpushed, and has never been through CI. The last CI-verified sha on this branch is aa9624e5. Every count in this report that comes from CI describes aa9624e5, not b7592a9a.

**Evidence.** `git rev-parse HEAD` → b7592a9ad15af982a6446516ad649bc6c123bba9; `git rev-parse origin/master` → aa9624e57a63f54a28d36e5127c247b96976d693; `git rev-list --left-right --count origin/master...HEAD` → `0	1`. `gh run list --workflow=ci.yml --limit 60 --json headSha,conclusion --jq 'select(.headSha=="b7592a9a...")'` → empty. That commit touches harness/verify/hython_verbs.py and .synapse/contracts/hython-verbs.yaml (mtimes 2026-09-18 13:19).

**For the artist.** none today, but the HEAD an audit reads is not the HEAD any gate has checked.

#### [WATCH · load-bearing] The .synapse contract gates are not enforced anywhere automatic, and their passing flags are stale. 25 contracts hold 104 feature entries: 13 passing:true, 91 passing:false. 100 carry a `verify:` command, but only 28 point at a test file that actually ran in CI — and 22 features marked passing:false have a CI test file that is fully green, meaning the flag is a human ratification that was never flipped, not a measurement.

**Evidence.** Script over .synapse/contracts/*.yaml (counting `passing:\s*true|false` and cross-joining `verify:` targets against per-test verdicts extracted from CI run 35284295063's ubuntu-3.11 leg, 9,663 verdict lines): "contracts: 25 / TOTAL feature ids: 104 / passing:true=13 passing:false=91"; "verify targets a test file that RAN in CI: 28 | did not: 72"; "features marked passing:false whose CI test file is fully green: 22". No contract step in .github/workflows/ci.yml (`grep -n 'checks.py|harness/verify' .github/workflows/ci.yml` → only a comment at :70). Only one test reads the tree as data: tests/test_panel_rhythm_docking.py:20 `CONTRACT = ROOT / ".synapse/contracts/docking-minimums.yaml"` — and that file is itself 90-tests-Qt-skipped on CI.

**For the artist.** none directly; but a reader scanning the contracts for "what works" gets a 12.5%-green picture that the test results contradict in at least 22 places.

#### [WATCH · load-bearing] Three release tags sit on commits whose CI concluded failure — v5.64.0, v5.65.0, v5.75.0 — and the repo ships a script whose entire purpose is to prevent exactly that, wired to nothing. v5.75.0's red was three version-conformance tests (platform-independent), fixed forward by v5.75.1 the same evening.

**Evidence.** Per-tag check: `for t in v5.64.0 v5.65.0 v5.65.2 v5.75.0 v5.75.1 v5.74.0; do sha=$(git rev-list -n1 $t); gh run list --workflow=ci.yml --limit 200 --json headSha,conclusion ...; done` → v5.64.0 2865f009 failure, v5.65.0 4becb1cf failure, v5.75.0 43ef30ca failure, v5.65.2/v5.74.0/v5.75.1 success. Failing tests on run 35280263679: `tests/test_phase0c_doc1_toolcount.py::test_readme_latest_tag_matches_version_file`, `tests/test_phase0c_doc1_version_conformance.py::test_version_single_sourced_and_docs_conform`, `tests/test_reach_public_agreement.py::test_live_tree_banner_equals_version` → `3 failed, 9458 passed, 485 skipped, 44 deselected, 4 xfailed`. Gate script: scripts/release_ci_gate.py exists (4,018 bytes, 2026-09-06); `grep -rn release_ci_gate` outside its own file and harness/notes → zero call sites. Self-documented at docs/releases/v5.75.1.md:15-17.

**For the artist.** An artist downloading a tagged build can get one whose CI was red. v5.75.0 additionally shipped zero assets while the README download button still pointed at v5.74.0.

#### [WATCH · load-bearing] The two most recent releases have no suite receipt at all. Every release from 5.70.0 through 5.74.0 has a dated cut.py, suite-<v>.txt and seat-<v>.txt under harness/notes/. There is no release-5.75.0 or release-5.75.1 directory — only docs/releases/*.md — so v5.75.1, the current tag, has no recorded stock-suite run, no seat run, and no install qualification.

**Evidence.** `ls -d harness/notes/release-5.7*/` → 5.70.0, 5.70.1, 5.71.0, 5.72.0, 5.72.1, 5.73.0, 5.74.0 (no 5.75.x). `find . -name '*5.75*'` → only docs/releases/v5.75.0.md and docs/releases/v5.75.1.md. HEAD-1 commit aa9624e5 states it plainly: "installer-verification.json is NOT shipped, and v5.74.0 did ship it ... No such run was performed for this build ... it has not been install-qualified, and the notes now say so."

**For the artist.** The installer currently on the release page was built from hash-verified inputs but was never probed against a running Houdini.

#### [WATCH] The release gate itself is re-authored per release rather than being a checked-in tool, so gate quality is not cumulative. cut.py is copied into each release directory and substantially rewritten each time.

**Evidence.** `find . -name cut.py` (excluding worktrees) → five copies, one per release dir, none in scripts/. `git diff --stat --no-index harness/notes/release-5.73.0/cut.py harness/notes/release-5.74.0/cut.py` → `1 file changed, 384 insertions(+), 433 deletions(-)`. The 5.74.0 copy documents a gate-width bug it inherited: cut.py:44-51 — v5.73.0's composer listed five seat reds while its own log showed six, so `new = measured - known` "re-manufactured the sixth as a NEW regression ... All three published installer-verification.json assets say NEW_RED over it."

**For the artist.** none directly; it means a gate fixed in one cut can be absent from the next.

#### [WATCH] There is no coverage gate. pytest-cov is a declared dev dependency and coverage config exists, but CI never passes --cov and no fail_under threshold is configured anywhere.

**Evidence.** pyproject.toml:35 `"pytest-cov>=4.0"`; pyproject.toml:187 `[tool.coverage.run]`, :191 `[tool.coverage.report]` with only `exclude_lines` (`pragma: no cover`). `grep -n 'cov|fail_under' .github/workflows/ci.yml` → no match; the two pytest invocations at ci.yml are `python -m pytest tests/ -m "not needs_houdini" -v -rs --tb=short` and the scoped SYNAPSE_MEMORY_BACKEND=moneta step.

#### [WATCH · load-bearing] No Houdini-dependent test runs automatically anywhere. There is exactly one workflow file, it has no `schedule`, no `cron` and no `workflow_dispatch`, and nothing in it invokes hython. Every hython lane — the seat suite, the 5 hytest-backed contract verifies, the hython_verbs contract — is human-triggered.

**Evidence.** `find .github -type f` → .github/ISSUE_TEMPLATE/bug.yml, .github/repository-metadata.json, .github/workflows/ci.yml only. `grep -rn 'schedule|cron|workflow_dispatch' .github/` → no matches. `grep -rn 'hytest.py'` across the tree (excluding worktrees and harness/notes) → 5 contract `verify:` lines (apex-truth-reseed, docking-minimums x1, failure-trail x2, model-picker), the shim itself, and docs. Contract verify classification: "hython shim (manual, no CI): 5".

**For the artist.** Every claim about how SYNAPSE behaves inside Houdini rests on somebody remembering to run it.

#### [WATCH] 23 test files are 100% skipped on CI — 182 tests that produce no verdict on any required context. The largest are tests/test_panel_faces.py (38), tests/test_loop_hanish_worker.py (30, gated on a private optional package), tests/test_retina_t1.py (22, gated on opencv-python-headless in a RETINA worker venv), tests/test_inspect_live.py (10, needs a live transport).

**Evidence.** Per-test verdict extraction from run 35284295063 ubuntu-3.11 leg (9,663 verdict lines): "test files appearing in CI leg: 512 / files where EVERY collected test skipped on CI: 23 / tests inside those files: 182". Gate for the Hanish cluster: tests/test_loop_hanish_worker.py:39 `pytest.importorskip("hanish", reason="real optional Hanish package is not on the test path")`. Note 512 files appear vs 531 on disk (`find tests -name 'test_*.py' | wc -l`) — the 19-file gap is the needs_houdini deselection.

**For the artist.** RETINA (render receipt) and the live-inspect path have zero automated verdict.

#### [INFO] The tautology scan is clean — this suite is not padded with tests that cannot fail. Of 7,889 parsed test functions, 270 (3.4%) carry no assertion, and those are overwhelmingly deliberate `*_noop` "this must not raise" pins. Exactly one `assert True`, zero tests whose entire body is `pass`, zero asserts on a bare mock attribute, and only 2 try-blocks where an assert sits above a bare-`pass` handler — both documented race-tolerant, not swallowed failures.

**Evidence.** AST scan over tests/**/test_*.py: "test functions parsed: 7889 / with NO assert / raises / fail / assert_* call: 270"; "tests whose entire body is pass: 0"; "try-blocks containing an assert whose handler is bare pass: 2" → tests/test_host_layer.py:1611 (`except TurnCancelled: pass  # also acceptable`, with the race explained in the three comment lines above it) and tests/test_load.py:123 (a `try: import pytest` guard, and the module is skipped unless SYNAPSE_LOAD_TEST=1). `grep -rnE '^\s*assert True\s*$'` → 1 hit. Representative no-assert cluster: tests/test_agent_state.py:193-209, `test_create_task_noop` etc., under a fixture docstring pinning the pxr-absent graceful-no-op path.

#### [INFO] The needs_houdini CI filter is honestly built and self-tested — it cannot hide a failure. A module earns the marker only by already refusing to run without its runtime (AST-detected, module-level only), the deselection is printed in the terminal summary rather than dropped silently, and the invariant has its own test. 44 items were deselected on the last green run, all from modules that would have skipped anyway.

**Evidence.** tests/conftest.py:826-860 documents the invariant and the conservatism rules; tests/conftest.py:971-995 is the reporter that prints "needs_houdini deselected (NOT silently dropped)" with each runtime reason. tests/test_needs_houdini_marker.py exists (9,630 bytes). CI summary from run 35284295063: `44 deselected`. Deselected modules read from the run log include tests/test_e2e_tops.py, tests/test_h22_cops_solver_live.py, tests/solaris/test_live_wiring.py, tests/test_apex_wire_hython.py.

#### [INFO] The suite's xfail usage is disciplined, not a dumping ground. All 4-5 xfails in a CI run come from tests/test_memory_seam_defects.py, which is explicitly a strict-xfail tripwire file: deterministic, red at HEAD, XPASS when fixed. Three other files mention xfail only to forbid it.

**Evidence.** `grep -rn xfail tests/ --include='test_*.py'` → tests/test_memory_seam_defects.py:10 "``Group A`` — strict-xfail tripwires. Deterministic, fail at HEAD, and XPASS the ..." with 8 occurrences; tests/test_marshal_lint.py:55 "do not extend the allowlist to make it green, do not mark it xfail"; tests/solaris/test_live_wiring.py:27 "Do NOT xfail, skip or weaken them"; tests/test_hou_reimport_guard.py:153. CI summary: `4 xfailed`; local Windows: `5 xfailed`.

### Refuter verdicts

- **CORRECTED** — Panel has almost no automated coverage: 296 of 485 per-leg CI skips (61%) are Qt/PySide; seat runs tests/panel ONLY (207 items, 6 red, baseline-tolerated); 169 Qt-gated tests/leg live OUTSIDE tests/panel (90 in test_panel_rhythm_docking.py, 30 in test_panel_sweep_a.py, 17 in test_chat_panel.py) and run nowhere.
  - *Correction:* The claim is directionally right and understated in every number. Real Qt share is 299/485 = 61.6%. Real split is 89/leg inside tests/panel and 210/leg OUTSIDE it, not 39/169. The 41-test gap is mostly tests/test_panel_faces.py (38/leg) -- which their own finding #13 names as the single largest fully-skipped file, so their two findings contradict each other. Separately, 'almost no automated coverage' overstates: on the ubuntu-3.11 leg tests/panel collects 201 items and 117 of them PASS. The accurate statement is that the panel's Qt-RENDERED behaviour has no automated coverage, not the panel module.
  - *Evidence:* Downloaded `gh run view 35284295063 --log` (7.45 MB, 44,449 lines). Summing every `SKIPPED [n]` bracket: 1,940 over 4 legs = 485/leg exactly. Qt/PySide-reasoned (substring 'qt' or 'pyside' in the reason tail) = 1,196 = 299/leg, not 296. Splitting on the file path in the tail, normalising backslashes: INSIDE tests/panel 89/leg; OUTSIDE 210/leg; 0 unparsed. Their 39/169 split came from a regex requiring `path:line: reason`, which silently drops every module-level skip. Seat scope and result reproduce exactly: `grep -n 'hytest.py", "tests/panel"' harness/notes/release-5.74.0/cut.py` -> :214; `grep -n 'SEAT_KNOWN = ('` -> :52; seat-5.74.0.txt last line `6 failed, 201 passed, 43 warnings in 72.68s`; the six FAILED lines match the six SEAT_KNOWN names one-for-one.
- **CONFIRMED** — The shipping/hython leg was last measured 2026-08-15 at 6,193 passed / 119 failed / 5 collection errors, the ratchet does not re-run it, and that is 659 commits stale.
  - *Evidence:* harness/verify/suite_baseline.json `/shipping`: passed=6193, failed=119, skipped=191, errors=5, collection_errors=5, measured='2026-08-15', commit='b2412b6b'. Its own `_what` reads 'RECORDED-NOT-RERUN by check_suite_baseline (shape-enforced, never re-run per sprint) ... Honestly BAD and recorded as measured'. `git rev-list --count b2412b6b..HEAD` -> 659. harness/verify/checks.py:2567 'The SHIPPING leg is recorded and shape-enforced but NOT re-run here'. Every figure reproduced.
- **CORRECTED** — The suite ratchet floor is ~3,070 passes behind reality (base 6389/0 vs 9,461 CI / 9,588 local) and its pass condition `failed_now <= failed_base and passed_now >= passed_base` would tolerate dropping 3,000 tests.
  - *Correction:* The arithmetic is right; the word 'enforced' is a doc claim they copied rather than measured. suite_baseline.json:8 says the gate leg is 'What CI enforces.' That is false -- CI never runs check_suite_baseline, no hook runs it, and there is no scheduled workflow. So the ratchet is not merely 3,070 passes behind; it is not attached to any automatic trigger, which is strictly worse than what they reported. They repeated the file's self-description as fact in a dimension whose brief told them to test docs against code.
  - *Evidence:* harness/verify/checks.py:2566 is verbatim `ok = failed_now <= failed_base and passed_now >= passed_base`. Baseline `/gate`: passed=6389, failed=0, promoted='2026-08-15'. CI summary line, identical on all four legs of run 35284295063: `9461 passed, 485 skipped, 44 deselected, 4 xfailed, 130 subtests passed`. harness/notes/release-5.74.0/suite-5.74.0.txt last line `9588 passed, 433 skipped, 5 xfailed, 625 warnings in 456.85s`. Arithmetic and code all reproduce. BUT: `grep -nE 'checks\.py|harness/verify|suite_baseline|ratchet' .github/workflows/ci.yml` returns only three COMMENT lines (:47, :70, :71). The only invoker of checks.py is harness/run.ts (:249, :366) -- a human-armed harness. `.git/hooks` holds nothing but .sample files; the real hooks (core.hooksPath -> harness/githooks) contain no pytest invocation at all.
- **CONFIRMED** — No Windows CI leg. Matrix is ubuntu-latest + macos-latest x Python 3.11 + 3.14, and master requires exactly those four contexts.
  - *Evidence:* .github/workflows/ci.yml: `os: [ubuntu-latest, macos-latest]`, `python-version: ["3.11", "3.14"]`. `gh api repos/JosephOIbrahim/Synapse/branches/master/protection` -> required contexts exactly ['test (ubuntu-latest, 3.11)', 'test (ubuntu-latest, 3.14)', 'test (macos-latest, 3.11)', 'test (macos-latest, 3.14)'], strict=False, enforce_admins=False, required_pull_request_reviews=None. Windows-only skip reasons present in the run log as claimed.
- **CORRECTED** — The .synapse contract gates run nowhere automatic and their flags are stale: 25 contracts, 104 features, 13 passing:true / 91 passing:false, 100 carry verify:, 28 point at a CI-running test file, 22 passing:false features have a fully-green CI test file.
  - *Correction:* Two defects in a finding whose numbers are otherwise exact. (1) The verify: count is 102, not 100 -- `grep -rhoE '^\s*verify:' .synapse/contracts/*.yaml | wc -l` -> 102; four features carry a passing flag with no verify. (2) 'Fully green' is a FILE-level test that admits an abstention as a pass -- exactly the gate-width failure this repo has a memory entry about. docking-minimums.yaml cites tests/test_panel_rhythm_docking.py, which on CI is P=1 S=90; counting that as green is counting a 99%-skipped file. And 16 of the 22 verify lines name a specific `::nodeid` that the file-level check never confirmed ran. (3) 'Stale' mis-frames the design: panel-truth.yaml:4 states in the file itself 'Every feature passing:false -- the flips are Joe's word (per act)'. The flag is a ratification by construction, which their own openQuestion concedes but their finding text asserts past.
  - *Evidence:* Independently reproduced by splitting each .synapse/contracts/*.yaml on `- description:` boundaries and cross-joining verify: targets against 9,663 per-test verdict lines from the ubuntu-3.11 leg: 'feature entries with a passing flag: 104 | true: 13 | false: 91'; 'verify names a test file that RAN in CI: 28 | did not: 72'; 'passing:false whose CI test file is fully GREEN: 22'; 'passing:true whose CI file is NOT green: 0'. All six headline figures reproduce exactly. tests/test_panel_rhythm_docking.py:20 `CONTRACT = ROOT / ".synapse/contracts/docking-minimums.yaml"` confirmed. No contract step in ci.yml confirmed.
- **CORRECTED** — Three release tags sit on red CI (v5.64.0, v5.65.0, v5.75.0) and scripts/release_ci_gate.py, whose purpose is to prevent that, is wired to nothing; it has failed to prevent this three times.
  - *Correction:* The three red tags are real. 'It has now failed to prevent the thing it exists to prevent three times' is false: the script did not exist when v5.64.0 and v5.65.0 were cut (created 2026-09-06; both tags 2026-09-05). It has failed once, at v5.75.0, and it WAS run by hand and passed at v5.75.1. Their grep evidence is also wrong as stated -- 'zero call sites outside its own file and harness/notes' does not hold: docs/architecture/overview.md:230, docs/releases/v5.75.1.md:15 and :54, harness/cto/BACKLOG.json:13 (a closure predicate that literally invokes it), harness/finesse-20260906/artifacts/release-readiness.md:41. None is an executable caller, so the substance survives; the cited command does not reproduce.
  - *Evidence:* Per-tag resolution against `gh run list --workflow=ci.yml --limit 300`: v5.64.0 2865f009 -> failure:33978285381; v5.65.0 4becb1cf -> failure:33994249587; v5.75.0 43ef30ca -> failure:35280263679; v5.65.2, v5.74.0, v5.75.1 -> success. Three red tags CONFIRMED. `ls -l scripts/release_ci_gate.py` -> 4018 bytes, Sep 6. BUT `git log --diff-filter=A -- scripts/release_ci_gate.py` -> b1ec9a8d, dated 2026-09-06. Tag dates: v5.64.0 2026-09-05, v5.65.0 2026-09-05. And docs/releases/v5.75.1.md:54 reads '`scripts/release_ci_gate.py` was run against this tag before publication: tag v5.75.1 53e4f9cb / ci completed success / OK'.
- **CONFIRMED** — The two most recent releases have no suite receipt: no harness/notes/release-5.75.0 or -5.75.1 directory, so v5.75.1 has no stock-suite run, no seat run, no install qualification.

Corroborated in-tree by docs/releases/v5.75.1.md:20-23, which records v5.75.0 shipping zero assets while the README button still pointed at v5.74.0.
- **CONFIRMED** — HEAD is one commit ahead of origin/master, unpushed, never through CI; every CI-derived count describes aa9624e5.
  - *Evidence:* `git rev-parse HEAD` -> b7592a9ad15af982a6446516ad649bc6c123bba9; `git rev-parse origin/master` -> aa9624e57a63f54a28d36e5127c247b96976d693; `git rev-list --left-right --count origin/master...HEAD` -> `0	1`. `gh run list --workflow=ci.yml --limit 300 --json headSha,conclusion --jq '[.[] | select(.headSha=="b7592a9a...")] | length'` -> 0. Marked loadBearing:false by them; I would raise it, since it is the premise under which every other CI number in the report is read.
- **CORRECTED** — The release gate is re-authored per release: five cut.py copies, one per release dir, none in scripts/; 5.73->5.74 diff is 384 insertions / 433 deletions.
  - *Correction:* 'One per release dir' is wrong: there are SEVEN release dirs and only FIVE cut.py. 5.70.0 and 5.70.1 have none -- they shipped compose_verification.py and compose_assets.py instead. So the release gate has changed NAME AND SHAPE across seven releases, not merely been rewritten across five, which makes their non-cumulative point stronger than they stated. Also: given the gate is the only thing standing between a tag and a red release page, and it has already been shown to mis-count reds across three published assets, loadBearing:false understates it.
  - *Evidence:* `git diff --stat --no-index harness/notes/release-5.73.0/cut.py harness/notes/release-5.74.0/cut.py` -> `1 file changed, 384 insertions(+), 433 deletions(-)` -- exact. `find . -name cut.py -not -path './.git/*'` -> 5 in the main tree (5.71.0, 5.72.0, 5.72.1, 5.73.0, 5.74.0) plus 6 worktree copies. The inherited gate-width bug at cut.py:44-51 reads verbatim as quoted.

Spot-checked as a loadBearing:false finding; it holds exactly.
- **CONFIRMED** — Tautology scan clean: 7,889 test functions parsed, 270 (3.4%) with no assertion, 0 whose body is only pass, 1 `assert True`, 2 try-blocks with an assert above a bare-pass handler.

No-assert cluster example tests/test_agent_state.py:193 test_create_task_noop confirmed. Spot-checked as loadBearing:false; reproduces within +/-1 on the parse count.
- **CONFIRMED** — The needs_houdini CI filter is honestly built and self-tested; 44 items deselected, all from modules that would have skipped anyway.

Their line cite 971-995 is one line late on the start; immaterial.

My contract scan independently classified the verify: commands: 'hython shim (manual): 5' at apex-truth-reseed.yaml, docking-minimums.yaml, failure-trail.yaml (x2), model-picker.yaml -- the exact five they name.
- **CONFIRMED** — 23 test files are 100% skipped on CI (182 tests); largest are test_panel_faces.py (38), test_loop_hanish_worker.py (30), test_retina_t1.py (22), test_inspect_live.py (10); 512 files appear in CI vs 531 on disk.
