Top-8 list reproduces their four named files at exactly 38, 30, 22, 10. `find tests -name 'test_*.py' | wc -l` -> 531. Spot-checked as loadBearing:false; exact.
- **CORRECTED** — xfail usage is disciplined: all 4-5 xfails come from tests/test_memory_seam_defects.py, a strict-xfail tripwire file; three other files mention xfail only to forbid it.
  - *Correction:* Substance holds -- every XFAIL on the green run is from the tripwire file. But 'three other files mention xfail only to forbid it' should be five, and the two they missed both strengthen the thesis: test_solaris_v3_acceptance_runner.py:107 is a test asserting that xfail and xpass are not product passes.
  - *Evidence:* `grep 'test (ubuntu-latest, 3.11)' run.log | grep -oE 'tests/[^ ]+ XFAIL'` -> exactly 4, all tests/test_memory_seam_defects.py (test_p1_recall_receipt..., two test_p2_bare_string..., test_p7_repeated_settlement...). tests/test_memory_seam_defects.py:10 docstring and :83/:132/:166/:203/:400 markers confirmed. BUT `grep -rln xfail tests/ --include='test_*.py'` -> SIX files, not four: they missed tests/test_pkg_bootstrap_invariant.py:374 and tests/test_solaris_v3_acceptance_runner.py (3 hits, including :107 `def test_xfail_and_xpass_are_not_product_passes`).
- **CORRECTED** — Trajectory: 200 master runs 2026-08-09..2026-09-17, 165 success / 35 failure; weekly failure rate 12/16 -> 5/59 -> 6/10 -> 9/41 -> 2/23 -> 1/49.
  - *Correction:* The aggregate and the direction are real; the published weekly series is not reproducible. Under ISO weeks the shape is 0, 13, 4, 14, 3, 1 -- non-monotone, with a 14-red week in early September -- not the smooth 12 -> 5 -> 6 -> 9 -> 2 -> 1 descent they printed. Both bucketings sum to the same 35 reds, so the difference is week boundaries, not data. 'Improving, and sharply' is defensible on the last two weeks (3/16 then 1/61); the clean monotone descent is an artifact of their chosen boundaries and should not be quoted as a series.
  - *Evidence:* `gh run list --workflow=ci.yml --branch master --limit 200 --json conclusion --jq '[.[].conclusion] | group_by(.) | map({k:.[0],n:length})'` -> [{failure:35},{success:165}]; date range min..max -> 2026-08-09T21:44:46Z .. 2026-09-17T22:54:00Z. Aggregate reproduces EXACTLY. Bucketing the same 200 rows by ISO week gives: W32 0/1, W33 13/42, W34 4/34, W36 14/46, W37 3/16, W38 1/61.

### What the surveyor did not look at inside its own dimension

- Four test trees outside `tests/` that CI never touches. `ci.yml` runs `python -m pytest tests/` only, and `grep -nE 'installer|agent/|retina|bastion' .github/workflows/ci.yml` -> NONE. On disk: agent/tests (8 files, ~129 test functions), installer/tests (2 files, ~37), .synapse/tests (3 files, ~16), harness/bastion/tests (1 file, ~10) -- roughly 192 test functions. installer/tests at least runs at release time (harness/notes/release-5.74.0/cut.py:219); agent/tests appears to run nowhere at all. The report's denominator -- '531 test files', '9,461 passing' -- silently excludes all of it, which is the same class of omission the report criticises elsewhere.
- The repo's only automatic gates, never mentioned. `git config core.hooksPath` -> C:/Users/User/SYNAPSE/harness/githooks, with tracked `pre-commit` and `pre-push`. A report asserting 'nothing runs automatically' should have named them, if only to say they run no tests (they do not -- `grep -nE 'pytest|checks.py' harness/githooks/pre-push` is empty). harness/bastion/FINDINGS_INDEX.md:182 already flags this as a code-exec-on-git-op surface no dimension scout modelled.
- Whether the suite ratchet is invoked at all. They characterised its floor as 3,070 behind but never asked who pulls the lever. Nobody does automatically: no CI step, no hook, no schedule, only harness/run.ts.
- The shipping baseline's interpreter is a different Houdini build. suite_baseline.json `/shipping/interpreter` = 'hython 3.13.10 (Houdini 22.0.368/bin/hython.exe)'. The seat suite pins 22.0.400 (every seat-*.txt tail states it) and the current hython lane is 22.0.417. So the 6,193/119/5 figure is stale by BUILD as well as by 34 days -- a much harder-to-close gap than re-running it.
- The panel's actual CI pass count. 117 of 201 `tests/panel` items PASS on the ubuntu-3.11 leg. Never counted, which is why finding #1's wording overshoots while its numbers undershoot.
- A second collection delta they did not notice. They flagged the 127-test stock/CI delta as an open question, but the seat run collects 207 items in `tests/panel` where CI collects 201 -- six items that exist only under real Qt. Same failure class, smaller and more tractable, and it sits inside the one lane they call load-bearing.
- Runtime and flakiness as health axes. CI legs run 176-297s, the local Windows suite 456.85s, the seat 72.68s. No look at re-run counts, no check for tests that pass on retry, no check whether any of the 35 master reds were the same test twice.
- Whether SEAT_KNOWN still matches reality. They cite the named baseline at cut.py:52-58 but never diffed it against the log. I did: the six FAILED lines in seat-5.74.0.txt match the six SEAT_KNOWN entries exactly, so the identity gate would have held at v5.74.0. That is a small green they left on the table in an otherwise-red finding.
- The 4 no-verify contract features. 104 features carry a passing flag, 100 carry a verify command -- four features are ratifiable with no stated way to check them at all. Worth naming in a finding about gate honesty.

### Open questions

- What is the shipping/hython leg's real state today? The only figure is 2026-08-15's 6,193 passed / 119 failed / 5 collection errors at b2412b6b, 659 commits back. Whether those 119 shrank, grew, or changed identity is UNKNOWN — and it is the interpreter artists run.
- Have the 169 per-leg Qt-gated tests that live outside tests/panel (90 of them in tests/test_panel_rhythm_docking.py alone) ever been run green anywhere? They skip on CI, they skip on the Windows stock run, and the seat selector is `tests/panel` only. I found no receipt covering them. UNKNOWN.
- What accounts for the 127-test delta between the local Windows run (9,588 passed / 433 skipped, v5.74.0) and CI (9,461 passed / 485 skipped)? 52 of it is the skip difference; the rest is presumably Windows-only tests plus the 44 deselected, but I did not reconcile it test-by-test. Worth confirming that none of the delta is a test that silently stopped collecting on one platform.
- Are the 22 contract features marked passing:false whose CI test file is fully green actually done and unratified, or does the feature need more than that one test file to be true? The flag is a human ratification by design, so this may be a backlog of unflipped ratifications rather than a defect — but nothing in the tree distinguishes the two.
- Should scripts/release_ci_gate.py be wired into the tag path rather than named in a runbook? It has now failed to prevent the thing it exists to prevent three times (v5.64.0, v5.65.0, v5.75.0). That is a decision, not a finding.
- Is there a reason the CI matrix has no Windows leg? Adding `windows-latest` would cost roughly one more 4-minute job per push and would be the first automated check on the platform the product ships to. I found no ADR or comment explaining the omission.

## harness-inventory — MIXED

> The harness layer has grown to ~40 boards, 87.6k lines of tracked driver code and a 649-item human-decision queue that its own aggregator says "has stopped moving"; the handful of pieces wired to something that actually executes (githooks, the statusline decision count, design_review, the CTO loop) clearly pay for themselves, while the majority are boards about boards that have never been retired.

**Trajectory.** Flat on governance quality, regressing on volume. The clearest inflection is 2026-09-15: a self-review (harness/notes/harness-review-2026-09-15/, 39 surviving findings, 2 blockers) was run and then partly acted on within hours — aead81d9 (#96) stripped CronCreate from flow-conductor and retired the five panel-relay agents to harness/retired/ (rows 1f+1g, verified: `ls .claude/agents/panel-relay` fails, `grep -c CronCreate .claude/agents/flow-conductor.md` → 0); 1399abff (#94) added the versioned pre-commit capability hook; d81ea8ae (#95) added the protected-path diff (row 1h); 8a06d0dd (#97) ran the subagent-inheritance probe (row 1e); 464b94bc (#104) fixed the pre-push range. Two bulk commits the same morning, 5c765a7f and b484825f (both 2026-09-15 11:16), put seven previously-untracked boards under git, closing ST-3. Against that: rows 1a, 1c, 1d and 1i are still open, including both of the review's blockers; and the underlying growth pattern did not change — `git log --since=2026-08-07 --diff-filter=D -- harness/` returns zero deletions while ~1,400 files were added, and 274 of 835 commits in the window touch only harness/. The most recent activity (df0eb9a0 2026-09-16, b7592a9a 2026-09-18) keeps adding boards rather than closing them: RANKED.md is a new cross-round index over three existing rounds, and harness/verify/hython_verbs.py landed today. Net: the harness is now better at auditing itself than at retiring itself.

**The refuter on where this report overstates itself.** Yes, in three specific ways, though the central thesis survives contact.

1. "~40 boards" is inflated. `ls -d harness/*/ | wc -l` → 39 top-level directories, and nine of those are not boards at all: __pycache__, githooks, lib, notes, probes, prompts, retired, state, verify. The real board count is roughly 28-30. The headline treats infrastructure directories as evidence of board proliferation.

2. "87.6k lines of tracked driver code" is exactly right — I measured 87,612 lines across tracked .py/.ts/.ps1/.js/.sh under harness/ — but it is the wrong number to lead with, and it understates their own case by a factor of 25. All tracked harness content is **2,220,271 lines** (715 .json + 479 .md + 187 .txt dominate). If the argument is maintenance burden, the artifact volume is the burden, not the driver code.

3. The exit-6 claim ("nothing consumes that exit code") is stated with more confidence than a grep that excluded *.py can support. It is refuted by harness/clear/verify.py:113-115. Ironically the correct version is more damning, so the overconfidence cost them the better finding.

Against that: status "mixed" is the right call and is if anything slightly conservative in the other direction. Eleven of fourteen findings reproduced with their numbers exact to the digit (duplication hashes, legs.json state counts, RSI registry rungs, branch counts, the 148-test run, 85c8fe29's +862/−48). The one wholly refuted finding is the one they marked loadBearing:false. The headline sentence "boards about boards that have never been retired" is supported by the hardest evidence in the report: zero deletions under harness/ in 835 commits against 1,422 files added, and thirteen files total in harness/retired/.

### Findings

#### [BLOCKER · load-bearing] The harness's own human-gate aggregator reports 649 open decisions, 544 of them older than 30 days, and it prints the verdict itself: "the queue has stopped moving." The aging gate exits 6 but nothing consumes that exit code.

**Evidence.** `python harness/decisions.py` → "DECISIONS 649 open"; sections: for_ruling (486), design rosters (17), receipts not green (120), flywheel awaiting ratified (26); tail: "544 item(s) older than 30d - the queue has stopped moving."; `echo $?` → 6. Consumer search: `grep -rn "decisions.py" --include='*.ps1' --include='*.yml' --include='*.ts' --include='*.js' .` and `grep -n decisions harness/githooks/*` both return nothing outside harness/statusline.py:265-268.

**For the artist.** Indirect but real: items in this queue include D-F1..D-F14 (the panel is cut mid-word at 340px, the consent card has no verb at REVIEW level) and H4 (the Solaris demo path). None can be started by anyone until Joe rules.

#### [BLOCKER · load-bearing] That 649 is an undercount: decisions.py reads only receipts, flywheel_queue.json and design_review/<date>/RULINGS-OPEN.md. It does not read the CTO backlog (27 open, 18 gate=joe), RANKED.md's Round-1 and Round-3 rulings, rope's blocked tasks, the conductor boards' own human_gates, or tasks.json. The "everything waiting on a human, in one place" claim in its own docstring is false.

**Evidence.** harness/decisions.py:62,82,223 are the only design_review references (ROSTERS = harness/design_review, file name RULINGS-OPEN.md); `grep -n "BACKLOG|RANKED|rope/STATE|loop/STATE" harness/decisions.py` → no hits. Only one roster exists: `find harness/design_review -name RULINGS-OPEN.md` → harness/design_review/2026-09-14/RULINGS-OPEN.md (Rounds 2026-09-05 and 2026-09-15 have none). Uncounted elsewhere: harness/cto/BACKLOG.json 27 non-closed (18 gate=joe / 7 crux / 2 auto); harness/design_review/RANKED.md R3-A..R3-F + R1-Dir/R1-Bold; harness/rope/STATE.json 2 blocked_human + 1 blocked_seat + 1 blocked; reach(5)/loop(2)/memory(6) human_gates keys; harness/tasks.json 13 tasks with human_gate. Conservative true total ≈ 723.

#### [BLOCKER · load-bearing] The flagship harness engine (harness/run.ts + tasks.json, 81 tasks) is being maintained but not run. Its MODE B trigger has been armed since 2026-08-31, its completion ledger file does not exist, and its progress log's last entry is 2026-07-16 — yet it received a code change on 2026-09-15.

**Evidence.** harness/state/drop.json exists, dropped_at 2026-08-31T23:12:36Z. `ls harness/state/done.json` → "No such file or directory" (run.ts:64 names it the completion ledger). `tail harness/state/claude-progress.md` last entry dated 2026-07-16; `stat harness/state/claude-progress.md` → mtime 2026-07-26. Last commit touching it: d81ea8ae 2026-09-15 "fix(harness): 1h - loud post-cycle protected-path diff in run.ts". No bun/node process running run.ts (Get-CimInstance Win32_Process over bun.exe/node.exe/pwsh.exe shows only MCP servers and Claude Code).

#### [BLOCKER · load-bearing] In six weeks the harness tree grew by roughly 1,400 tracked files with zero directory deletions. 274 of 835 commits since 2026-08-07 (33%) touch only harness/ — no product file, no test file.

**Evidence.** `git log --since=2026-08-07 --oneline | wc -l` → 835; harness-only commit count via per-commit name-only filter → 274; `git log --since=2026-08-07 --diff-filter=D --name-status --format='' -- harness/ | wc -l` → 0; `--diff-filter=R` → 26, every one an archive move (harness/autorevise/missions/w3_*.json → missions/archive/). Files added by dir: notes 475, battleplan 293, autorevise 171, design_review 86, solaris_v3 79, memory 59. harness/retired/ holds 13 files total.

#### [BLOCKER · load-bearing] The SYNAPSE V2 blueprint — the forward product plan — is excluded from git by .gitignore and exists only on this disk. It would not survive a clean clone.

**Evidence.** `git check-ignore -v harness/v2-20260913/artifacts/v2-instantiate-gate/SYNAPSE_V2_BLUEPRINT_B.1.md` → ".gitignore:244:harness/*/artifacts/"; file is 52,216 bytes, mtime 2026-09-13 15:27. `git ls-files harness/v2-20260913 | wc -l` → 1 of 1,370 files on disk (40 MB).

#### [BLOCKER · load-bearing] Backup-Branches still pushes every non-master worktree branch to the public origin with no content gate and swallows push output — the higher of the two blockers the 2026-09-15 self-review raised (FENCE-2) is unfixed, and so are review rows 1a, 1c, 1d and 1i.

**Evidence.** harness/orchestrate.ps1:742-772 — the only filter is `if ($br -match '^worktree-wf_') { continue }`; pushes at :764 and :770 both end `2>&1 | Out-Null`; no secret grep anywhere (`grep -n "AKIA|secret" harness/orchestrate.ps1` → no hits). Row 1d acceptance check: `python -c "..."` on .claude/settings.local.json still returns ['Bash(python:*)', 'Bash(git:*)'] (lines 7 and 158 of 187 allow entries). Row 1i: `ls .claude/hooks/` → no guard-dispatch.py. Findings list: harness/notes/harness-review-2026-09-15/findings.json (39 survived, 2 blockers).

#### [BLOCKER · load-bearing] The Solaris v3 acceptance harness was built and has never been run: all 19 gates are NOT_RUN and the promotion rule's bindings map is empty. CTO backlog H4 (gate=joe) flagged this on 2026-09-05 and it has not moved.

**Evidence.** harness/solaris_v3/GATES.json — `Counter(status)` over its 19 rows → {'NOT_RUN': 19}; promotion_rule.bindings == {}. Newest file in the dir: harness/solaris_v3/runs/acceptance-validation/receipt.json, mtime 2026-09-05 09:12. harness/cto/BACKLOG.json item H4, status open, gate joe: "Solaris v3 demo is 7 days out with 19/19 gates NOT_RUN".

**For the artist.** Direct. The Solaris demo path an artist would actually drive has no passing acceptance evidence of any kind.

#### [WATCH · load-bearing] Four wave harnesses carry copy-pasted machinery: bus.py is byte-identical across apexforge, autorevise and battleplan; mission_schema.py exists in six divergent copies; compile_wave.py in four; make_control.py in four (two identical).

**Evidence.** md5sum over harness/{apexforge,autorevise,bastion,battleplan,autoresearch,loop}/: bus.py → fa28206cd949 in apexforge/autorevise/battleplan (6,324 bytes each), cb7abfd42be7 in bastion, 644611d3c887 in loop. make_control.py → ac34faf4f198 in apexforge+autorevise, two other hashes. mission_schema.py → six distinct hashes, sizes 4,725–13,945. compile_wave.py → four distinct hashes. No shared module: harness/lib/ holds one file, quote-safe.ps1.

#### [WATCH · load-bearing] The top-level status board renders from a manifest frozen five weeks ago. legs.json's newest rows are the 2026-08-13 wave-2 legs; 35 legs still read 'ready'; nothing from battleplan BP1–BP4, the six UX milestones, the three design-review rounds, or releases v5.66→v5.75.1 appears on it.

**Evidence.** `stat harness/legs.json` → 2026-08-13 10:55:30; last commit 29e55bf0 2026-08-13. `Counter(state)` over its 57 legs → {'done':16,'ready':35,'blocked':1,'held':5}. Leg ids end at W2-S5; `[i for i in ids if 'BP' in i.upper()]` → []. harness/status.py:20,100 reads MANIFEST = harness/legs.json. This is OBS-3 from the 2026-09-15 review, still open.

#### [WATCH · load-bearing] The RSI harness is internally honest and has produced no measured benefit: its own verifier passes 10/10 while reporting zero loops at the beneficial rung, after nine registered loops of which three are retired.

**Evidence.** `python harness/rsi/verify.py` → "10 PASS / 0 FAIL / 0 PENDING", with "P6 PASS benefit is not activity — no L5 claims to check (0 loops beneficial)". harness/rsi/REGISTRY.json: 9 loops, 6 LIVE / 3 RETIRED; rungs_proven maxima — A1/R/E/C at L1 or above, only C reaches L4; A1, R, E blocked_at L2; O and S blocked_at L1. Ladder L5 = "BENEFICIAL — a task-outcome metric moved, measured before AND after".

### What the surveyor did not look at inside its own dimension

- THE CENSUS THEY WERE ASKED FOR. The dimension named ~21 harnesses and asked for a LIVE / DORMANT / ABANDONED verdict on each. The report never produces one — it is fourteen thematic findings with no per-harness classification. Nine of the named harnesses get no verdict anywhere: clear (last commit 2026-08-02, 7 commits), bench (2026-09-03, 3), tidy (2026-08-09, 2), blocks (2026-08-06, 2), hardening (2026-09-15, 1), latency (2026-08-02, 13), autoresearch (2026-08-17, 13), apexforge (2026-08-18, 2), bastion (2026-08-18, 7). Measured with `for d in ...; do git log -1 --format=%ad --date=short -- harness/$d; done`.
- harness/rope, almost entirely. It is the second-most-committed board in the tree (61 commits, last 5c765a7f 2026-09-15) and appears in the report only as a four-row status tally inside the undercount finding. rope/STATE.json: gate A, branch rope/gate-a, 36 tasks, Counter(status) = {verified:22, needs_review:10, blocked_human:2, blocked_seat:1, blocked:1}. The 10 needs_review tasks are human-waiting and appear in neither the 649 nor the ≈723. L2-4 is blocked_seat on 'Dead PDG rollback kwarg (remove_files->remove_outputs)' — the same live defect CLAUDE.md §1.7 documents as never having executed.
- THE RATIO THAT ANSWERS THE QUESTION THEY WERE ASKED. The brief's final question is 'net asset or maintenance tax — evidence either way.' They never measured the tax. harness/ is 409 tracked .py files / 81,386 lines against python/ at 1,398 files / 306,848 lines and tests/ at 547 files / 158,694. Harness Python is ~17% of the repo's Python. That single ratio is the load-bearing evidence for the verdict they were asked to reach, and it is absent.
- A LEDGER/BACKLOG CONTRADICTION INSIDE THEIR OWN ASSET-HALF SOURCE. harness/cto/LEDGER.md:6 says '8 closed by predicate (B2-B8,B10)' — that set includes B6. BACKLOG.json's eight closed items are B1,B2,B3,B4,B5,B7,B8,B10. B6 is still status=open, gate=joe ('Recipe engine is dead code in the shipped product'). They cited the ledger row as proof the CTO loop works without checking it against the backlog it governs.
- THAT THE MODE-B TRIGGER IS UNTRACKED. harness/state/drop.json returns nothing from `git ls-files`. It is local working-tree state, and run.ts:63 (`MODE = existsSync(DROP) ? "B" : "A"`) means a fresh clone of b7592a9a is in MODE A. Every claim about what run.ts is or is not doing is therefore a claim about this machine.
- A SUBSTRING-MATCH ARTIFACT INSIDE THEIR OWN OPEN QUESTION — the exact failure class this repo codified as R5 word-boundary matching. They wrote 'harness/CLAUDE.md (84 lines) mentions only rsi and reach.' `grep -icE "\brsi\b|\breach\b" harness/CLAUDE.md` → 1, and that single hit is line 23, '**Reach tools by verb × context** (texture, scatter) × (COP, LOP)' — a UI palette statement, not the harness/reach board. Zero word-boundary 'rsi' hits; the apparent matches are the substring inside 've-rsi-on'. Same for README.md: its one `\breach\b` is 'reach the same file by another shape' at :201. Their CONCLUSION is right and stronger than they stated — neither doc names a single board — but the evidence offered for it was a substring artifact.
- WHETHER THE 649 IS REPRODUCIBLE ON A CLEAN CLONE. They never checked. It is: all four inputs (harness/notes/receipts, harness/state/flywheel_queue.json, harness/state/resolved.json, harness/design_review/2026-09-14/RULINGS-OPEN.md) return from `git ls-files`, and `git status --porcelain` on those paths is empty. This is the strongest thing about their headline number and they left it on the table.

### Open questions

- Was harness/run.ts deliberately superseded by orchestrate.ps1 plus the 18 .claude/workflows/*.js, or is it an unnoticed orphan? No document in harness/ says either way, and it is still being patched. UNKNOWN.
- Does SYNAPSE_V2_BLUEPRINT_B.1.md exist anywhere other than this disk — another machine, a branch, an untracked worktree? I could not verify a second copy. If not, the forward plan has a single point of failure.
- There is no retirement policy anywhere. harness/README.md (215 lines) describes only run.ts and mentions none of the ~40 other boards; harness/CLAUDE.md (84 lines) mentions only rsi and reach. What condition ends a harness?
- Is the 649-item queue actually blocking work, or is it a write-only log? decisions.py's own docstring says 24 of 26 flywheel items 'gate nothing mechanically' and calls the bottleneck 'triage ATTENTION, not authority' — that reasoning has never been re-tested against the 486 for_ruling items, which are the bulk of the number.
- harness/verify/checks.py is 3,244 lines and its only production driver (run.ts) is idle, but ~10 test files import it (test_phantom_guardrail, test_s_track, test_v6_track, test_protected_paths, …) so its functions are exercised as units. Whether the *loop* those checks were written to gate still exists is UNKNOWN.
- harness/cto/STATE.json records "runs": 2 while harness/cto/LEDGER.md's last row is labelled "cto crank 3". Minor, but the counter and the ledger disagree and neither is pinned by a test.

## h22-build-posture — MIXED

> The H22 symbol table and node catalogue are genuinely current for 22.0.400 and well-engineered, but the rulebook that CLAUDE.md names as law is an empty H21-baselined shell, the phantom lint never runs in CI, and the live `/mcp` scout on this host is today answering H21.0.671 verdicts with the gate reporting itself armed — including a wrong "does not exist" on `hou.undos.group`, which 16 server files call.

**Trajectory.** Improving sharply on the artifact side, flat-to-abandoned on the rulebook side. Over the last six weeks: `86af98c4` (2026-08-09) re-stamped the symbol table on 22.0.400; `8682afef` (2026-08-16) dumped the live node catalogue from 22.0.400; `27d609a7` (2026-08-17) added the version-agreement stamp; then a strong CTO wave on 2026-09-05 — `c0e65e67`/`582f1d97` (B10: hytest prefers the symbol-table stamp, killing the "newest hython is folklore" hazard), `7a948028` (B6: recipes off the deprecated karma LOP), `e11e9319` (B2: walk SWIG namespaces, +564 symbols, `hou.undos.group` finally in the table). Against that, `rulebook/` has had exactly one content commit since its charter — `e11e9319`, which only added a note saying the rulebook is empty and naming an interim authority. `git log --since=2026-08-07 -- rulebook/` returns that single commit. `docs/SUPPORT_MATRIX.md` has not been updated since `bdb6f63b` (2026-08-13) and is now stale against the table it describes.

**The refuter on where this report overstates itself.** Mostly no — and that is unusual. I re-ran 16 of their probes and the numbers came back to the digit every time (36472, 33255, 689/716/28/1, 1146/1274/152/24, 5576, 18 categories, 16 files, 157 .py files). Their live scout reproduction is exact, including the verbatim JSON fields. This is a well-measured report.

Three places where confidence outruns evidence:

1. The trajectory paragraph is the weakest thing in the report. "`git log --since=2026-08-07 -- rulebook/` returns that single commit" is simply not what that command returns — it returns two, and the full rulebook log returns four. Stating a command and its output is the report's own evidentiary standard, and this is the one place they did not meet it.

2. "The phantom lint never runs in CI" (headline, and finding 5's claim sentence) is stated more absolutely than the evidence supports. A quarantine phantom lint does run in CI, on every pull request, and passes because its input is empty. That is a materially different and worse fact than absence, and the headline's phrasing hides it.

3. The status "mixed" is right but the headline's framing of the symbol-table/catalogue side as unqualified strength papers over that the *consumers* of that good artifact are the problem. The table is excellent; the three things that read it (the /mcp scout, the harness phantom guardrail, the recipe catalogue resolver) each pick the wrong authority on this host. The artifact is not the achievement they credit it as until something reads it correctly.

One thing I want to credit rather than attack: they correctly refused to treat rag/catalog/h22.0.400/dump_receipt.txt as anything but a receipt-shaped claim, and I confirmed by measurement that the receipt is honest. That is the discipline this repo keeps getting burned for lacking.

### Findings

#### [BLOCKER · load-bearing] The live `/mcp` scout — the tool CLAUDE.md Safety Rule 15 mandates before writing any unfamiliar `hou.*` call — is serving the H21.0.671 symbol table on this H22-only host, with `gate_armed: true` and `stale: false`, and it returns `exists_in_runtime: false` for `hou.undos.group`, which is real on 22.0.400 and called in 16 files under `python/synapse/server/`.

**Evidence.** Live `mcp__synapse__synapse_scout` call (query: `hou.undos.group and hou.lopNetworks and pdg.PyEventHandler`) against the running seat returned: `"table":{"houdini_version":"21.0.671","loaded":true,"path":"...\\h21_symbol_table.json","stale":false,...}`, `"gate_armed":true`, and `{"symbol":"hou.undos.group","exists_in_runtime":false}`. Cross-check: `python -c` over both tables → `hou.undos.group h21=False h22=True`. `git grep -c "hou\.undos\.group" -- python/synapse/server/ | wc -l` → 16. Root cause at python/synapse/cognitive/tools/scout.py:651-663 (`_pkg_symbol_table_path`: "No major obtainable ... -> the H21 table").

**For the artist.** An agent asked to wrap a scene edit in an undo group is told the undo API does not exist, and will either route around it or emit something unverified. The gate that exists to stop hallucinated APIs is, on the MCP path, itself hallucinating absence.

#### [BLOCKER · load-bearing] `docs/studio/UPGRADE.md` promises the phantom gate is fail-closed on any build change ("scout refuses ... raises rather than serve unverified verdicts"). That promise does not cover this host's actual failure mode: the no-running-Houdini path deliberately does not set `stale`, so the gate stays armed and answers.

**Evidence.** docs/studio/UPGRADE.md:1-11 ("The default `refuse` policy is fail-closed: `synapse_scout` raises rather than serve unverified verdicts"). Contradicted by python/synapse/cognitive/tools/scout.py:743-762, whose comment states "`stale` is deliberately NOT flipped: it is the DRIFT_POLICY=\"refuse\" trigger ... and the ruling is WARN, not refuse" (R-M5b-1). Live proof: the scout call above returned `stale: false`, `gate_armed: true`, and served verdicts. The honest half is present — `verified_against_running_build: false` plus a warning naming the gap — but it sits below two fields that say the gate is fine.

**For the artist.** None directly. It means a reader of the runbook will believe an upgrade cannot silently disarm the gate, when on the MCP path it already has.

#### [BLOCKER · load-bearing] The rulebook that CLAUDE.md declares to be law is an empty shell pinned to H21: `surfaces/` holds only `.gitkeep`, `phantoms.json` is `{"phantoms": []}`, `manifest.json` has `sections: []` and a `runtime_baseline` of graphical 21.0.671 / hython 21.0.631 / python 3.11, and neither `scripts/rulebook_harvest.py` nor `scripts/rulebook_diff.py` exists.

**Evidence.** `ls -la rulebook/surfaces/` → `.gitkeep` only (0 bytes, dated Jul 26). `cat rulebook/phantoms.json` → `{"phantoms": []}`. `cat rulebook/manifest.json` → `runtime_baseline: {houdini_graphical: 21.0.671, hython: 21.0.631, python: 3.11}`, `sections: []`. `ls scripts/rulebook_harvest.py` and `ls scripts/rulebook_diff.py` → both "No such file or directory". Live host is 22.0.400 GUI / 22.0.417 hython / Python 3.13.10 (docs/HANDOFF_MEMORY_DUAL_WRITE.md:7). Only RBK-M0 (charter) ever shipped; SYNAPSE_RULEBOOK.md:154 defines the harvester as Mile 1 and :167 defines the H22 surface diff as Mile 5 ("horizon, do not start").

**For the artist.** None directly. But the planned mechanism for generating the H22 migration worklist ("harvest surfaces/h22.x.y, run the differ, the report IS the G2 migration worklist") was never built, so the H22 port surface is still discovered by hand, one defect at a time.

#### [BLOCKER · load-bearing] The phantom lint does not run in CI. CI runs pytest only; `check_phantom_clean` is a harness sprint guardrail, and the tests that touch it only scan synthetic strings, never the repository's own source.

**Evidence.** `.github/workflows/ci.yml` — the only workflow file — has no phantom, rulebook, or `harness/verify` step; `grep -rn "phantom" .github/workflows/*.yml` returns nothing. `harness/verify/checks.py:638` `check_phantom_clean(ctx)` is documented as a "Cross-cutting guardrail (tasks.json guardrails.checks)" consumed by run.ts. The two real-table tests, tests/test_phantom_guardrail.py:286 and :300, pass hand-written source strings to `_phantoms_in_source`, not repository files. rulebook/RULEBOOK.md:13 claims "If a quarantined phantom symbol appears in source, CI fails"; tests/rulebook/test_rulebook_meta.py:247 returns early when `phantoms.json` is empty, which it is.

**For the artist.** None directly. A phantom API can reach master through CI; only a harness sprint run would catch it, and only on added lines.

#### [WATCH · load-bearing] The same H21 default makes the scout answer `exists_in_runtime: true` for `pdg.PyEventHandler`, the exact symbol CLAUDE.md §1.7 quarantines as an H21.0.671 phantom ("no constructor defined").

**Evidence.** Same live scout call: `{"symbol":"pdg.PyEventHandler","exists_in_runtime":true}`. Table cross-check: `pdg.PyEventHandler h21=True h22=True` (the attribute name exists at depth 1 in both; the defect is that it has no constructor, which a `dir()` table structurally cannot express). CLAUDE.md §1.7: "⚠ H21.0.671 phantom: `pdg.PyEventHandler(fn)` has no constructor".

**For the artist.** None directly, but an agent building a PDG cook bridge gets a green light on the call shape that crashed before. The symbol table is a membership authority, not a constructability authority, and nothing in the scout response says so.

#### [WATCH · load-bearing] Two hython lanes are pinned to two different builds at HEAD: `tools/hy.ps1` hardcodes 22.0.417, while `.synapse/hytest.py` resolves 22.0.400 from the symbol-table stamp. Both were touched by the HEAD commit.

**Evidence.** tools/hy.ps1:30 `$Build = '22.0.417'` (with :12 "Build 22.0.417 explicitly"); `tools/hy_verbs_baseline.json` → `{'frozen_from_build': '22.0.417', 'python': '3.13.10'}`. Against that: `python .synapse/hytest.py --which` on this host → `C:/Program Files/Side Effects Software/Houdini 22.0.400/bin/hython.exe (symbol-table stamp h22 = 22.0.400)`. HEAD is b7592a9a "chore(hython): land the verb contract", which landed the .417 lane.

**For the artist.** None directly. It means a verb sweep and a panel test suite run on different Houdinis on the same machine, and the symbol table only describes one of them — a `hou.*` verdict cut in the .417 lane has no authority behind it.

#### [WATCH · load-bearing] The live node catalogue `rag/catalog/h22.0.400/` is real and complete (5,576 node types across 18 categories, 0 probe errors, dumped from live 22.0.400) — but its path is hardcoded with no build resolution in `python/synapse/recipes/spec.py`, so a seat on any other 22.x build silently validates recipes against the .400 catalogue.

**Evidence.** rag/catalog/h22.0.400/dump_receipt.txt final line: `DONE: build=22.0.400 category_files=18 node_types=5576 probe_errors=0`. Hardcode: python/synapse/recipes/spec.py:65 `repo_root() / "rag/catalog/h22.0.400/Lop.json"` and :73 `repo_root() / "rag/catalog/h22.0.400"` — no `_running_houdini_major()` call, unlike its siblings. python/synapse/validation/catalog.py:37 `DEFAULT_BUILD = "h22.0.400"` (that one at least honours `$SYNAPSE_PARM_CATALOG_BUILD` and returns None honestly).

**For the artist.** On a non-.400 seat, a recipe could be type-pinned against node types that moved or were renamed, and the failure surfaces as a `createNode` error mid-build rather than at validation.

#### [WATCH · load-bearing] `lop_solaris_knowledge_22.json` is stamped 22.0.368 while the h22 symbol table is stamped 22.0.400. The repo's own detector returns FAIL on this tree today, and nothing in CI or production calls it — the drift is pinned by a test that asserts the drift exists.

**Evidence.** `python -c` running `harness/verify/version_agreement.py::lop_catalog_agreement()` → `status: FAIL`, `reason: catalog lop_solaris_knowledge_22.json is stamped 22.0.368 but the introspected runtime authority h22_symbol_table.json is stamped 22.0.400`; the 21 pair passes as the control. tests/test_lop_catalog_stamp_agreement.py:118-135 `test_shipped_h22_catalog_is_in_observed_drift` asserts `result["status"] == "FAIL"`. `git grep lop_catalog_agreement` outside version_agreement.py matches only that test file.

**For the artist.** GraphValidator's LOP ordering advice on a 22.0.400 seat comes from a 22.0.368 harvest. It is advisory-only and never blocks, so the worst case is stale or missed advice, not a broken build.

#### [WATCH · load-bearing] A wide class of load-bearing runtime facts in production code is stamped `VERIFIED-RUNTIME 22.0.368` — a build that no longer has hython installed, so those claims cannot be re-probed without a reinstall.

**Evidence.** `git grep -n "22\.0\.368" -- '*.py'` (excluding notes/docs) hits python/synapse/blocks/canonical.py:38,95,144, blocks/plan.py:121,156,334, blocks/runtime.py:43,52,505, mcp_server.py:209, autonomy/evaluator.py:129, harness/verify/checks.py:500 (hdefereval vendor-pin), harness/blocks/invariants_m5.py:28,208,297. Installed hython roster on this host: `ls "/c/Program Files/Side Effects Software/Houdini "*/bin/hython.exe` → 21.0.773, 22.0.400, 22.0.417, 22.0.429 only (the 22.0.368 and 22.0.397 directories exist but carry no hython.exe).

**For the artist.** None observed. These are same-major claims and most will hold across point releases, but "verified" here means verified two point releases ago on a build that is no longer executable.

#### [WATCH · load-bearing] `tests/solaris/test_live_wiring.py` asserts the host is exactly 22.0.368 and is therefore unrunnable on this machine; it skips off-host, so nothing goes red and the live Solaris wiring evidence is frozen at a dead build.

**Evidence.** tests/solaris/test_live_wiring.py:59 `PINNED_BUILD = "22.0.368"` and :86 `assert hou.applicationVersionString() == PINNED_BUILD`, guarded by the module-level `pytestmark = pytest.mark.skipif(_NO_LIVE_HOU, ...)` at :43. docs/SUPPORT_MATRIX.md:33 acknowledges it: "Test-side: `tests/solaris/test_live_wiring.py::PINNED_BUILD` stays `22.0.368`".

**For the artist.** The five Solaris tool implementations (component_builder, create_variants, import_megascans, scene_template, set_purpose) have no live-host regression coverage on the build the artist actually runs.

#### [WATCH] `docs/SUPPORT_MATRIX.md` — the document that exists to name exact tested builds — is stale against the table it describes: it records 35,908 symbols for 22.0.400; the committed table now carries 36,472.

**Evidence.** docs/SUPPORT_MATRIX.md:9 `| H22.0.400 | 35,908 · stamped · gate armed ... | 2026-08-09 |` versus the table's own `symbol_count: 36472`. `git log -1 -- docs/SUPPORT_MATRIX.md` → bdb6f63b, 2026-08-13 — before the 2026-09-05 regeneration that added the 564 SWIG-namespace symbols.

#### [INFO · load-bearing] On a 22.0.417 lane the scout gate correctly refuses rather than lying — the build-mismatch path works. The hazard is confined to the no-build-known path.

**Evidence.** `python -c` driving `scout._load_symbol_table()` with `HOUDINI_VERSION=22.0.417` → `syms is None: True`, `reason='table stamp 22.0.400 != expected 22.0.417'`. Same probe with nothing set → `loaded=True`, `houdini_version=21.0.671`, `verified_against_running_build=False`. Implementation at python/synapse/cognitive/tools/scout.py:729-742.

**For the artist.** None. This is the part of the design that holds: inside Houdini, on any build, a mismatched table is refused outright rather than trusted.

#### [INFO · load-bearing] The H22 symbol table is genuinely current for 22.0.400 and is the strongest artifact in this dimension: 36,472 symbols, `truncated: false`, blake2b-stamped, regenerated 2026-09-05 with the SWIG-namespace walk that closed the `hou.undos` false-phantom class.

**Evidence.** `python -c` over python/synapse/cognitive/tools/data/h22_symbol_table.json → `schema=scout_symbol_table/v1, houdini_version=22.0.400, symbol_count=36472, truncated=False, blake2b=e408ca0b3e41762282a911d6a8ac2f62`. Producer: host/introspect_runtime.py, whose `_is_namespace_instance` (line 80) documents the CTO B2 fix. `git log -1 -- .../h22_symbol_table.json` → e11e9319 2026-09-05.

**For the artist.** Positive: inside a 22.0.400 Houdini seat, API-existence verdicts are trustworthy and build-accurate.

#### [INFO · load-bearing] Quantified blast radius of the H21 default: 28 depth-1 `hou.*` symbols and 152 depth-2 `pxr.*` symbols exist on H22 but not H21 (false-phantom vectors), against 1 `hou.*` and 24 `pxr.*` the other way (false-pass vectors on an H22 target).

**Evidence.** `python -c` set-differencing the two committed tables: depth-1 `hou.*` — h21=689, h22=716, h22-only=28 (`hou.Camera`, `hou.CameraPrim`, `hou.CopCable`, `hou.UniNode`, `hou.OpenCLDevice`, `hou.ApexUniGraphDebugger`, …), h21-only=1 (`hou.ChannelEditorPane`). `pdg.*` depth-1 — h22-only=2, h21-only=1. `pxr.*` depth-2 — h21=1146, h22=1274, h22-only=152, h21-only=24.

**For the artist.** None directly — this sizes the previous findings rather than adding a new one. The depth-1-only scanner limits the phantom lint's exposure, but 28 real H22 classes would read as absent under the H21 table.

#### [INFO] `packages/synapse.json` carries no Houdini version constraint, so the package loads into any of the four installed hython-capable builds with no guard that the symbol table or catalogues match that build.

**Evidence.** `cat packages/synapse.json` — keys are `name`, `enable`, `load_package_once`, `env` (SYNAPSE_ROOT, PYTHONPATH, MONETA_SRC, PXR_PLUGINPATH_NAME, SYNAPSE_MEMORY_BACKEND), `hpath`. No `hversion`, no version predicate. Installed builds with hython: 21.0.773, 22.0.400, 22.0.417, 22.0.429.

**For the artist.** Launching SYNAPSE from 22.0.429 gets a working panel with a disarmed API gate and a .400-pinned recipe catalogue, with nothing at install time saying so.

#### [INFO] The documented `SYNAPSE_HYTHON` pin is absent from the actual `.env` on this host, so every shell lane falls through to discovery. Discovery currently lands correctly on 22.0.400, but rule 2 (`hython` on PATH) outranks the symbol-table stamp and would silently win if a PATH entry ever appeared.

**Evidence.** `.env` contains only `NVIDIA_API_KEY` and `ANTHROPIC_API_KEY` (`grep -c HYTHON .env` → 0), despite `.env.example:18` and `.claude/skills/deploy/SKILL.md:27` both instructing the pin. Resolution order at .synapse/hytest.py:12-17 places PATH (rule 2) above the symbol-table stamp (rule 3); `_candidates_with_reason` at :100-119 implements that order. `which hython` currently returns nothing, so the stamp rule wins today.

**For the artist.** None today. It is a latent reordering hazard in an otherwise sound fix.

SYNAPSE_RULEBOOK.md:154 defines the harvester as Mile 1; :167 "**Mile 5 — H22 diff (horizon, do not start).**" Live host is 22.0.400 GUI / 22.0.417 hython lane. All exact.

(a) The RULEBOOK quarantine lint DOES run in CI: tests/rulebook/test_rulebook_meta.py::test_meta5_phantom_lint is a plain test under tests/, collected by ci.yml:109's `pytest tests/ -m "not needs_houdini"`. I ran it narrowly: `python -m pytest tests/rulebook/test_rulebook_meta.py -m "not needs_houdini" -q` -> "7 passed". It passes VACUOUSLY — its own docstring at :247-248 says "Empty phantoms.json (Mile 0) => trivially green" and the body returns at :251 `if not symbols`. That is an abstention printed as a pass, which is worse than absence. (b) The symbol-table phantom SCANNER (check_phantom_clean) is the one that genuinely never runs in CI. The surveyor's headline conclusion (a phantom can reach master through CI) survives, but 'the phantom lint does not run in CI' is false as stated, and the more damning fact — a green CI check that structurally cannot fail — went unnamed. Also: check_phantom_clean does NOT read rulebook/phantoms.json at all (checks.py:645-651 imports scout's _load_symbol_table as its membership authority), so the empty quarantine is irrelevant to it.
  - *Evidence:* .github/workflows/ci.yml is the only workflow (`ls -la .github/workflows/` -> one file). `grep -rn "phantom" .github/workflows/*.yml` -> rc=1, no output. harness/verify/checks.py:638 `def check_phantom_clean(ctx)` with the comment "Cross-cutting guardrail (tasks.json guardrails.checks)". tests/test_phantom_guardrail.py:286 and :300 do pass hand-written strings.
- **CORRECTED** — Two hython lanes are pinned to different builds at HEAD: tools/hy.ps1 hardcodes 22.0.417 while .synapse/hytest.py resolves 22.0.400 from the symbol-table stamp.
  - *Correction:* The split is real and the builds are right, but 'hardcodes' is wrong in a way that matters: tools/hy.ps1:35 is `if ($env:HY_BUILD) { $Build = $env:HY_BUILD }`, and the comment at :31-34 says the literal stays the DECLARED pin precisely so a cross-build run produces an artifact whose stamp no longer matches and "freshness fails loudly instead of silently overwriting the frozen baseline's evidence." So the .417 lane is a declared-pin-with-override that self-detects drift, not a hardcode. The surveyor's artistImpact — that a hou.* verdict cut in the .417 lane has no symbol table behind it — still holds and is the real point.
  - *Evidence:* tools/hy.ps1:30 `$Build    = '22.0.417'`; :12 "Build 22.0.417 explicitly." `python -c` over tools/hy_verbs_baseline.json -> {'frozen_from_build': '22.0.417', 'python': '3.13.10'}. `python .synapse/hytest.py --which` on this host -> "C:/Program Files/Side Effects Software/Houdini 22.0.400/bin/hython.exe (symbol-table stamp h22 = 22.0.400)". `git log -1` -> b7592a9a "chore(hython): land the verb contract, and close the dual-write handoff".

No env -> `loaded True ver 21.0.671 stale False vabrb False`. Implementation at scout.py:729-742 (`expected = _running_build() or _pkg_table_version()`; mismatch sets stale=True, loaded=False). Exactly as reported.

The comment at version_agreement.py:768-774 says so explicitly: "DELIBERATELY ABSENT from the exit gate below ... gating here would stop releases on a harvest that is nobody's current work." The accurate finding is sharper than the one reported: this is not an orphan check, it is a check that RUNS on every release, PRINTS FAIL, and is wired out of the exit code on purpose — with a TODO ("wire it into the line below once the 22.x catalog is re-harvested") that has not been actioned.

The surveyor's underlying point — that surfaces/, phantoms.json and manifest.json are all untouched since Mile 0, and Miles 1-5 never started — is correct and is the thing worth saying. But the specific evidentiary claim they used to prove it is false, and it is exactly the class of error they were sent to catch: a command reported with an output it did not produce.
  - *Evidence:* `git log --since=2026-08-07 --oneline -- rulebook/` returns TWO commits: e11e9319 and 31de38b8. `git log --oneline -- rulebook/` returns FOUR total: 7b82cd97 (2026-07-17, Mile 0 charter), 50b72e4b, 31de38b8, e11e9319 — three since the charter, not one.

### What the surveyor did not look at inside its own dimension

- THE BIG ONE — the H21 default reaches a SECOND consumer they never checked: the harness phantom guardrail itself. harness/verify/checks.py:645-651 (check_phantom_clean) calls scout's own `_load_symbol_table()` as its membership authority. I executed it on this host: it loads the 21.0.671 table, n=33255. I then fed it a synthetic added line and it returned offenders `[(3,'hou.Camera'), (4,'hou.CopCable'), (5,'hou.OpenCLDevice')]` against the H21 table and `[]` against the H22 table. All three classes are REAL on 22.0.400. So a sprint that legitimately adds any of the 28 H22-only classes gets short-circuited to a repair ticket by a guardrail citing a table for a build nobody is running. The surveyor sized the 28-symbol blast radius (finding 14) but never connected it to the one mechanism that would actually fire on it.
- THE SCOUT'S OWN TOOL CONTRACT IS HARDCODED TO H21 — in production code, at HEAD, not a doc. python/synapse/cognitive/tools/scout.py:1103, :1106, :1108 hardcode the strings "Houdini 21.0.671 documentation RAG", "exists in the live H21.0.671 runtime", "does NOT exist in H21.0.671" into SYNAPSE_SCOUT_SCHEMA['description']. This is the text every agent reads BEFORE calling the tool CLAUDE.md Safety Rule 15 mandates. I can quote it back from the live registration: the schema I loaded via ToolSearch this session begins "Scout the Houdini 21.0.671 documentation RAG...". The module docstring repeats it at :5-6, :15, :32, :52, :133, :946. This is a bigger H21 surface than the symbol-table default and it is 100% independent of which build is running — it would say 21.0.671 inside a correctly-gated 22.0.400 seat.
- THE ENTIRE RETRIEVAL CORPUS IS H21-NAMED AND H21-TYPED. `ls rag/skills/` -> exactly one directory, `houdini21-reference`, holding 104 .md files. scout_ingest.py:74 hardcodes `rag_root / "skills" / "houdini21-reference"` as the reference dir and :128 stamps every non-VEX snippet `"type": "houdini21-reference"`. My own live scout call returned a hit with `"type":"houdini21-reference"`. The surveyor audited the membership authority thoroughly and never looked at the grounding corpus sitting next to it — which is the other half of what scout returns, and is wholly un-migrated.
- THE 'REAL TABLE' PHANTOM TESTS CARRY A STALE IN-CODE STAMP. They cited tests/test_phantom_guardrail.py:286 and :300 correctly, but did not read the comment at :286-287: "Exercises the REAL table (h22_symbol_table.json, 35903 syms, stamp 22.0.368)". Both numbers are wrong at HEAD (36472 @ 22.0.400). It is the 'fixed claim where only one artifact moved' class, living inside the test that is supposed to be the evidence.
- THE CATALOGUE AUDIT QUESTION IS ANSWERABLE WITHOUT A LIVE HYTHON RUN — they left openQuestion #4 as UNKNOWN citing cost, but rag/catalog/h22.0.400/_audit.json is committed and readable: verdict 'pass', sample_n 20, total_mismatches 0, build 22.0.400, generated by scripts/audit_node_catalog.py with note 'deterministic sample; live re-read vs committed catalog'. That is a different artifact from the node-type ASSAY in SUPPORT_MATRIX.md:9 (2 missing types + 266 parm deltas). The open question conflates a passing dump-audit with an open assay; naming both would have been more useful than one UNKNOWN.
- THE REPO ANSWERS MOST OF THEIR PL-MILE OPEN QUESTION. docs/SYNAPSE_PROOF_STRATEGY_BLUEPRINT.md:11 states plainly "both PL-M1 twins have since merged (C-MTLX + C-U5). The leg now genuinely stands at PL-M2 — the live session." Lines :119-127 map every remaining mile (PL-M2 = G2 live session, PL-M3 = C.4/C.3/C.10 Copernicus, PL-M4 = RETINA M2, PL-M5 = RETINA M3, PL-M6 = G5 port waves + G6 counters), and :171 carries a binding CRUCIBLE correction that M3 is a separate gate. Nothing is closed past PL-M1, and every PL-M2+ mile is explicitly gated on a Joe-at-the-GUI session that has not happened. That is a reportable finding, not an UNKNOWN.
- CLAUDE.md'S OWN CURRENT-STATUS TABLE IS H21-STAMPED AND WAS NEVER TESTED. CLAUDE.md:454-464 marks six components "✅ Verified H21" (bridge, evolution, router, blast radius, PDG async cook, viewport sync) against a doc whose line 3 declares the target 22.0.400. CLAUDE.md:90 and :373 describe the scene hash as using "H21-native primitives"; :385 says "H21 uses the `pdg` module". 13 H21 references in the file the surveyor was told to treat as a claim to test. They tested §1.7's phantom note and nothing else in that class.
- THE LIVE /mcp SCOUT IS ALSO FUNCTIONALLY FLAKY, not merely mis-tabled. My first call returned `{"error":"ProgrammingError","message":"SQLite objects created in a thread can only be used in that same thread. The object was created in thread id 42800 and this is thread id 73532."}`. The retry succeeded. A gate that intermittently throws a raw SQLite error at the caller is a second, independent reliability finding about the same mandated tool, and the surveyor's single successful call would not have surfaced it.
- NOT CHECKED BY EITHER OF US, AND IT SHOULD BE: whether the running GUI seat's gate is actually armed on H22. I narrowed it — version_injector.py:43 is called from daemon.py:240 and referenced at panel/synapse_panel.py:528, and I proved HOUDINI_VERSION=22.0.400 loads the 36472-symbol table — so both the injection path and the environment path should land on H22. But neither of us probed the live seat, and I did not, because doing so requires houdini_execute_python against a live Houdini with a real memory store. Still UNKNOWN, and now with the reason stated.

### Open questions

- Does the in-Houdini `/synapse` path load the h22 table correctly? `synapse.host.version_injector` is called from host/daemon.py and panel/synapse_panel.py, so the GUI seat should inject 22.0.400 — but I did not probe the running seat's own `scout._load_symbol_table()` result, only the `/mcp` stock-python path. UNKNOWN whether the live panel's gate is armed on H22 or on H21.
- Is the H21 default a deliberate standing ruling or unfinished work? harness/cto/runs/2026-09-05/forge_report.json names it as "the remaining hollow" and says fixing it "reverses a ruling" (R-M5b-1). Which ruling, and whether defaulting to the newest committed per-major table would actually reverse it, is a decision I cannot make from the evidence.
- PL-M2 through PL-M6: PL-M1 is closed at v5.29.0 (CHANGELOG.md:452) on 22.0.368, but I found no closure receipt for the other five miles — `git grep PL-M` returns only the blueprint, CHANGELOG, two review docs, flywheel_queue.json and one test. UNKNOWN whether Miles 2-6 are open, abandoned, or tracked under different cycle IDs in harness/state/flywheel_queue.json (36 of 52 cycles are still `candidate`).
- Whether the `_audit.json` in rag/catalog/h22.0.400/ still passes — SUPPORT_MATRIX.md:9 records an open node-type assay with 2 missing types (`instancer`, `layout`) and 266 parm deltas as of 2026-08-09. I did not re-run scripts/audit_node_catalog.py (it needs a live hython run and would have been a heavier probe than this pass allowed).
- Whether any `22.0.429`-related hazard is real: BP3 notes (harness/battleplan/notes/BP3-CRUX_verdicts.md:33) claim 22.0.429's hython passes the hytest usability gate while BP4 mission files claim it fails. I did not run the ~3-minute `import pytest, PySide6` probe against 22.0.429 to settle it; the stamp rule makes it moot today either way.

## panel-ux — MIXED

> The panel is real, actively worked, and visually much improved this month — but its one always-visible status word can say "Ready" while memory is degraded, its honest health surface has been demoted into a hamburger menu, roughly a third of `panel/` is unreachable from the panel Houdini actually loads, and the most recent artist-experience audit (plus its merged fix) targeted the wrong panel file.

**Trajectory.** Improving, sharply, in the last ten days. `git log --since=2026-08-07 --oneline -- python/synapse/panel/ | wc -l` = 124 commits. The 2026-09-14..17 window alone carries the artist-facing burst: a752ca5b (bridge-down send guard), a2af501b (Undo Receipt #90), 1d982426 (durable TOPs render jobs + render workspace #85), 6ec3df2e (#99 consent card phrasing), 3630ace5 (#100 one type scale), daba84d2 (#102), 7f2fa460 (#98 copy pass), 85c8fe29 (#106, the four measured readability defects), df0eb9a0 (four subtractions + RANKED.md). Four unnumbered `fix(panel)` commits on 2026-09-14 close honesty bugs (b475ee22 "stop showing artists 'Create usd prim' and a map running at 4 of 8", 616a3469 "stop a REVIEW consent card filing a rejection that never happened", b8aa22da, eab47244). The regression is in governance, not code: `harness/state/DECISIONS.md` header reads "Generated 2026-08-01 21:05 ... 286 open" and `harness/design_review/*/RULINGS-OPEN.md` exists only for 2026-09-14, so the rounds-2/3 design calls are tracked by a hand-written index (RANKED.md, 2026-09-16) and not by the board.

**The refuter on where this report overstates itself.** Partly, and in one place seriously.

The headline's three named blockers all survive independent verification — the false-green status word, the demoted health strip, and the wrong-panel audit round are each reproducible from code I read myself. "mixed" is a fair status and "improving sharply" is arithmetically true (124 commits, verified).

Where the report is more confident than its evidence supports:

1. **Finding 10 is stated with a confidence its own method cannot earn, and it is wrong.** "Red only in a layout profile no artist can reach — so it measures a spec, not a shipped defect", severity info, artistImpact "None". The surveyor read `('curious', ...)` out of the assertion message and inferred profile-scoping, without noticing that `PROFILES = ("curious", "expert", "ml")` puts curious first and the loop aborts there. The expert profile — the only one the panel composes — is equally red. This converts an "info, no artist impact" line into a live shipped four-hue violation and promotes R2-B1 from spec cleanup to real decision. It is the one place where their confidence actively misdirects the reader.

2. **Finding 11's conclusion outruns its measurement.** One of the contract's three `passing: false` features was re-run; the other two were not. "The backlog is one item smaller than it reads" is asserted on a third of the goalposts, and the contract's own description ("400px") is stale against the token it grades with (420).

3. **The chat_panel openQuestion inherits a stale doc verbatim.** "The only panel with GateWidget wired from the start" comes from docs/design/SYNAPSE_PANEL_REDESIGN.md, and the registered panel has GateWidget in nine places. The report's own ground rule was that docs are claims to test.

4. **Two loadBearing:false marks are misclassifications in the same direction.** The preflight-never-fires finding is the artist-facing half of their own blocker-severity freeze finding, and it is worse than they stated (on the live-bridge path the advisory is not even logged). Both were marked not load-bearing.

Counting corrections: 2 REFUTED, 4 CORRECTED, 10 CONFIRMED. The corrections are detail-level except finding 10, which is a directional error.

### Findings

#### [BLOCKER · load-bearing] The panel's one visible status sentence is derived only from Houdini reachability, so it says "Ready" (GROW green) while memory is degraded, no model is connected, or the bridge is down. This is the exact false green `health_strip.py` was written to prevent, reintroduced one layer up.

**Evidence.** python/synapse/panel/synapse_panel.py:3868-3882 — `_apply_context` sets `self._conn_state = "connected"` in the else branch of `if self._gate_stale_reason`, i.e. purely from a successful context gather. `:3720-3734` `_render_state` reads only `_was_busy` / `_turn_state` / `_conn_state`. python/synapse/panel/designsystem/tokens.py:764,769 — `STATUS["connected"] = (GROW, "connected", "Ready")`, `STATUS["disconnected"] = (SLATE, "offline", "Not connected")`. No memory, job, or model fact reaches `_set_header`.

**For the artist.** An artist reads "Ready" as "the whole thing works." It means only "import hou succeeded and the phantom-API gate is fresh." Memory silently discarding everything they ask to be remembered leaves the word green.

#### [BLOCKER · load-bearing] The health strip is no longer a strip. Its own module docstring promises "always-visible" cells where "Degraded is loud ... no click required to notice"; at HEAD the `HealthStrip` widget is never instantiated and the four cells are built only when the overflow menu is opened.

**Evidence.** python/synapse/panel/health_strip.py:1,11-23 (docstring: "always-visible strip of four FACT-sourced cells", "no click required to notice") vs python/synapse/panel/synapse_panel.py:3891-3902 `_update_health_strip` whose entire body is `self._health_facts = (connection, project)` with the comment "the strip left the rail ... no rail pixels", and :2795-2806 which calls `_hs.build_cells(_hs.gather_snapshot(...))` inside `menu.addMenu("Health")` with `fact.setEnabled(False)` and the reason only on `setToolTip`. Grep for `setObjectName("health_strip")` returns one hit, health_strip.py:598, inside the uninstantiated class. The QSS rule `#health_strip QLabel` (designsystem/qss.py:950) therefore paints nothing, and `cell_html`'s inline-reason path (health_strip.py:566-576, "loud without a click") is unreachable.

**For the artist.** The RED "memory — not saving — anything you ask to be remembered is being discarded" message exists, is correct, and is three interactions away: open overflow, open Health, hover a disabled row.

#### [BLOCKER · load-bearing] Two panels ship on disk and only one is registered. The 2026-09-15 "firstrun" artist audit and its merged fix both targeted the unregistered one, so the FR-1 send guard is not in the code path Houdini loads.

**For the artist.** None directly — the registered panel's `_send` never touches the WS bridge, so FR-1's silent-queue-and-replay class does not exist there. The cost is that a whole artist-experience review round bought no change to what artists run. UNKNOWN whether any user has hand-installed synapse_chat.pypanel into their prefs.

#### [WATCH · load-bearing] About 30 of 110 modules under python/synapse/panel/ are unreachable from the registered panel's import closure, including named artist features: error_translator, working_indicator, decision_log, explain_mode, network_trace, bookmarks, scene_doctor, dependency_map, cross_scene, exposure_seam, performance_profiler, prompt_to_hda, image_prep, recipe_card, save_shot, shot_login, apex_explainer, apex_trace, voice_contract, agent_prompts.

**Evidence.** AST transitive-import walk over python/synapse/panel/ rooted at synapse.panel.synapse_panel (handles absolute, relative and `from pkg import submodule` forms): `total 110 reachable 80 unreached 30`. Cross-checked by grep: `grep -rn "panel.error_translator" --include=*.py python/` (excluding the file itself) returns nothing; same for working_indicator, decision_log, explain_mode, network_trace, bookmarks. `session_journal` is reached only server-side (python/synapse/mcp/tools.py:152). Caveat: a string/importlib-based import would be missed by this walk.

**For the artist.** Features that read as shipped in the tree — a scene doctor, a dependency map, an error translator that would turn raw exceptions into artist language — are not reachable from the panel. The error_translator case is the sharpest: raw provider exceptions reach the transcript with a translator sitting unused in the same package.

#### [WATCH · load-bearing] The panel can still freeze the Houdini UI during a render, by design. The mitigation is a refusal budget, not an out-of-process render.

**Evidence.** python/synapse/server/foreground_guard.py:3-11 — "An in-process render CAPTURES Houdini's main thread for its entire duration (handlers_render.py node.render() — the confirmed freeze site)" and on Indie "the render must stay in-process". Budgets at foreground_guard.py:_BUDGETS: karma_cpu allow 256*256 @ 16 samples, karma_xpu allow 512*512 warm. python/synapse/server/handlers_render.py:472-490 probes then `if not verdict["allow"]: raise RuntimeError("Foreground render refused: ...")`; `payload.get("force_foreground")` overrides the refusal. `houdini_render` is in `_KNOWN_SLOW_TOOLS` (python/synapse/panel/bridge_adapter.py:299).

**For the artist.** Any allowed render locks the viewport, node selection and the panel for its whole duration. The escape hatch is the TOPs render workspace (`/render`, commit 1d982426), which is out-of-process and durable.

#### [WATCH] The "this may briefly freeze the UI" heads-up is log-only and never reaches the artist: the production dispatch path passes emit_preflight=False, and no panel slot connects the signal anyway.

**Evidence.** python/synapse/panel/tool_executor.py:413 declares `preflight_warning = QtCore.Signal(str, str)`; :494 emits it only `if emit:`; :744 `execute_tool_off_main` calls `self._dispatch(request, emit_preflight=False)` with the docstring at :728-731 saying it "deliberately skips the preflight_warning Qt emit". `grep -n preflight_warning python/synapse/panel/*.py` shows zero `.connect(` in synapse_panel.py. The worker uses the off-main path (claude_worker.py:527-552).

**For the artist.** The UI goes unresponsive with no warning. The advisory text exists and is well written; it lands in the log file only.

#### [WATCH · load-bearing] The command palette advertises 26 slash commands; the registered panel implements 4 of them. The rest are sent to the language model as literal text, and nothing in the system prompt teaches the model what they mean.

**Evidence.** python/synapse/panel/command_palette.py:87-112 — `_SLASH_COMMANDS` has 26 rows (/help, /diagnose, /fix, /preflight, /render, /journal, /explain, /trace, /vex, /recipes, /saved-recipes, /lookdev-suggestion, /events, /hda, /login, /apex ×6, /scene, /inspect, /search). python/synapse/panel/tool_palette.py:161-165 — for `e.category == "command"` the palette's `send` value is `e.command`, the literal string. python/synapse/panel/synapse_panel.py:3156-3179 — `_send` intercepts exactly `/render`, `/events`, `/saved-recipes`, `/lookdev-suggestion`, `/restore-session`; everything else falls through to the model. `grep -n "slash\|/diagnose\|/vex\|/apex" python/synapse/panel/system_prompt.py` returns no slash-command guidance.

**For the artist.** An artist who picks "/diagnose — Scene health audit and diagnostics" from the palette sends the model the word "/diagnose" and gets whatever the model improvises. Discoverability promises a command surface the panel does not have.

#### [WATCH · load-bearing] Two different controls are called "Connect", and the more prominent one is not the one the README tells a new artist to use.

Use Connect models below the prompt." Filed unruled as R2-misc in harness/design_review/RANKED.md.

**For the artist.** First send with no model configured produces a message pointing at a ghost button in the footer, while a primary-variant button labelled "Connect" sits in the rail doing something unrelated. This is the most likely first stumble.

#### [INFO] The multi-provider relay is real, not aspirational: five engines are wired and four of them carry a working tool-calling transport. The engine switch is only reachable through the overflow menu.

**Evidence.** python/synapse/panel/providers/registry.py:109 `PROVIDER_IDS = ("claude", "gemini", "nemotron", "ollama", "custom")`. anthropic_provider.py (431 lines, prompt-cache aware), gemini_provider.py:154-276 translates functionDeclarations to `tool_use` blocks, nemotron_provider.py:133-229 translates Anthropic tool schemas to OpenAI function tools, ollama_provider.py and custom_provider.py both subclass NemotronProvider and inherit that transport verbatim (ollama_provider.py:34, custom_provider.py:30). Switch UI: synapse_panel.py:2811-2821, an "Engine" submenu built inside the overflow menu. registry.py:26-33 records a live probe dated 2026-07-28 (claude 6 declared / 11 served, nemotron 2/102, ollama 1/13, zero declared-but-absent).

**For the artist.** Switching engines works but is buried. The model-row provenance is now ~7 weeks old; providers/probe.py is the live authority and marks a dropped row `available=False, reason="declared_but_absent"`.

RANKED.md's "What shipped already" section agrees.

#### [INFO · load-bearing] Live confirmation that the honesty problem the health strip names is real: the running bridge still returns a hardcoded `"healthy": true`, and the live Moneta store cannot answer for its own write health.

**Evidence.** Live read-only MCP calls against the running Houdini 22.0.400: `synapse_ping` → `{"pong":true,"protocol_version":"4.0.0"}`. `synapse_health` → `"healthy":true` plus `write_plane.store.write_health.sources`: `"MonetaBackedStore":{"answered":false,"detail":"MonetaBackedStore exposes no health() -- the store write-health contract ... is absent on this object, so its write state is unknown, not healthy"}` and `"MonetaBackedStore._jsonl_net":{"answered":true,"sick":false,"records":1159}`. health_strip.py:36-38 names exactly this: "NEVER synapse_health.healthy — that field is a hardcoded True liveness echo (handlers.py) and would pin the cell green forever."

**For the artist.** Today the store is healthy and the verdict comes from the JSONL dual-write safety net one level down. If Moneta alone were serving without that net, the panel's memory verdict would be UNKNOWN by design — which is correct, and which nothing on the visible surface would show.

#### [INFO] synapse_panel.py is a 4,083-line single class carrying the rail, ribbon, three faces, composer, palette anchoring, worker lifecycle, consent slot, profile machinery and overflow menu.

**Evidence.** `wc -l python/synapse/panel/synapse_panel.py` = 4083; `class SynapsePanel(QtWidgets.QWidget)` at :509 with no second top-level class before the module end. By comparison chat_panel.py is 1083, health_strip.py 665, claude_worker.py 874.

**For the artist.** None directly. It is why a design call like R2-C2 ("a per-turn receipt line") comes with a warning that an implementer copying the local idiom builds the expensive fixed-chrome version — the file offers no cheap place to put a new thing.

No memory, job, model or bridge fact reaches any of them. tokens.py:764 `"connected": (GROW, "connected", "Ready")`, :769 `"disconnected": (SLATE, "offline", "Not connected")` — both line numbers exact. Their cited range 3868-3882 is a two-line drift on the write and 3720-3734 a four-line drift on the reader; substance is exact.
