Two things they missed: (a) the contract's description says "400px tall" but the test grades against `t.PANEL_MIN_HEIGHT`, which tokens.py:786 sets to **420** ("was 400; raised for Mile-1b") — so the contract's prose is stale by 20px as well as its status; (b) the SAME contract carries two more `passing: false` features (`.synapse/verify.py max-min-height python/synapse/panel 200` at :23-25 and `pytest -q` at :26-28) that they did not run, so "the backlog is one item smaller than it reads" is asserted on one of three unchecked goalposts.
- **CONFIRMED** — The multi-provider relay is real: five engines wired, four carrying a working tool-calling transport; switch only reachable through the overflow menu (marked loadBearing:false — spot-checked).
  - *Evidence:* registry.py:109 `PROVIDER_IDS = ("claude", "gemini", "nemotron", "ollama", "custom")` — exact line. anthropic_provider.py is 431 lines; gemini_provider.py 294 with `stream(... tools ...)` at :154; nemotron_provider.py 512 with `_to_openai_tools` at :133-145 and the tool_use→tool_calls translation at :228+. Engine submenu at synapse_panel.py:2782-2791 inside the overflow. registry.py:20-33 carries the 2026-07-28 probe table with the claude 6/11, nemotron 2/102, ollama 1/13, zero declared-but-absent rows verbatim. Line drift only: `class OllamaProvider(NemotronProvider)` is at ollama_provider.py:96 (they said :34) and `class CustomProvider(NemotronProvider)` at custom_provider.py:28 (they said :30).
- **CONFIRMED** — The four measured readability defects from 2026-09-15 are genuinely closed at HEAD (marked loadBearing:false — spot-checked).
  - *Evidence:* `grep -n "pt;" python/synapse/panel/designsystem/qss.py` returns nothing (D1 closed). synapse_panel.py:560 `c.apply_placeholder_palette(self)` with the helper defined at designsystem/components.py:344 (D3 closed). `git show --stat 85c8fe29` = "design(panel): four measured readability defects — a unit, a fill, the quiet ramp, and four weights (#106)", touching qss.py, tokens.py, components.py, synapse_panel.py plus 530 new lines of tests/test_panel_readability_defects.py — so this is not a doc-only "fixed" claim. I did not re-run the WCAG contrast assertions themselves, so the D2/D4 numbers are verified as shipped-code change, not as recomputed ratios.
- **CONFIRMED** — The running bridge returns a hardcoded "healthy": true, and the live Moneta store cannot answer for its own write health.
  - *Evidence:* Re-ran the live read-only MCP calls against the running Houdini 22.0.400. `synapse_ping` → `{"pong":true,"protocol_version":"4.0.0",...}`. `synapse_health` → `"healthy":true` with `write_plane.store.write_health.sources.MonetaBackedStore` = `{"answered":false,"detail":"MonetaBackedStore exposes no health() -- the store write-health contract (is_degraded / degraded_reason / health) is absent on this object, so its write state is unknown, not healthy"}` and `MonetaBackedStore._jsonl_net` = `{"answered":true,"sick":false,"records":1159,...}` — their quoted values reproduce exactly, including the 1159. The hardcode is real: `grep -n '"healthy"' python/synapse/server/handlers.py` → :937 `"healthy": True,` (a literal, not a computed value). The health_strip warning they cite is at health_strip.py:36.
- **CORRECTED** — synapse_panel.py is a 4,083-line single class (marked loadBearing:false — spot-checked).
  - *Correction:* synapse_panel.py is a 4,083-line module holding four top-level classes; `SynapsePanel` itself spans :509 to end, about 3,575 lines. The architectural point (no cheap place to put a new thing) survives intact; the figure does not.
  - *Evidence:* `wc -l` = 4083 confirmed. But `grep -n "^class " python/synapse/panel/synapse_panel.py` returns FOUR top-level classes: `_ShortcutLayout` :184, `_GrowingInput` :277, `_InputResizeGrip` :400, `SynapsePanel` :509. Their "`class SynapsePanel` at :509 with no second top-level class before the module end" is true only because they looked forward from 509 and not backward.
- **REFUTED** — openQuestion: chat_panel.py "is the only panel with GateWidget wired from the start" (docs/design/SYNAPSE_PANEL_REDESIGN.md:20).
  - *Correction:* The registered panel has GateWidget, a dedicated consent slot and a consent-sync method. Whatever else chat_panel.py uniquely holds (the HDA wizard, send_guard, the WS bridge), the consent gate is not it, and the redesign doc that says otherwise is stale by design-generations.
  - *Evidence:* The registered panel wires GateWidget too. `grep -c GateWidget` → chat_panel.py 2, **synapse_panel.py 9**. synapse_panel.py:101 `from synapse.panel.gate_widget import GateWidget`, :1475-1481 builds `_consent_slot` (hidden while empty), :1514 `_sync_consent_slot`, and the module's own docstring at :5-7 says it reuses "ClaudeWorker streaming + ToolExecutor + ChatDisplay + GateWidget ... Closes the consent-gate gap the shipped legacy panel had: GateWidget is wired in, so HumanGate proposals surface as actionable cards." The doc they cited describes the registered panel as a "3032-line CDATA" monolith with "**no gate UI**" — that file is 4,665 bytes and delegates to the package (houdini/python_panels/synapse_panel.pypanel:56). This is the failure class the brief named first: a claim copied out of a doc rather than measured from code, in a report whose own ground rule was that docs are claims to test.
- **CONFIRMED** — Trajectory: 124 commits on python/synapse/panel/ since 2026-08-07; improving sharply.
  - *Evidence:* `git log --since=2026-08-07 --oneline -- python/synapse/panel/ | wc -l` → 124, exact. Spot-checked two of the named commits by `git show --stat`: a752ca5b ("fix(panel): refuse chat sends while the bridge is down (FR-1) (#91)") and 85c8fe29 (#106) both exist with the contents described. a2af501b ("feat(trust): the Undo Receipt — tell the artist what one Ctrl+Z reverses (#90)") is real and, unlike FR-1, it DID land in the registered panel (synapse_panel.py:1478, :1496-1510 build `_turn_receipt`, `_receipt_badge`, `← REVERT`).

### What the surveyor did not look at inside its own dimension

- A third panel tree still ships. `python/synapse/ui/panel.py` plus `python/synapse/ui/tabs/` exist at HEAD — the "Orphaned admin" panel docs/design/SYNAPSE_PANEL_REDESIGN.md:21 calls "registered by nothing". It sits outside python/synapse/panel/, so it is absent from both the "two panels" claim and the 110-module denominator, meaning the dead-panel surface is larger than the report's 30-of-110 figure conveys.
- The consent card — the panel's artist-facing safety surface — was never assessed, only mis-attributed. synapse_panel.py:1475-1481 builds a `_consent_slot`, :1514 `_sync_consent_slot` shows it "iff something in it is live", and gate_widget is imported at :101. What a REVIEW/APPROVE proposal actually looks like to an artist, whether it blocks, and what its timeout does is the highest-stakes artist interaction in the panel and it is not in the report.
- The Undo Receipt (#90) is live in the registered panel (synapse_panel.py:1478, :1496-1510: `{n} CHANGES` badge + `← REVERT`) and is arguably the panel's strongest artist-trust surface. The report cites the commit in the trajectory paragraph and never assesses the feature — including the obvious question of whether the receipt's count matches what one Ctrl+Z actually reverses, given CLAUDE.md §1's own "wrapping is not reversing" caveat.
- The MCP-path bypass. claude_worker.py:432 tries `try_mcp_tool_call` FIRST and only falls through to the off-main dispatch when "the MCP path is down" (:528). Since preflight lives only in ToolExecutor (`estimate_inline_cost` has exactly two references, both in tool_executor.py), every tool call on the normal live-bridge path gets neither warning nor log line. The report analysed only the fallback path.
- Concurrency UX. `_ACTIVE_PANEL_WORKERS` (synapse_panel.py:51, :3168, :3215) is a process-wide reservation: a second send is refused with "A SYNAPSE panel task is still running on this workstation." One artist, two docked panels, or a queued second thought — all refused with a text line in the transcript. Not examined.
- Host-DPI legibility was cited but not tested — and the premise they passed through is stale. R3-C's "Nothing in the panel scales — `scaled()` defaults to 1.0" is quoted uncritically in finding 9's evidence, but synapse_panel.py:551 `self._chrome_scale = self._host_font_scale()` and :553 `qss.stylesheet(self._chrome_scale)` mean the host font IS read and drives the whole build; tokens.py:585-598 floors the scale ladder at the measured host scale. Whether an artist on a 192-DPI display actually sees 11px beside Houdini's 27px at HEAD is a live measurable question the report left as a backlog quote.
- The chat surface's behaviour, as opposed to its composition. The brief asked "what the chat surface does" and the report answers with module inventory. Streaming and token rendering, how tool calls appear mid-turn, how a provider exception is surfaced (they note error_translator is unreachable but never check what DOES reach the transcript), the Stop button's semantics versus Cancel cook versus Emergency halt (three verbs in the overflow at :2813+), image/attachment handling, and keyboard navigation are all unexamined.
- The Help path they claim to answer for a cold artist. synapse_panel.py:3911 `self._open_doc("docs/help/index.html")` and that file exists — nobody opened it. "What an artist hits first" is asserted without checking the one in-panel document written for exactly that artist.
- Two of the three `passing: false` features in .synapse/contracts/docking-minimums.yaml (:23-25 `verify.py max-min-height python/synapse/panel 200`, :26-28 `pytest -q`) were never run, although the first is cheap, read-only and directly decides whether R2-D1 is closed.
- The health-strip producers were read but never exercised. `gather_snapshot` / `build_cells` (health_strip.py) are the code that would decide RED-vs-green if the strip were visible; running them against the live process would have shown whether today's MonetaBackedStore `answered:false` renders UNKNOWN or green — which is the actual test of whether the honest layer is honest, not just of whether it is buried.

### Open questions

- If an artist opened the panel cold today, the first thing they hit is the model handshake: the panel greets with "What are we building?" (synapse_panel.py:1373), the rail flips from "Not connected" to "Ready" within one 2s tick purely because `import hou` worked, and then the first Send returns "Connect a model before sending. Use Connect models below the prompt." — pointing at a ghost button in the footer while a primary-styled button labelled "Connect" sits in the rail starting a bridge server instead. Second thing: the command palette (Ctrl+K or "/") listing ~26 slash commands of which 4 do anything locally. Third, and the one that costs real work: an allowed in-budget render freezes the whole UI with no warning, and nothing visible would ever tell them memory had stopped saving.
- Does anyone still run chat_panel.py? It is the only panel with GateWidget wired from the start, the full HDA wizard, and the FR-1 send guard, and docs/design/SYNAPSE_PANEL_REDESIGN.md:20 calls it "the only tested one — but not installed". UNKNOWN whether any installed prefs tree carries synapse_chat.pypanel. If nobody does, ~6 modules (chat_panel, quick_actions, hda_views, send_guard, styles, context_bar) are dead weight that still absorbs review attention.
- Was R2-D1 (the faces stack 30–54px too tall at 400px) fixed between 2026-09-14 and HEAD, or does it only pass under hython and not under the bare python313 interpreter the contract's width feature names? The contract still says passing: false and RANKED.md still ranks it as an open unowned assignment.
- Does the panel's Health submenu get opened by anyone, ever? If the answer is no — and a submenu inside an overflow menu is the shape of a thing nobody opens — then the anti-silent-degradation work in health_strip.py (665 lines, extremely carefully reasoned) is currently buying nothing, and the decision is whether to give it rail pixels back or fold its RED states into the one state sentence.
- Nothing in the shipped panel routes provider/transport exceptions through error_translator.py. Was that module superseded by something in the registered panel, or did it just never get connected?
- UNKNOWN: I did not verify what the running Houdini's panel actually looks like. I confirmed the bridge is live (ping/health over /mcp) but sent no query that would tell me which pypanel interface is open or which profile it composed.

## open-work — MIXED

> The debt is far smaller than the branch count suggests — 49 branches are unmerged by ancestry but only 14 carry content master lacks — yet today's HEAD commit is unpushed and six local-only branches holding finished, untriaged work sit outside every triage the 2026-09-15 closeout performed.

**Trajectory.** Sharply improving, but discontinuously — one flush, not a flow. PR merges by day (`gh pr list --state merged --limit 60 --json number,mergedAt --jq '.[].mergedAt[:10]' | sort | uniq -c`): 4 on 2026-08-15, then a 24-day drought with 1 PR on 09-08 and 1 on 09-10, then **24 PRs merged on 2026-09-15** (#83-#106) and 2 on 09-17 (#107, #108). Master commit volume per week (`git rev-list --count master --since=.. --until=..`): 260 (Aug 14-21), 2 (Aug 21-28), 145, 207, 101 (Sep 11-18). The 09-15 closeout train (`harness/notes/closeout-2026-09-15/merge_train*.stdout`) is what discharged the backlog: it merged ef690e50, b86676b1, 1d982426, b046846a, 5ad12f04, 8bdd3f70, ada16990, a2af501b, a752ca5b, 2bff0cc2 — all verified in master via `git merge-base --is-ancestor`. What has *not* improved is disposition follow-through: of 16 branches given written verdicts in `verdicts.json`, the ARCHIVE_DELETE on `stray/audit-log-threading` was never executed, and the six `worktree-wf_*` branches created 2026-09-14 — one day before the train — were never triaged at all. Since the train, 7 of the last 12 master commits bypassed PR entirely (direct push).

**The refuter on where this report overstates itself.** Yes, in one specific place — and understated in another, which is the more interesting error.

Overstated: the headline's second half. "Only 14 carry content master lacks" is presented as a measurement and is not one. It is 49 minus three buckets, and one of those buckets (the 7 branches scored by "zero files absent from master") uses a test that cannot detect unlanded content inside a file that already exists on master. Their own finding #4 is the counterexample: worktree-wf_8a870870-1d5-3 scores zero-files-absent and carries 117 unlanded lines. I reproduced their 18-branch patch-id sub-number exactly, so the method they used for that bucket is sound; the 7-branch bucket borrowed the conclusion without the method. My independent measurement brackets the answer between 12 (branches introducing new files) and 31 (branches with at least one commit whose patch-id is absent from master). "14" should have been a range with the 7-branch bucket marked UNKNOWN.

Also overstated in kind: "no migration path on master at all" for agent.usd (migrate_to_v2 exists at agent_state.py:168 with zero production callers), and "demonstrably superseded" for worktree-wf_061df37e-b74-4 (master's gate_widget.py does carry the REVIEW-keeps-REVERT rule and master's test_consent_way_back.py is 317 lines vs the branch's 143 — but the branch's whole architectural point, the Qt-free consent_verbs.py whose docstring says "the invariant sits in ONE place something without a seat can prove", is absent from master).

Understated: the status "mixed" and the blocker framing on the local-only cohort are, if anything, too mild. The loss-risk set is 15 branches, not 6, and there is a second unpushed commit they did not find. Their trajectory paragraph, by contrast, is the strongest part of the report — every weekly commit count (260/2/145/207/101), every PR-merge-by-day figure, and all ten merge-train squash shas reproduced exactly on re-run.

One grounding note they should have surfaced out loud: the orchestrator handed them "154 branches" and the repo has 153 refs (94 local + 59 remote). Their evidence line does the arithmetic correctly but the headline still argues against "154", so a wrong number survives into the sentence a reader remembers.

### Findings

#### [BLOCKER · load-bearing] Six `worktree-wf_*` branches from 2026-09-14 are LOCAL-ONLY (never pushed), absent from the 2026-09-15 closeout triage, and three of them carry work that exists nowhere else.

**Evidence.** `git rev-parse --verify -q origin/worktree-wf_061df37e-b74-3` (and the other five) all fail -> LOCAL-ONLY. None appears in `harness/notes/closeout-2026-09-15/verdicts.json` (16 rows, printed in full; branch names are design/rulings, feature/windows-installer, feature/tops-renderfarm, fix/cto-render, fix/demo-repairs, bp2/nits, rope/beacon, mem/m2-pgdrm, feat/scene-model, feat/panel-ux-pass, wip/panel-goalposts, repair/ledger-moneta-seam, repair/h1-schemas-b, stray/audit-log-threading, feat/cache-insert, rope/gate-a). None has an `archive/*` tag, though a prior generation (`archive/worktree-wf_f379ed45-0fb-1..5`) does.

**For the artist.** indirect — two of the six fix artist-visible panel and memory behavior (see next two findings).

#### [BLOCKER · load-bearing] `worktree-wf_8a870870-1d5-3` holds a complete agent.usd schema-migration path that master does not have at all: `_upgrade_agent_usd()` in scene_memory.py plus a 5-test `TestLegacyAgentUsdUpgrade` class.

**Evidence.** `grep -rn "_upgrade_agent_usd" python/ tests/` on master -> no output. `grep -rn "TestLegacyAgentUsdUpgrade" tests/` -> no output. `git diff master worktree-wf_8a870870-1d5-3 -- python/synapse/memory/scene_memory.py | grep -E '^\+.*(def |LEGACY|upgrade)'` -> `+def _upgrade_agent_usd(agent_usd: str) -> str:`, `+        agent_usd_schema = _upgrade_agent_usd(agent_usd)`, plus log lines `"agent.usd upgraded to schema %s: %s (backup: %s)"`. Branch tip 42ae6899 2026-09-14 "fix(memory): upgrade a legacy agent.usd instead of dropping its decisions". Tests include `test_decision_written_against_legacy_store_survives`, `test_upgrade_happens_and_is_additive`, `test_unmigratable_store_reads_unknown_not_success`.

**For the artist.** An artist opening a scene whose agent.usd was written under the older schema currently has no migration path on master — the branch's own commit subject says decisions get dropped instead of upgraded. CLAUDE.md's status table advertises "agent.usd Schema: Built (SCHEMA_VERSION 2.0.0)" with no mention of what happens to 0.1.0 stores.

#### [BLOCKER · load-bearing] `worktree-wf_51b5f8aa-295-1` holds a 25-test, 448-line suite pinning panel state-sentence honesty (`tests/test_panel_header_sentence_floor.py`) that is absent from master with no equivalent.

Test names include `test_server_status_string_cannot_clip_the_sentence`, `test_an_honest_error_status_keeps_the_shape_the_crucible_confirmed`, `test_a_failed_call_releases_the_held_sentence_too`, `test_emergency_halt_reads_differently_from_ordinary_work`. Branch tip de325abd 2026-09-14 "fix(panel): stop the rail reporting success over a failed cancel". A second branch, worktree-wf_061df37e-b74-3, holds a smaller 183-line version of the same file.

#### [INFO · load-bearing] The "154 branches" figure overstates merge debt by roughly 3.5x. 49 local branches are unmerged by ancestry, but 35 of those already have their content on master (28 via squash-merged PRs or patch-id match, 7 as superseded ancestors). Only 14 carry content master lacks.

### What the surveyor did not look at inside its own dimension

- The two branches in their own six-branch cohort that they never examined: worktree-wf_e54942e8-c4a-4 and -c4a-5 (tips 93c41007 and 5d162d4b, both 2026-09-14, both NOT-ANCESTOR, both local-only and untagged). c4a-5 carries a 152-line producer `harness/v2-20260913/artifacts/v2-baseline/export_baseline_csv.py` plus baseline.csv and manifest.json, two of three absent from master. A producer for an evidence CSV is exactly the artifact class this project has ruled load-bearing, and it exists on this machine only.
- The second unpushed commit. They framed b7592a9a as the one thing not on GitHub. `git rev-list --left-right --count origin/$b...$b` across all 94 local branches finds a second: mem/m2-pgdrm is +1 ahead of origin with `6df21262 fix(pgdrm): commit the M2 kernel repair that sat uncommitted in the worktree` — an unpushed repair on the lineage behind the only open PR.
- The archive-tag safety net was never checked against the loss-risk set. Resolving every `archive/*` tag to a commit sha and comparing against each unmerged branch tip shows 15 branches that are simultaneously local-only, ancestrally unmerged, and untagged: the six worktree-wf_* from 09-14 plus feature/tops-renderfarm-20260907, fix/demo-repairs-20260909, pr106 and rebase/m2-pgdrm. Their report names six.
- Four registered worktrees: .claude/worktrees/bp3-probe, bp3-tidy, bp4-b7fix, and C:/Users/User/synapse-m2-pgdrm-wt. The first three hold untracked binary evidence in no commit (b6_wl_component.usdc, b7_wl_fixture.exr, synapse_blueprint_probes.hip, and an entire untracked harness/fixtures/ tree under bp3-probe). The fourth is the second non-OneDrive worktree outside the repo and sits on the open PR's branch family.
- A second worktree with staged, never-committed work: .claude/worktrees/wf_8d5eccfe-bc2-4 has 1 file / 9 insertions / 19 deletions staged against harness/notes/PROBE_2026-09-15_subagent_inheritance.md. They reported only bc2-3's staged delete, so a reader following their prune advice loses this one silently.
- The `survived` flag in verdicts.json. Every row carries it (feat/scene-model survived=False, stray/audit-log-threading survived=True) — it records whether the closeout's own refuter upheld the verdict. A dimension about disposition follow-through should have used it; a verdict that did not survive its own refuter is weaker evidence than one that did, and they treated all 16 rows as equally authoritative.
- Whether master's four `test (os, py)` required contexts actually ran on the seven PR-less commits. They cited CLOSEOUT.md's explanation of why bypass is possible (enforce_admins: false) but never queried the check-run status of those seven shas, so 'skipped the CI gate' is inferred from the absence of a PR, not measured. `gh api repos/.../commits/<sha>/check-runs` would have settled it.
- The three OneDrive worktrees' ` T` typechange rows are all under `harness/solaris_v3/runs/20260905T.../pure/tmp/` — meaning committed paths in that tree are tracked as one type and present on disk as another (symlink/regular mismatch). They labelled it 'generated-file typechange noise' and moved on without asking whether harness run artifacts should be tracked at all; it is the same shape as the pytest-pollutes-the-production-log finding already in this project's memory.

### Open questions

- Which of the six local-only worktree-wf_* branches should be kept? worktree-wf_061df37e-b74-4 is demonstrably superseded (master's gate_widget.py:219-236,333,423,458 implements the REVIEW-keeps-REVERT rule the branch's consent_verbs.py encodes, and tests/panel/test_consent_way_back.py on master is larger than the branch's), but 8a870870 (agent.usd upgrade) and 51b5f8aa (panel sentence-floor suite) have no master equivalent. Nobody has ruled on them.
- worktree-wf_061df37e-b74-3 and worktree-wf_51b5f8aa-295-1 both create tests/test_panel_header_sentence_floor.py at 183 and 448 lines respectively. UNKNOWN whether these are two attempts at the same goal or complementary halves — I did not diff the two versions against each other.
- For worktree-wf_8a870870-1d5-3: UNKNOWN how many real agent.usd stores are on the 0.1.0 schema today. The live Moneta snapshot was out of scope for this dimension and I did not read it. Without that count the urgency of the migration path is unknown.
- Is b7592a9a unpushed deliberately (held pending a check) or by omission? The commit body says the tooling "has been living untracked" and was committed "rather than re-derived next session", which reads like an end-of-session save, not a staged hold.
- docs/store-incident-2026-09-17: which VERDICT.md is authoritative — master's (from PR #107) or the branch's later-authored but master-behind draft? The branch commit subject says "the audit that says it is not solved", which suggests it carries a conclusion master's copy may not.
- The closeout gave `feature/tops-renderfarm-20260907` SUPERSEDED and I confirmed all 42 of its files are on master — but its OneDrive worktree is still registered and still checked out on that branch. UNKNOWN whether it is being kept for a reason or was simply missed in the 18-worktree prune.
- I did not verify whether the 09-15 closeout's own follow-up items (the v5.72.1 abandonment, the `_MCPLocalClient.available` superseding test that CTO_RULINGS_04 RULING 1 requires and that CLOSEOUT.md says "does not exist and has no owner") have moved since. Those are tracked debt outside the branch graph.

## known-defects — MIXED

> SYNAPSE's defect-recording discipline is real and unusually honest at the code-comment level, but the ledger is scattered across dated audits, forensic notes and in-code prose with no single index — and three of the six named defects are materially mis-stated at HEAD (one understates blast radius by 13 verbs, one has a false-green guard, one describes a mechanism that no longer exists), while the artist-facing README discloses none of them.

**Trajectory.** Improving where the work is, frozen where it is quarantined. The marshal/freeze class has been actively repaired over the window: b9eca08e (2026-06-26) routed MCP+PDG dispatch through run_on_main, d625ee61 (2026-08-14) added the fast-path-2 in-flight register, d318ab7a (2026-08-14) wired the F3 emergency net, a2af501b (2026-09-15) shipped the Undo Receipt, ae34ed96 (2026-09-17) closed the memory dual-write and disarmed the unasked prune. Against that, `git log --since=2026-08-07 -- python/synapse/server/handlers_tops/` returns exactly ONE commit (d625ee61) and it is instrumentation, not a fix — the TOPS defect is deliberately quarantined (tests/test_d_track.py:40 `tops_path_untouched`). Net: the defects being worked on are shrinking; the ledger describing them is drifting. 835 commits since 2026-08-07.

**The refuter on where this report overstates itself.** Yes, in four specific places, though the report is well above the median for this kind of work and most of its evidence survived hostile re-checking.

The STATUS ("mixed") is right and if anything generous to itself — I'd hold it. The TRAJECTORY paragraph is fully reproducible: all five cited repair commits resolve with the dates and subjects given, `git log --since=2026-08-07 -- handlers_tops/` returns exactly one commit (d625ee61, instrumentation), and 835 commits since 2026-08-07 is exact.

The HEADLINE is where confidence outruns evidence. "Three of the six named defects are materially mis-stated" is defensible, but "the ledger is scattered ... with no single index" is stated as established fact on the strength of an `ls docs/` grep. harness/cto/BACKLOG.json is a 35-item structured defect ledger with severity, evidence, repro, gate and closure_predicate fields, 23 of them open — and VERDICT.md, a document they read and quoted, cites it by line. Publishing "there is NO canonical defect ledger" as the report's one structural finding, on a search that could not have found it, is the single largest overclaim here.

Three smaller ones. (1) Finding 4 — "the false-green is NOT a systemic class ... the dirtyAllTasks fingerprint is the single stale one" — is a negative claim proved by checking three literals in other functions while never checking the second literal inside the function they had just flagged. That second limb also matches nothing, so check_farm_headless cannot go red at all. The conclusion happens to survive (limb two is stale because the defect was genuinely fixed) but they could not have known that, and the gate is wider open than the report says. (2) Finding 5's grep — "returns exactly one hit" — is not reproducible from the command as written; it returns two. In a repo whose stated law is that every number carries a producer path, the finding's whole evidentiary basis is a miscounted grep. (3) Finding 7's "the README describes the WRONG half of the gap" and "an artist ... believes background renders are the unstoppable ones" overreads a row that is scoped to Emergency halt and that explicitly tells the artist background renders can be stopped deliberately. The defect is omission of the foreground gap, which is bad enough without the inversion framing.

One structural note on severities: three findings are marked "blocker" and none of the three is a code defect that blocks anything shipping — they are a vacuous check, a documentation omission, and a prompt/corpus conflict. All three are real and worth fixing; "blocker" applied to a doc gap and a stale substring dilutes the word against finding 3's actual data-loss path.

### Findings

#### [BLOCKER · load-bearing] Defect (2) PDG dirtyAllTasks signature mismatch is STILL PRESENT at HEAD, and the harness check built to catch it is a FALSE GREEN — its literal fingerprint matches zero lines of the file it inspects.

**Evidence.** shared/bridge.py:2377 — `lambda: top_node.dirtyAllTasks(remove_files=remove_files)`. The live 22.0.400 signature is `dirtyAllTasks(self, remove_outputs)` (repo's own probe, harness/notes/h22_compat_ledger.json:197, settled by inspect.Signature.bind; corroborated by h22_compat_ledger_v2.json:102 reading the build-shipped hom.zip, incl. the deprecation in favour of hou.TopNode.dirtyAllWorkItems). Both symbols exist in the 22.0.400 table: `python -c` over python/synapse/cognitive/tools/data/h22_symbol_table.json (houdini_version 22.0.400, symbol_count 36472) prints hou.TopNode.dirtyAllTasks and hou.TopNode.dirtyAllWorkItems. THE FALSE GREEN: harness/verify/checks.py:1785 tests `if bridge_src and "dirtyAllTasks(remove_files=True)" in bridge_src:`; `grep -c 'dirtyAllTasks(remove_files=True)' shared/bridge.py` -> **0**, so check_farm_headless falls through to :1796 `return {"ok": True, "detail": "no reachable dirtyAllTasks(remove_files=True)..."}` while the defect is live.

**For the artist.** Real but narrow. When a farm/TOPS cook fails through the /mcp bridge, the rollback raises TypeError on every invocation. The failure is now reported honestly (bridge.py:2382-2387 emits "TASKS NOT DIRTIED — a retry may recook against stale work items"), which is a genuine improvement over the pre-v5.35.0 silent swallow. But an artist who retries still recooks against undirtied work items and silently reuses bad caches. The compounding harm is the false green: a studio-readiness roll-up reports this surface clean.

#### [BLOCKER · load-bearing] Defect (5) "no API cancels an in-flight ROP render" is PARTLY REFUTED and partly still true, and the README describes the WRONG half of the gap. rps/rkill works for background renders; foreground renders are unreachable by any control. README's stop table names only the recoverable gap.

**Evidence.** python/synapse/server/render_stop.py:5-12 — hou.RopNode has no cancel verb and hou.ActiveRender is absent at runtime, but hscript rps/rkill exist and work (PID-selective, verified live 22.0.368, producer harness/notes/h3b/rkill_probe_evidence.json). render_stop.py:74-76: "A **foreground** render (soho_foreground=1, or any in-process RopNode.render() that blocks the main thread) never becomes a background process, never appears in rps, and cannot be reached from here." README.md:82-85 lists the Emergency halt limit as "Doesn't stop background renders. They are reported back so you can stop them deliberately" — the inverse of the real hazard. `grep -n -iE 'unresponsive|freez|foreground render|blocks the UI' docs/getting-started/ docs/releases/v5.75.1.md` -> no matches.

**For the artist.** An artist reading the README believes background renders are the unstoppable ones. In fact background renders are the ONLY stoppable ones, and a foreground render — which the panel's own system prompt tells the model to produce — cannot be stopped by Stop, Cancel cook, Emergency halt, or synapse_render_stop. Separately undisclosed: render_stop.py:52-63 records that a killed mantra render leaves a structurally valid, pixel-free 1,015-byte EXR at the declared output path that passes any exists-and-parses check.

#### [BLOCKER · load-bearing] UNRECORDED AS A DEFECT: the panel's live system prompt instructs the model to set soho_foreground=1 — the exact render mode that freezes the GUI unbounded and is unreachable by every stop control — and the repo contains three mutually contradictory teachings on this parameter.

**Evidence.** python/synapse/panel/system_prompt.py:184 `- Set soho_foreground=1 on usdrender ROP for synchronous file write.` and python/synapse/panel/agent_prompts.py:65 the same. Contradicted by harness/notes/forensic/s1_artifacts/mcp_surface_probe.json:501 (another live prompt head): "Never use soho_foreground=1 for heavy scenes -- it blocks the WebSocket server." And by python/synapse/core/render_presets.py:10 which defaults to background. And docs/training/karma_xpu_solaris_production_setups.md:247 teaches the inversion outright: "Render hangs | soho_foreground=0 | Set soho_foreground=1". The conflict IS noted at harness/notes/forensic/S2_PREMORTEM.md:330 and :907 — a forensic premortem, not a defect ledger — and the prompt line is unchanged at HEAD.

**For the artist.** Direct. Every panel turn that renders is steered toward the one mode that freezes Houdini for the render's duration and that no stop control can reach. The foreground_guard refusal at handlers_render.py:478 is the only thing standing between the artist and an unbounded GUI freeze, and it gates on resolution/samples/OptiX-cache warmth — not on the prompt's instruction.

#### [BLOCKER · load-bearing] There is NO canonical defect ledger. Every known defect I verified lives in a different artifact type, and none is indexed. This is the structural finding behind "documented where someone would find it."

**Evidence.** `ls docs/ | grep -iE 'defect|known|issue|ledger|limitation'` -> only RFC_agent_usd_ledger.md, SCIENCE_HARNESS_LEDGER.md, verification_ledger.md — none is a defect index. The six defects are scattered: (1) docs/HANDOFF_MEMORY_DUAL_WRITE.md:171 (a handoff brief) + tools/hy_verbs_baseline.json (a frozen test baseline); (2) docs/reviews/synapse-review-2026-08-01.md:344 (a dated one-off audit) + harness/state/DECISIONS.md:101,:119 (two open rulings) + CLAUDE.md §1.7 (citing stale lines 1718/1723; actual 2377/2382); (3)+(4) in-code comments only; (5) render_stop.py module docstring; (6) docs/HANDOFF_MEMORY_DUAL_WRITE.md:226 under a heading titled "Decisions that are mine, not yours." `grep -n -iE 'known limitation|known defect|not fixed|limitation' README.md` -> **zero matches** in 219 lines.

**For the artist.** An artist or a new contributor has no single place to learn what is broken. This contradicts the project's own stated convention in CLAUDE.md: "Every known limitation is stated plainly rather than omitted - a document that hides a gap is worse than one that names it." docs/SYNAPSE_NEXT_SYSTEM_BLUEPRINT.md:909 already proposes the fix ("Mirror critical known defects into GitHub Issues with severity, affected release, and evidence links") and it has not been done.

#### [WATCH · load-bearing] Defect (1) TOPS dead `import hdefereval` is PRESENT at HEAD and its blast radius is 3x what the operator-facing record states. The record (docs/HANDOFF_MEMORY_DUAL_WRITE.md:171-176) says "Six tops_* verbs" and names two files. The real count is 20 handler bodies across five files plus the shared helper. Only 6 are pinned because the frozen baseline sweeps read-only verbs only.

**Evidence.** `for f in python/synapse/server/handlers_tops/*.py; do grep -c '^\s*import hdefereval\s*$' $f; done` -> cook.py:8, diagnostics.py:4, work_items.py:4, render_sequence.py:2, wedge.py:2, _common.py:1 = 21 sites. `grep -o 'reg.register("tops_[a-z_]*"' python/synapse/server/handlers.py` -> 19 tops verbs registered; `sed -n '207,240p' python/synapse/server/handlers.py` read-only set contains exactly 6 tops verbs. `python -c` on tools/hy_verbs_baseline.json -> tops_defect == ['tops_diagnose','tops_get_cook_stats','tops_get_dependency_graph','tops_get_work_items','tops_pipeline_status','tops_query_items'] — identical to the read-only 6. The other 13 tops verbs (tops_cook_node, tops_generate_items, tops_configure_scheduler, tops_cancel_cook, tops_pause_cook, tops_resume_cook, tops_dirty_node, tops_batch_cook, tops_cook_and_validate, tops_monitor_stream, tops_render_sequence, tops_multi_shot, tops_setup_wedge) plus `wedge` (handlers.py:710) carry the same dead import and are outside the sweep, so nothing pins them.

**For the artist.** None in a GUI Houdini session — `import hdefereval` succeeds there, and python/synapse/mcp/server.py:7 confirms the /mcp endpoint "runs INSIDE Houdini". The failure is total for any headless lane: hython CI, offscreen panel verification, and any farm/headless deployment lose all 19 TOPS verbs plus `wedge` on the first statement after the HOU_AVAILABLE check. Evidence of the live symptom: harness/notes/context_capability_21.json:402 `_StepFail: tops_cook_node: ... hdefereval is only available in a graphical Houdini`.

#### [WATCH · load-bearing] The (1) defect record is STALE about its own mechanism. docs/HANDOFF_MEMORY_DUAL_WRITE.md:173-176 says the shared marshal is "_common.py, wrapping hdefereval.executeInMainThreadWithResult - the pre-migration pattern" and that the six have "no timeout, no stall detector, no C4 zombie-kill, no C6 telemetry." At HEAD _common.py routes through run_on_main, which has all four. The only live residual is the dead import itself.

**Evidence.** python/synapse/server/handlers_tops/_common.py:81-82 — `from ..main_thread import run_on_main` / `result = run_on_main(func, timeout=effective_timeout, label="tops_common:_run_in_main_thread_pdg")`. The migration landed in b9eca08e (2026-06-26) "perf(resilience): route MCP + PDG main-thread dispatch through run_on_main (L8 min mitigation)", i.e. ~3 months before the record was written. `grep -rn 'hdefereval\.' python/synapse/server/handlers_tops/*.py` returns only _common.py:50 and :70 — both comment/docstring text, zero attribute access anywhere in handlers_tops.

**For the artist.** None directly. It matters because the record overstates the danger (implying unbounded main-thread holds that no longer exist) while understating the scope (6 vs 20), so anyone triaging from the record fixes the wrong thing. The stale docstring at _common.py:50 is separately recorded as doc-debt in harness/notes/receipts/W8-SLIFE.json:38 (L18) — "could mislead a maintainer into 'restoring' the blocking call."

#### [WATCH · load-bearing] Defect (3) render freeze is FIXED as a deadlock and PARTLY OPEN as a freeze. Zero live calls to the blocking marshal remain repo-wide, and the lint enforcing that is green at HEAD. But on two of three caller paths the timeout is structurally unenforceable and the GUI still freezes for the render's duration.

**Evidence.** `grep -rn 'executeInMainThreadWithResult(' --include=*.py python/ shared/ synapse/` returns exactly one hit, python/synapse/server/main_thread.py:6, which is docstring text. I ran `python -m pytest tests/test_marshal_lint.py -q` -> **4 passed** in 9.77s (line-scoped allowlist, no entry for the phantom). _handle_render now marshals via run_on_main at handlers_render.py:963-965 with _RENDER_MAIN_TIMEOUT_S. THE RESIDUAL, recorded in-code at handlers_render.py:91-119: on /mcp the outer 120s governs and fast path 1 discards the 3600s; on panel-inline the Qt slot IS main, run_on_main takes fast path 2 and "the payload runs inline with NO bound of any kind... The panel freezes for the render's duration." main_thread.py:506-509 confirms: "NO bounding is applied here... Any 'timeout' on this path would be a lie. Pure observation."

**For the artist.** The permanent unrecoverable deadlock is gone — that was the worst case. What remains: a panel-initiated render freezes the Houdini GUI for its full duration with no bound. Mitigations are real and wired: foreground_guard.assess_foreground_render is called at handlers_render.py:478 inside _handle_render_bounded (the verb actually registered at handlers.py:707), refusing cold-OptiX-cache XPU renders and over-budget pixel/sample combos.

#### [WATCH · load-bearing] An UNRECORDED-in-user-docs hazard sits on the /mcp render path: abandon-then-continue double-render. It is documented thoroughly in code and in dated audits, but appears in no release note, README, or getting-started doc.

**Evidence.** handlers_render.py:121-132 states it verbatim: "on a 200s frame the transport reports FAILURE while the render keeps going and writes the frame. An operator (or an agent) who retries then double-renders." Cause: C4's abandoned flag cannot stop a payload already inside fn(). `grep -rln 'abandon-then-continue|double-render'` over docs/ + README.md hits only docs/reviews/freeze-recon-full.json, docs/reviews/synapse-review-2026-08-01.md, docs/sprint_freeze/done_signoff.md, docs/sprint_freeze/gate_log.md — four internal audits, zero artist-facing surfaces. The code names a specific unclaimed fix at handlers_render.py:539-546 (register a render_session on the inline path) and declines it because tests/test_render_bounded.py::test_main_thread_caller_renders_inline pins `rs.summary() == []`.

**For the artist.** An agent-driven render longer than 120s on /mcp reports failure, retries, and renders the frame twice — burning a render slot and potentially racing two writers onto one output path. The recognisable symptom is named in-code: "a main-thread timeout error accompanied by a frame that appears on disk ~80s later."

#### [WATCH · load-bearing] UNRECORDED: the RAG corpus teaches a premise the repo's own live 22.0.400 probe REFUTED, and that premise is the sole justification the corpus gives for the foreground-render advice.

**Evidence.** rag/skills/houdini21-reference/karma_rendering_guide.md:596 — "**soho_foreground=0 (default) returns before the EXR is written.** Any script that reads the render output immediately after rop.render() will find no file. Always set soho_foreground=1 for scripted/MCP renders." Refuted by docs/RELEASE_NOTES_v5.49.0.md:26, citing producer harness/notes/probe_render_offload.results.20260815T123824Z.json item (b): "What does node.render() do with soho_foreground=0? **BLOCKS_UNTIL_PIXELS** — 1.84s return ~ husk wall time; background mode is NOT async dispatch on this build." `grep -rn 'BLOCKS_UNTIL_PIXELS'` across the whole tree returns only the release note and the two probe result files plus the probe script — nothing links the finding back to the corpus, and the corpus text is unchanged.

**For the artist.** The agent retrieves guidance built on a false premise and produces freezing, unstoppable renders for a reason that no longer applies on this build. This is the exact code/corpus-divergence class the project already knows about (fixing code is not enough; the corpus re-teaches it) applied to a new symbol. The corpus directory is also still named houdini21-reference while the runtime is 22.0.400.

#### [WATCH · load-bearing] Defect (6) "headless PDG has no timeout from any source" is CONFIRMED for the live /synapse handler path and REFUTED for the /mcp bridge path. The two paths were conflated in the record.

**Evidence.** LIVE PATH: python/synapse/server/handlers_tops/cook.py:66 calls `node.cook(block=bool(blocking))` — an unbounded blocking cook. _common.py's _PDG_DEFER_TIMEOUT (60.0, _common.py:42) bounds only the off-main dispatch wait; on a main-thread caller run_on_main takes fast path 2 and no bound exists (main_thread.py:506-509). The record at docs/HANDOFF_MEMORY_DUAL_WRITE.md:224-228 states the cause precisely: "_common.py's timeout is enforced at the WebSocket layer via _SLOW_COMMANDS, which does not exist when handlers are called in-process, so headless PDG currently has NO timeout from any source and tools/hy.ps1's watchdog is the only backstop." BRIDGE PATH: shared/bridge.py:2301-2311 bounds the cook with `cook_timeout = float(operation.kwargs.get("cook_timeout", DEFAULT_COOK_TIMEOUT_S))` and a deadline poll, pinned by tests/test_pdg_cook_timeout.py which asserts cancelCook(), cache-preserved dirty, delta_hash=='pdg_timeout', and the 1800s backstop constant.

**For the artist.** A TOPS cook driven through the live /synapse path from the panel can hang indefinitely with no automatic recovery. The bridge path used by /mcp is bounded and tested. The record's phrasing ("NO timeout from any source") reads as universal and is not.

#### [INFO · load-bearing] The (2) false-green is NOT a systemic class — I tested the neighbouring literal-substring checks in the same file and they all match live source. The dirtyAllTasks fingerprint is the single stale one.

**Evidence.** `grep -cF` against real source: 'OPERATION_GATES.get(self.operation_type, GateLevel.REVIEW)' in shared/bridge.py -> 1 (checks.py:1609 matches); 'consent_callback=lambda op: True' in shared/bridge.py -> 1 (checks.py:2250 matches); '_TOOL_TO_OPERATION.get(tool_name, "set_parameter")' in python/synapse/panel/bridge_adapter.py -> 1 (checks.py:2231 matches). Only 'dirtyAllTasks(remove_files=True)' returns 0.

#### [INFO] Defect (4) the H22 blocking-marshal self-deadlock class is CLOSED and well-defended — the strongest defect-handling in the repo.

**Evidence.** tests/test_marshal_lint.py: I ran it, 4 passed. It bans the blocking primitive AND the phantom hdefereval.executeInMainThread repo-wide with a line-scoped allowlist that itself has staleness tests (test_allowlists_are_documented_and_real, test_allowlist_is_line_scoped_not_file_scoped at :284/:336). The vendor defect is written up in full at handlers_render.py:65-76: _queueDeferred(block=True) parks in _condition.wait() with no thread check, notified only from _processDeferred() on the main thread. run_on_main's fast path 2 (main_thread.py:484) short-circuits it. #### [INFO · load-bearing] The memory-store incident recorded as NOT SOLVED on 2026-09-17 is materially CLOSED at HEAD and in the live store. I verified the primary directly rather than trusting either the verdict or the release note.

### Refuter verdicts

- **CONFIRMED** — Defect (1) TOPS dead `import hdefereval` is PRESENT at HEAD; real blast radius is 20 handler bodies across five files plus the shared helper, while the record says six verbs / two files. Only the 6 read-only verbs are pinned.
