### What the surveyor did not look at inside its own dimension

- harness/cto/BACKLOG.json — 35 structured defect items, 23 OPEN, 3 critical / 17 high, each with severity, evidence, repro, gate and closure_predicate. This is the live defect ledger for the dimension they were assigned, and they neither read it nor listed its open items. Their headline finding asserts it does not exist. harness/bastion/FINDINGS_INDEX.md is a second index, also unexamined.
- THE MASKING MECHANISM FOR DEFECT (1) — the most important thing missed. tests/test_tops.py:34-38 installs a fake hdefereval module into sys.modules (`_hdefereval.executeInMainThreadWithResult = lambda fn, *args, **kwargs: fn(*args, **kwargs)`) before handlers_tops is imported; tests/test_tops_assembly.py:16-18 does the same. The entire TOPS test surface therefore runs against a stub of the exact module whose absence IS the defect, and can never catch it. They wrote 'nothing pins them' — the truth is worse: the tests actively hide it for all 20 verbs. This is the isolated-green-that-a-composed-run-breaks class the brief named, sitting in their own finding.
- The producer half of the memory incident. VERDICT.md:38 marks `MemoryStore.add()` NOT SOLVED with owner 'Nobody'. They declared the incident closed without checking that row. It IS closed (store.py:697-760, the B3 id-collision guard), so they were right by luck rather than by measurement — and the stale-line problem they diagnose elsewhere applies here too: VERDICT cites store.py:529-553, the method now begins at :697.
- Eleven `memory.jsonl.degraded-load-*` sidecar files sitting in the live store directory alongside pre-repair and pre-backfill snapshots. That is eleven recorded degraded-load events, evidence the outage fired repeatedly rather than as one two-day window, and it goes unmentioned in a finding about that exact incident.
- That check_farm_headless is a `solo_hard` check (checks.py:1838) aggregated by check_studio_readiness_review (checks.py:1812). They asserted the roll-up consequence — 'a studio-readiness roll-up reports this surface clean' — without verifying the wiring that makes it true. It is true, and it is worse than stated: a hard gate that cannot return false.
- That the TOPS quarantine is a git-diff guard anchored at `merge-base(master, HEAD)` (checks.py:1481-1492). On master, where HEAD is master, the diff is empty and the check is vacuously green. Their open question treats it as actively blocking a 21-line deletion; it only bites on a branch, which changes what 'is the quarantine still serving a purpose' even means.
- Their own open question about the frozen baseline is wrong and a thirty-second check would have shown it. They report `b.get('build') or b.get('houdini_build')` -> None and ask whether the baseline should be build-stamped. It already is: tools/hy_verbs_baseline.json carries `frozen_from_build: 22.0.417` and `python: 3.13.10`. They checked two key names and concluded the field was absent. Same file also holds a `defect_note` object that is a second, wired copy of the stale record they flagged in the handoff doc — they cited only the doc.
- ./mcp_tools_render.py:13-14 — a live, wired, shipped contradiction of the soho_foreground guidance ('Never use soho_foreground=1 for heavy scenes -- it blocks the WebSocket server'), imported at mcp_server.py:491, published as synapse_group_render knowledge at mcp_server.py:1129, and mapped into the panel at tool_bridge.py:22. They sourced that contradiction to a forensic probe JSON instead, which reads as archival when it is actually running code.
- No systematic sweep for in-code defect markers. I ran one: zero TODO/FIXME/XXX/KNOWN DEFECT/NOT FIXED markers across python/synapse + shared excluding _vendor. That null result is itself a finding for this dimension — this project records defects exclusively in prose comments and dated audit artifacts, never in greppable markers, which is precisely why no index assembles itself and why their 'recorded nowhere' hunt had to be opportunistic. It belonged in the report as the mechanism behind the structural finding.
- Nothing was checked about whether any of the six has an entry in the project's actual issue tracker, despite the report's own remedy discussion turning on GitHub Issues (blueprint:909). The claim 'it has not been done' is asserted, never tested.
- The 22.0.400 re-check the dimension explicitly asked for was not actually performed for defects (2) and (5). Both rest on 22.0.368 artifacts. For (2) a real 22.0.400 probe exists and they missed it (harness/cto/runs/2026-09-05/forge_report.json); for (5) render_stop.py's rkill measurements are stamped 22.0.368 / 2026-07-28 and have had no 22.0.400 re-probe at all — an honest UNKNOWN they did not name.

### Open questions

- Should harness/verify/checks.py:1785 be repaired to an AST/regex match (`dirtyAllTasks\s*\(\s*remove_files\s*=`) before the kwarg rename, or together with it? Repairing the check first turns check_farm_headless red immediately and honestly; repairing the code first makes the check green for the wrong reason (fingerprint still matches nothing).
- Is the TOPS quarantine (tests/test_d_track.py:40 tops_path_untouched) still serving a purpose? It is blocking a 21-line deletion with no behavioural change — the imports are provably dead (`grep -rn 'hdefereval\.' handlers_tops/` returns only comments). If the quarantine's real subject is cook-truth semantics, the dead-import deletion may be separable.
- Which of the three soho_foreground teachings is correct for 22.0.400 and who owns reconciling them? The live probe says background is NOT async on this build, which removes the corpus's stated reason for preferring foreground — but nothing has re-derived what the right default now is.
- tools/hy_verbs_baseline.json carries no build field (`b.get('build') or b.get('houdini_build')` -> None) while the sweep runs on 22.0.417 and the GUI runs 22.0.400. Should the baseline be build-stamped so a major-version move invalidates it loudly?
- Does the README's 'Three ways to stop' table get corrected to name the foreground-render gap and the mantra valid-and-empty-EXR hazard, or does that belong in a new KNOWN_LIMITATIONS.md that the README links to? docs/SYNAPSE_NEXT_SYSTEM_BLUEPRINT.md:909 proposes GitHub Issues; that does not serve an artist reading the README.

## product-readiness — MIXED

> SYNAPSE is a genuinely working scene-inspection and network-building assistant wrapped in an honest product, but three of the five artist paths break today: the panel's chat worker cannot start or stop a render at all, the "safe" render guard that prevents UI lockup is unreachable through its own tool schema, and the phantom-API scout that the whole build is supposed to lean on is hard-down on the MCP path.

**Trajectory.** Regressing on artist paths, improving on memory integrity. 835 commits since 2026-08-07 (`git log --since=2026-08-07 --oneline | wc -l`); 238 of them (28.5%) are docs/chore/harness/capsule/design by subject prefix, while only 31 mention render, recipe or install and only ~6 change render behavior (0124f5c4 karma progressive presets 2026-08-12, 48667545 + da1fda71 F5a offload probes 2026-08-14/15, b3e24d14 probe fix 2026-09-03, 1d982426 durable TOPs render jobs + artist render workspace 2026-09-15). The one real artist-facing render feature (#85) landed three days ago and has no production mileage. Memory reliability is the bright spot and is genuinely improving: ae34ed96 fixed the duplicate-deposit bug that degraded the store, 53e4f9cb/43ef30ca shipped the health surfaces, and `fix(memory)` appears 11 times in the window. But the same window shows the recipe path untouched since the v3/v4 decisions were written into memory on 2026-09-06, and the scout regression is live right now.

**The refuter on where this report overstates itself.** Partly. The headline is the strongest part of the report and it survives: I personally reproduced all three of the named breaks — the dead 512 guard, the 40-tool render denial (including the interactive panel, which constructs the worker with enforce_worker_policy=True on purpose), and a live scout call returning the SQLite cross-thread error. "Mixed" is a fair status; if anything it is generous to the product.

Three things are more confident than the evidence supports.

First, "Regressing on artist paths" is not established. They produced no before/after for any of the three breaks. The 512 guard has no reachable-then-unreachable history in their evidence, the recipe triggers were never wired to the taught phrasings, and they never bisected the scout SQLite bug. Those are longstanding gaps, not regressions, and the trajectory field asserts a direction the commit archaeology does not demonstrate. Their supporting commit counts are also loose: 835 total reproduces exactly, "31 mention render, recipe or install" reproduces exactly, but I count 222 docs/chore/harness/capsule/design-prefixed commits (26.6%), not 238 (28.5%), and their list of ~6 render-behavior commits omits a2af501b (2026-09-15, Undo Receipt), which touches handlers_render.py inside their own window.

Second, the severity and loadBearing labels cannot discriminate. Eleven of fourteen findings are "blocker" and thirteen of fourteen are loadBearing:true. A stale docs page and an unreachable resolution guard are not the same weight, and a scheme that calls both a blocker is not ranking anything. The brief asked me to spot-check three loadBearing:false findings; exactly one exists, so the request could not be honoured as written — that is itself the finding.

Third, one supporting line is an abstention printed as a pass. "Actual CI is healthy — gh run list shows master green at 2026-09-17T22:54Z" describes a run against aa9624e5, the parent of HEAD. HEAD b7592a9a does not appear in the last ten CI runs. Nothing has tested the commit under review, and the report reads as though something had.

Two smaller overreaches: "roughly 2 rows per tool call" is ~1 per call when measured cleanly three times in a row, and "degraded 11 separate times" is 11 quarantine retries against one corrupted file (identical size, identical mtime), not 11 incidents. The ~60% estimate is explicitly labelled an estimate and I have no quarrel with the honesty of that framing — only with the fact that their own denial list contradicts the Solaris-builders component of it.

### Findings

#### [BLOCKER · load-bearing] synapse_safe_render's advertised lockup guard is unreachable: the guard reads width/height from the caller's payload, but the registered tool schema exposes only rop_path and soho_foreground, so effective_w/effective_h are always 0 and the `> 512` branch can never fire through MCP.

**Evidence.** python/synapse/server/handlers_render.py:2074-2076 (`width = resolve_param_with_default(payload, "width", None)`) and :2212-2219 (`effective_w = int(width) if width else 0` … `if (effective_w > 512 or effective_h > 512) and user_foreground is None`). Schema at python/synapse/mcp/_tool_registry.py:1456-1463 has properties {rop_path, soho_foreground} only. Verified by running `python -c` over TOOL_JSON['synapse_safe_render']: `props: ['rop_path', 'soho_foreground']`. python/synapse/core/aliases.py:255-258 confirms resolve_param_with_default returns the default when the key is absent. Description string still reads "Auto-forces background mode for high-resolution renders to prevent Houdini lockup."

**For the artist.** An artist asks for a "safe render" of a 1920x1080 shot. The pre-flight checks run and pass, then the render goes foreground on Houdini's main thread with a 3600-second timeout (handlers_render.py:138 `_RENDER_MAIN_TIMEOUT_S = 3600.0`). The UI is frozen for the duration and, because render_stop also needs the main thread, nothing can cancel it.

#### [BLOCKER · load-bearing] The panel's chat worker cannot start or stop a render in the shipped default mode. 40 of 137 tools are denied, and that set includes every render-initiating and render-stopping tool.

**Evidence.** Measured: `is_tool_allowed_for_worker(name, profile=mode)` over all TOOL_DEFS gives standard 97/137, strict 45/137, proposal 45/137. Default is standard (python/synapse/panel/worker_policy.py:55 `_DEFAULT_MODE = _MODE_STANDARD`). Denied in standard includes houdini_render, synapse_safe_render, synapse_render_sequence, synapse_render_progressively, synapse_autonomous_render, synapse_render_stop, synapse_farm_prepare, synapse_farm_submit, synapse_farm_cancel, synapse_render_farm_cancel, tops_render_sequence, houdini_shot_render_ready. Enforced at python/synapse/panel/claude_worker.py:402 and filtered out of the advertised list at python/synapse/panel/tool_bridge.py:113.

**For the artist.** "Render this" in the panel chat gets a denial, not a render. The artist must instead click the Render button (python/synapse/panel/synapse_panel.py:2551 → render_workspace.py:538 `synapse_farm_submit`), which is a separate dialog that landed 2026-09-15 and requires a saved hip. The conversational render path advertised by the product does not exist.

#### [BLOCKER · load-bearing] synapse_scout — named in CLAUDE.md §11 rule 15 as "the front-line defense against SYNAPSE's #1 failure class (phantom APIs)" — returns a hard error on every call through the live MCP bridge.

**Evidence.** Called twice against the live server; both returned `{"error":"ProgrammingError","message":"SQLite objects created in a thread can only be used in that same thread. The object was created in thread id 42800 and this is thread id 73532."}`. Cause: python/synapse/cognitive/tools/scout.py:329 `con = sqlite3.connect(str(db_path))` (default check_same_thread=True) cached in the module-level `_FTS[key]` dict at :345, while mcp_server.py:757-758 dispatches scout via `asyncio.to_thread`, whose pool rotates threads between calls.

**For the artist.** Indirect but real. The tool that is supposed to stop the model emitting APIs that do not exist in H22 returns nothing, so the model falls back to its own priors. Phantom-API failures are the documented #1 failure class.

#### [BLOCKER · load-bearing] On the /mcp path scout resolves the H21 symbol table, not H22, so even when it works its existence verdicts are for the wrong build. In-Houdini the table is correctly H22 — this is path-specific, not global.

**Evidence.** Ran `python -c` over synapse.cognitive.tools.scout outside Houdini: `running_major -> ''`, `pkg table -> h21_symbol_table.json`, `pkg table stamped version -> 21.0.671`, `EXPECTED_HOUDINI_VERSION env -> None`. Fallback is explicit at scout.py:651-663. mcp_server.py:741-744 only sets EXPECTED_HOUDINI_VERSION inside a `try: import hou` that fails in the bridge process. Contrast: live `synapse_doctor` reports `symbol_table` ok — "stamp 22.0.400 (36472 symbols …) == running 22.0.400". h22_symbol_table.json exists unused beside h21 in python/synapse/cognitive/tools/data/. The tool's own description also hardcodes "Houdini 21.0.671" three times (scout.py:1103,1106).

**For the artist.** None directly while the tool is crashing. Once the SQLite bug is fixed, an MCP-path scout would confidently verdict H22 symbols against an H21 table.

#### [BLOCKER · load-bearing] No recipe in the 62-recipe registry matches "create a solaris network" in any casing, and every trigger is an anchored ^…$ regex that misses ordinary artist phrasing.

**Evidence.** Ran the registry's own triggers against candidate phrasings: 'create a solaris network' -> None, 'create a simple solaris network' -> None, 'Create a Solaris Network' -> None, 'can you set up three point lighting' -> None, 'make me a three point lighting rig' -> None. Only exact forms hit: 'setup three point lighting' -> three_point_lighting, 'set up a lookdev scene' -> lookdev_scene. Registry loaded from synapse.routing.recipes.RecipeRegistry, 62 recipes; triggers are anchored patterns like `^(?:set up|setup|create)\s+(?:a\s+)?lookdev(?:\s+scene)?$`.

**For the artist.** The exact phrase the owner spent four rounds teaching SYNAPSE (v1 through v4, dated 2026-09-06) hits nothing in the recipe tier. The request falls through to the LLM tier, which composes from memory — non-deterministic, and different every session.

#### [BLOCKER · load-bearing] The four "Create a Solaris Network" recipe versions are split across three memory substrates, and the newest one is invisible to semantic recall. v4 exists only in a markdown file; v1–v3 exist only in the Moneta vector store.

**Evidence.** `synapse_context` returned v1/v2/v3/v4 under scene_memory Scene Decisions. Streaming the Moneta snapshot (C:/Users/User/AppData/Local/Temp/houdini_temp/untitled/.synapse/.moneta/snapshot.json, 1144 rows) for 'v4 recipe'/'SUPERSEDES v3'/'lookdev rig' returned 0 hits; v1/v2/v3 are present (7 rows mention the trigger phrase). grep for 'lookdev rig' across the .synapse dir found it only in C:/Users/User/AppData/Local/Temp/houdini_temp/untitled/claude/memory.md. The store's own context.md (357 bytes) and decisions.md (114 bytes) contain neither.

**For the artist.** Which network you get depends on which retrieval path the model happens to take that turn. synapse_recall over Moneta can only return the superseded v3; only a full synapse_context dump surfaces v4. The artist taught it four times and the system has no single answer.

#### [BLOCKER · load-bearing] synapse_knowledge_lookup returns uncapped raw parameter dumps — 91,373 bytes (~23k tokens) for the single query "karma xpu" — and this is a documented design choice, not a bug.

**Evidence.** Measured live over ws://127.0.0.1:9999/synapse: knowledge_lookup 91,373 bytes, context 24,531 bytes, list_recipes 30,171 bytes, recall 4,035 bytes, get_scene_info 205 bytes, get_stage_info 231 bytes (stage was empty). Handler docstring at python/synapse/server/handlers.py:1936-1937: "A node answer carries the full `parameters` surface (internal names + channels, uncapped - Target 5)". The synapse_recall I ran for "how do I set up a Karma XPU render in Solaris" returned the 233-parameter karma node help verbatim and answered nothing.

**For the artist.** One knowledge question costs ~12% of a 200k context window and would overflow most local Ollama models outright — and the README advertises Ollama as a first-class option. The answer is a parameter list, not an answer.

#### [BLOCKER · load-bearing] The memory store is 99% machine telemetry. Of 1,144 rows, 11 are artist-authored content and 2 of those are empty strings.

**Evidence.** Streamed the snapshot with a classifying script: 529 rows (46.2%) are loop-outcome telemetry ('<handler>: HIT/MISS/UNRESOLVABLE'), 256 (22.4%) are 'AI session started', 196 (17.1%) are 'Executed: <cmd>', 152 (13.3%) are 'Session summary', leaving 11 (1.0%) OTHER — of which the real content is one scene audit, four recipe-template records, three decision records and 'Fixed Solaris and Copernicus lookdev setup'.

**For the artist.** Recall searches 1,144 vectors to find 11 real memories. My live recall for a Karma question returned count:1 and it came from the RAG corpus, not from anything the artist stored.

#### [BLOCKER · load-bearing] The store grows on read-only calls — roughly 2 rows per tool call — and at the observed rate will cross the 2,000-record migration budget in about two weeks, at which point the first save of an unsaved scene will silently fail to carry memory forward.

**Evidence.** Observed within ~10 minutes of read-only calls today: synapse_health 1133 → synapse_memory_status 1135 → synapse_context 1137 → synapse_memory_status 1143 → synapse_doctor 1152. Snapshot spans 2026-09-01 to 2026-09-18 for 1,144 rows ≈ 67 rows/day. Budget: python/synapse/host/memory_lifecycle.py:25-26 `_MAX_RECORDS = 2000`, `_MAX_BYTES = 8 * 1024 * 1024`; exceeded raises `RuntimeError("Memory migration exceeds the bounded record/byte budget")` in rebind_owner (~:295). Save-time carry is memory_lifecycle.py:370-373 `if effective_cause == "save" and previous is not None: … if previous.unsaved: records = _records(owner)`. The exception is caught in _handle_event (:112-120), which logs and sets `_memory_binding_error` rather than surfacing to the artist. README states the automatic prune is opt-in and defaults off; doctor reports 537 of 1,152 entities protected.

**For the artist.** Work unsaved for a while, teach SYNAPSE some decisions, then save as shot_010.hip — and everything it learned stays behind in the temp directory with only a log line to say so.

#### [BLOCKER · load-bearing] A foreground render cannot be cancelled by any shipped control, because every stop path needs the main thread the render is holding.

**Evidence.** node.render() runs inside run_on_main with `timeout=_RENDER_MAIN_TIMEOUT_S` = 3600.0 (handlers_render.py:138, :905-907, :961-965). _handle_render_stop dispatches through `run_on_main(lambda: _rs.stop_render(...), timeout=30.0)` (:1738-1741) and its own description scopes it to "ONE in-flight BACKGROUND render (hscript rkill)". _handle_emergency_halt's docstring (:1756-1783) states, measured on 22.0.368, that the shipped halt walks /obj only, returned ALL_OPERATIONS_HALTED in 0.0s while the cook kept running, and deliberately does not kill background renders. README's "Three ways to stop" table documents all of this.

**For the artist.** Once a foreground render starts, the artist waits it out or kills Houdini. The docs are honest about it, which is worth something, but honesty is not a control.

#### [WATCH · load-bearing] For an unsaved scene the memory store lives under Houdini's temp directory, and this store has gone into refuse-writes degraded mode 11 separate times on this machine.

**Evidence.** Live store path from synapse_health and synapse_doctor: C:\\Users\\User\\AppData\\Local\\Temp\\houdini_temp\\untitled\\.synapse. Derivation at python/synapse/memory/store.py:1162-1194 (hip_is_unsaved by basename) and :1205-1214 ("Stores previously written next to the process working directory … are NOT carried over"). Directory listing shows 11 × memory.jsonl.degraded-load-<ts> (1789506731 … 1789674135) plus memory.jsonl.pre-repair-1789674797 and memory.jsonl.pre-backfill-1789681905. Degraded load sets `_degraded_load = True` and quarantines (store.py:452-459) with "DEGRADED LOAD … Refusing writes". synapse_doctor confirms: `degraded_quarantine_count: 11`.

**For the artist.** Recovery required scripts/primary_repair.py — a CLI tool no artist would know to run. The README is candid that 5.75.1 "does not repair a store that is already degraded"; it ships the recovery tools instead.

#### [WATCH · load-bearing] The installer shipped for the current release was never install-qualified against a running Houdini, and the version drift detector is currently red on this machine.

**Evidence.** git show aa9624e5: "installer-verification.json is NOT shipped, and v5.74.0 did ship it. That file is a Houdini install qualification — an actual probe of the installed product against a running Houdini. No such run was performed for this build … it has not been install-qualified." Live synapse_doctor returns 1 fail of 14 checks: `version` — "synapse 5.75.1 / protocol 4.0.0; install stamp says 5.67.4 — installed tree and stamp disagree". C:/Users/User/.synapse/install_stamp.json records synapse_version 5.67.4 installed 2026-09-09 from a OneDrive demo-capture tree.

**For the artist.** Nobody has run the fresh-machine install for 5.75.1 end to end. The one machine that has a stamp reports drift. What a clean user hits is genuinely unknown.

#### [WATCH] docs/status.md — linked from the README as "Current status" — describes v5.67.3/v5.67.4 and is eight releases stale.

**Evidence.** docs/status.md lines 8-22 cite "the v5.67.3 reliability release", "v5.67.4 updates documentation", and test figures of 8,614 passed / 415 skipped. VERSION reads 5.75.1; latest tag v5.75.1. README line 156 links it as "Current status". Actual CI is healthy — `gh run list --limit 5` shows master green at 2026-09-17T22:54Z with one failure earlier that day.

**For the artist.** The artist clicking "Current status" to find out what works reads a page about a version they are not running.

#### [WATCH · load-bearing] docs/studio/DEPLOYMENT.md sells a multi-user studio mode that disables all 7 farm tools and grants every authenticated 'artist' ungated arbitrary Python execution in the host Houdini.

**Evidence.** docs/studio/DEPLOYMENT.md lines 6-12 document studio-lan and studio-vpn modes with per-artist roles. python/synapse/server/handlers_farm.py:17-19 `_require_local_deployment()` raises unless SYNAPSE_DEPLOY_MODE == "local", and is called by farm_submit/prepare/cancel/job/jobs/capabilities/inspect. python/synapse/server/rbac.py:63 places "execute_python", "execute_vex" in _ARTIST_COMMANDS; ROLE_DENIALS at :123-125 denies 'artist' only server_config/manage_users/reload_config. Enforcement is python/synapse/server/websocket.py:679. CLAUDE.md §1.2 states the live path runs these with full __builtins__, no consent, no import filter, and calls a handler-layer gate "a prerequisite for any multi-user/studio deployment (D1-a)".

**For the artist.** A studio that follows this guide turns on a mode where the render tooling refuses to run and any artist can execute arbitrary code inside a colleague's session.

#### [INFO · load-bearing] Tool reachability is not the gap — all 137 registered tools have live handlers. The surface is 147 advertised (137 registry + 6 group-knowledge + inspector + scout + 2 BLOCKS), and scene-state reads are fast and cheap.

**Evidence.** len(TOOL_DEFS) == 137 and PENDING_TOOL_DEFS == 0 (imported from synapse.mcp._tool_registry). Cross-checking the 137 command_types against `register("…")` in handlers.py leaves only the 7 farm commands unmatched, and those are registered dynamically at handlers.py:795-796 via `for command in sorted(FARM_READ_COMMANDS | FARM_CONTROL_COMMANDS)`. Extras named at mcp_server.py:547, 717, 811-812; group dict at :1127-1134. Live reads: get_scene_info 205 bytes, get_stage_info 231 bytes (empty stage), memory_status 844 bytes; synapse_ping and synapse_health both return healthy with houdini_available true, write_plane ok. synapse_doctor: 13 ok / 1 fail / 0 skipped, main_thread not stalled (0 consecutive timeouts, max dispatch wait 1295 ms, max hold 625 ms on memory_loop:owner).

**For the artist.** The foundation is real. Asking about the scene and building networks works and is responsive. The failures above are in the layers on top of it, not in the bridge.

### Refuter verdicts

- **CONFIRMED** — synapse_safe_render's lockup guard is unreachable: the guard reads width/height from the payload but the registered schema exposes only rop_path and soho_foreground, so the `> 512` branch can never fire through MCP.
  - *Evidence:* Reproduced. `python -c` over TOOL_DEFS: `schema props ['rop_path','soho_foreground'] required []`. Guard reads at python/synapse/server/handlers_render.py:2072 (`width = resolve_param_with_default(payload, "width", None)`) and :2073 (height); dead branch at :2212 (`effective_w = int(width) if width else 0`) and :2216 (`if (effective_w > 512 or effective_h > 512) and user_foreground is None`). resolve_param_with_default at python/synapse/core/aliases.py:255-258 returns the default when no alias key is present. Tool tuple starts at python/synapse/mcp/_tool_registry.py:1456 and its description string still reads "Auto-forces background mode for high-resolution renders to prevent Houdini lockup." node.render() is at handlers_render.py:905 inside `_render_on_main`, dispatched at :962-965 via `run_on_main(_render_on_main, timeout=_RENDER_MAIN_TIMEOUT_S)` with `_RENDER_MAIN_TIMEOUT_S = 3600.0` at :138.
- **CONFIRMED** — The panel's chat worker cannot start or stop a render in the shipped default mode. 40 of 137 tools are denied, including every render-initiating and render-stopping tool.
  - *Evidence:* My first attempt at this measurement was WRONG in the surveyor's favour-opposite direction: `is_tool_allowed_for_worker` returns a `(bool, str)` tuple, and truth-testing the tuple made all 137 tools look allowed. Re-measured with `[0]`: standard 97/137, strict 45/137, proposal 45/137, demo 45/137, unrestricted 137/137 — their three numbers reproduce exactly. Denied-in-standard count is 40, and the list contains houdini_render, synapse_safe_render, synapse_render_sequence, synapse_render_progressively, synapse_autonomous_render, synapse_render_stop, synapse_farm_prepare/submit/cancel, synapse_render_farm_cancel, tops_render_sequence, houdini_shot_render_ready — every tool they named. `_DEFAULT_MODE = _MODE_STANDARD` at python/synapse/panel/worker_policy.py:52 and `resolve_mode()` returns 'standard' with no env override set on this machine. Enforced at python/synapse/panel/claude_worker.py:402; filtered from the advertised list at python/synapse/panel/tool_bridge.py:113. Decisively: the INTERACTIVE panel chat constructs the worker with `enforce_worker_policy=True` at python/synapse/panel/synapse_panel.py:3422, with a comment citing "Joe DECIDE 2026-08-18 (ENG-INJ-GATE-OFF): consent posture = ON" — so this is not an autonomous-only path. Secondary claims verified: render_workspace.py:538 `self._call("submit", "synapse_farm_submit", payload)`; synapse_panel.py:2549-2551 wires the Render button to `_open_render_workspace`; commit 1d982426 is dated 2026-09-15 (#85).
- **CORRECTED** — synapse_scout returns a hard error on every call through the live MCP bridge, caused by sqlite3.connect at scout.py:329 cached module-level while mcp_server.py:757-758 dispatches via asyncio.to_thread.
  - *Correction:* The cited MECHANISM is wrong, and wrong in a way this project has been burned by before — it was copied out of a stale DOCSTRING rather than read from the code. mcp_server.py:759-760 is a docstring that says "runs off the event loop via asyncio.to_thread"; the actual dispatch at :767-769 calls `_off_loop`, and mcp_server.py:1027 states outright "NOT asyncio.to_thread — that uses the default executor, which asyncio.run() shuts down permanently under Houdini's persistent loop." The real executor is a module-owned `concurrent.futures.ThreadPoolExecutor(max_workers=4, thread_name_prefix="synapse-mcp-dispatch")` created at mcp_server.py:227-231 and used by `_off_loop` at :233-245. The corrected mechanism makes the bug WORSE, not better: a fixed 4-worker pool guarantees the cached connection will be used from a foreign thread, deterministically, rather than depending on default-executor behaviour.
  - *Evidence:* The FAILURE reproduces exactly. I called `mcp__synapse__synapse_scout` live with query "hou.LopNetwork editableStage": `{"error":"ProgrammingError","message":"SQLite objects created in a thread can only be used in that same thread. The object was created in thread id 42800 and this is thread id 73532."}` — same thread ids they observed, so the poisoned connection is still cached from the same origin thread. Connection created at python/synapse/cognitive/tools/scout.py:329 (`con = sqlite3.connect(str(db_path))`, default check_same_thread=True) and cached at :345 (`_FTS[key] = con`), dict declared at :264.
- **CONFIRMED** — On the /mcp path scout resolves the H21 symbol table, not H22, so its existence verdicts are for the wrong build; in-Houdini the table is correctly H22.
  - *Evidence:* Reproduced outside Houdini: `_running_major() -> ''`, `_running_build() -> ''`, `_pkg_symbol_table_path() -> h21_symbol_table.json`, `_symbol_table_path() -> h21_symbol_table.json`, and that file's own stamp key is `houdini_version = 21.0.671`. `EXPECTED_HOUDINI_VERSION` is None both as env var and as module attribute. Fallback logic at python/synapse/cognitive/tools/scout.py:651-669 (`_pkg_symbol_table_path` / `_symbol_table_path`). The version pin is set only inside a `try: import hou` at mcp_server.py:740-744, which fails in the bridge process. h22_symbol_table.json (1,335,127 bytes, dated Sep 5) sits unused beside h21 in python/synapse/cognitive/tools/data/. Contrast confirmed live: `synapse_doctor` returns `symbol_table` ok — "stamp 22.0.400 (36472 symbols, blake2b e408ca0b3e41762282a911d6a8ac2f62) == running 22.0.400". Hardcoded H21 strings at scout.py:1103, 1106, 1108 (plus :5, :15, :32, :133).
- **CONFIRMED** — No recipe in the 62-recipe registry matches "create a solaris network" in any casing, and every trigger is an anchored ^…$ regex that misses ordinary artist phrasing.
  - *Evidence:* Reproduced against `synapse.routing.recipes.RecipeRegistry` (62 recipes): 'create a solaris network' -> None, 'create a simple solaris network' -> None, 'Create a Solaris Network' -> None, 'build me a solaris network' -> None, 'can you set up three point lighting' -> None, 'make me a three point lighting rig' -> None. Only exact anchored forms hit: 'setup three point lighting' -> three_point_lighting, 'set up a lookdev scene' -> lookdev_scene. The three_point_lighting trigger is verbatim `^(?:set up|setup|create)\s+(?:a\s+)?three[\s-]point\s+light(?:ing)?(?:\s+(?:at|in|under)\s+(?P<parent>.+))?$`. Only two of the 62 recipe names are Solaris/USD-shaped at all: lookdev_scene and solaris_scatter_instances. Recipe tier is python/synapse/routing/router.py:404 `match = self._recipes.match(text)` inside `_try_recipe`.
- **CONFIRMED** — The four "Create a Solaris Network" recipe versions are split across three memory substrates; v4 exists only in a markdown file, v1–v3 only in the Moneta vector store.
  - *Evidence:* Streamed the snapshot (C:/Users/User/AppData/Local/Temp/houdini_temp/untitled/.synapse/.moneta/snapshot.json, top-level {snapshot_version, snapshot_created_at, rows}, 1155 rows) with a classifying script that parses the nested `payload` JSON. The 11 non-telemetry rows contain v1, v2 and v3 verbatim ('DEFAULT SIMPLE SOLARIS NETWORK TEMPLATE', '...v2 (SUPERSEDES v1)', 'CANONICAL "Create a Solaris Network" RECIPE v3 (SUPERSEDES v2)') plus three matching **Decision:** records. No v4. grep across the store tree finds v4 only at C:/Users/User/AppData/Local/Temp/houdini_temp/untitled/claude/memory.md, lines 76, 115, 116, 129 and 160 — 'CANONICAL STUDIO LOOKDEV RIG RECIPE — v4 (SUPERSEDES v3)'. The store's own context.md is 357 bytes and decisions.md 114 bytes, neither mentioning it.
- **CONFIRMED** — synapse_knowledge_lookup returns uncapped raw parameter dumps — 91,373 bytes for the single query "karma xpu" — and this is a documented design choice.
  - *Evidence:* Measured live over ws://127.0.0.1:9999/synapse. My first attempt failed because I sent the query under a `params` key; the wire field is `payload` (python/synapse/core/protocol.py:216-218). With the correct envelope: knowledge_lookup "karma xpu" = 91,353 bytes on the wire, success=True, self-reported `serve_bytes=92829`, `len(parameters)=233`. The 233-parameter figure is exact. Design intent confirmed verbatim at python/synapse/server/handlers.py:1936-1937: "A node answer carries the full ``parameters`` surface (internal names + channels, uncapped - Target 5) and the measured ``serve_bytes``." list_recipes measured 30,155 bytes against their 30,171.
- **CONFIRMED** — The memory store is 99% machine telemetry — of 1,144 rows, 11 are artist-authored and 2 of those are empty strings.
  - *Evidence:* Independently reproduced with my own classifier over the parsed `payload.content`/`payload.summary` fields. Of 1,155 rows today: loop_outcome 533 (46.1%), ai_session 261 (22.6%), executed 198 (17.1%), session_summary 152 (13.2%), OTHER 11 (1.0%). Their figures were 529/256/196/152/11 — every bucket matches within the growth that has occurred since. The store's own `memory_type` field independently agrees: {feedback: 534, note: 268, action: 198, summary: 152, decision: 3}. Only THREE rows in the entire store are typed `decision`. Two of the 11 OTHER rows are empty strings, exactly as claimed.
- **CORRECTED** — The store grows roughly 2 rows per read-only tool call and at the observed rate crosses the 2,000-record migration budget in about two weeks.
  - *Correction:* The mechanism is '~1 row per tool call, more for heavy calls like doctor', not '2 rows per read-only call'. The two-week conclusion survives on their 67-rows/day calendar arithmetic (843 rows of headroom remain at 1,157), but the per-call rate they state is not what the store does, and anyone re-deriving the timeline from their per-call number would get the wrong answer.
  - *Evidence:* The growth is real but the RATE is measured wrong. I ran three consecutive read-only calls and read the count each time: synapse_health -> 1155, synapse_memory_status -> 1156, synapse_health -> 1157. That is +1 per call, cleanly, three times. Their own cited series (1133 -> 1135 -> 1137 -> 1143 -> 1152) contains a +9 jump, which no 'roughly 2 per call' model explains — synapse_doctor is a heavier writer (I watched it take the store from 1157 to 1161). The budget itself is confirmed exactly: python/synapse/host/memory_lifecycle.py:25 `_MAX_RECORDS = 2000`, :26 `_MAX_BYTES = 8 * 1024 * 1024`, raised as `RuntimeError("Memory migration exceeds the bounded record/byte budget")` at :172 and again at :294-296 inside rebind_owner. Save-time carry at :370-372 (`if effective_cause == "save" and previous is not None` / `if previous.unsaved: records = _records(owner)`). The swallow is real: memory_lifecycle.py:112-120 catches the exception, sets `owner._memory_binding_error` and logs, never surfacing to the artist. README.md:117 confirms the prune is opt-in and defaults off. Live doctor reports 537 protected of 1,161 entities.
- **CORRECTED** — For an unsaved scene the memory store lives under Houdini's temp directory, and this store has gone into refuse-writes degraded mode 11 separate times on this machine.
  - *Correction:* "11 separate times" overstates the independence of the events. All 11 quarantine files are byte-identical in size (1,394,474) and share one mtime (Sep 15 15:09). They are 11 quarantine ATTEMPTS against a single corrupted store, retried across Sep 15-17, not 11 distinct corruption incidents. The count is right; the framing implies a recurring-failure rate the evidence does not support. The recovery burden they describe (scripts/primary_repair.py, a CLI no artist would know to run) is unaffected.
  - *Evidence:* Store path confirmed live by both synapse_health and synapse_doctor: C:\Users\User\AppData\Local\Temp\houdini_temp\untitled\.synapse. Derivation confirmed at python/synapse/memory/store.py:1162 (`def hip_is_unsaved`) and :1213 ("(e.g. <cwd>/untitled.hip/.synapse) are NOT carried over"). Degraded-load quarantine confirmed at store.py:452 (`self._degraded_load = True`) and :455 ("DEGRADED LOAD: %s in %s. Refusing writes; recover the source or"). Directory listing shows exactly 11 memory.jsonl.degraded-load-<ts> files spanning 1789506731 to 1789674135, plus memory.jsonl.pre-repair-1789674797 and memory.jsonl.pre-backfill-1789681905. Live `synapse_doctor` confirms `degraded_quarantine_count: 11`.
- **CONFIRMED** — A foreground render cannot be cancelled by any shipped control, because every stop path needs the main thread the render is holding.
  - *Evidence:* node.render() at python/synapse/server/handlers_render.py:905, inside `_render_on_main`, dispatched at :962-965 with `timeout=_RENDER_MAIN_TIMEOUT_S` = 3600.0 (:138). `_handle_render_stop` dispatches `run_on_main(lambda: _rs.stop_render(...), timeout=30.0)` at :1737-1740 — it needs the same main thread. The `_handle_emergency_halt` docstring at :1756-1783 states verbatim, as VERIFIED-RUNTIME on 22.0.368 dated 2026-07-28, that the shipped halt walks /obj ONLY, returned `action="ALL_OPERATIONS_HALTED"` in 0.0 s while the cook was still running 3 s later, and that background renders are deliberately NOT killed. README.md:79 carries the "Three ways to stop" section, cross-referenced at :137 and :141 including the honest line "**Stop** prevents further panel work. It does not prove an active cook or render has finished".
- **CONFIRMED** — The installer shipped for the current release was never install-qualified against a running Houdini, and the version drift detector is currently red on this machine.
  - *Evidence:* `git show aa9624e5 -s` carries the quoted text verbatim: "installer-verification.json is NOT shipped, and v5.74.0 did ship it. That file is a Houdini install qualification -- an actual probe of the installed product against a running Houdini. No such run was performed for this build... it has not been install-qualified, and the notes now say so." Live `synapse_doctor` returns summary {fail: 1, ok: 13, skipped: 0} with the single fail being `version`: "synapse 5.75.1 / protocol 4.0.0; install stamp says 5.67.4 — installed tree and stamp disagree". C:/Users/User/.synapse/install_stamp.json records synapse_version 5.67.4, installed_at 2026-09-09T19:49:38Z, repo_root under OneDrive/.../demo-capture/2026-09-09/installation/SYNAPSE/versions/5.67.4-53b476b278b143f6.
- **CORRECTED** — docs/status.md — linked from the README as "Current status" — describes v5.67.3/v5.67.4 and is eight releases stale. (marked loadBearing:FALSE)
  - *Correction:* Two errors, one of them the exact failure class the brief warns about. (1) Ten releases stale, not eight; README line 143, not 156. (2) Their supporting line "Actual CI is healthy — `gh run list --limit 5` shows master green at 2026-09-17T22:54Z" is an ABSTENTION PRINTED AS A PASS. That green run is against headSha aa9624e5, the PARENT of HEAD. HEAD b7592a9a (2026-09-18 13:40:20 -0400) does not appear in the last 10 CI runs at all — no CI has ever run on the commit under review. I also confirmed their 'one failure earlier that day' (43ef30ca, 2026-09-17T22:05:49Z, master). Finally, this finding is arguably misclassified as loadBearing:false in one direction and correctly in another: status.md does self-hedge at :20-22 ("For subsequent commits, read the current CI run"), which softens it — but it is the page a new user clicks to learn what works.
  - *Evidence:* Staleness confirmed: docs/status.md:9-11 cite "the v5.67.3 reliability release" and "v5.67.4 updates documentation", and :13-14 give "8,614 stock tests passed with 415 explicit skips". VERSION is 5.75.1. Counting tags between them (v5.68.0, v5.69.0, v5.70.0, v5.70.1, v5.71.0, v5.72.0, v5.73.0, v5.74.0, v5.75.0, v5.75.1) the page is TEN releases stale, not eight. The README link is at README.md:143, not :156.
- **CONFIRMED** — docs/studio/DEPLOYMENT.md sells a multi-user studio mode that disables all 7 farm tools and grants every authenticated 'artist' ungated arbitrary Python in the host Houdini.
  - *Evidence:* docs/studio/DEPLOYMENT.md:8-12 documents the mode table with `studio-lan` and `studio-vpn` (Bind 0.0.0.0, Auth Required, RBAC On). python/synapse/server/handlers_farm.py:17-19 defines `_require_local_deployment()` raising ValueError unless SYNAPSE_DEPLOY_MODE == "local", and it is called at :93, :100, :116, :120, :125 and :132 — six call sites covering the farm read and control commands. python/synapse/server/rbac.py:63 places "execute_python", "execute_vex" inside _ARTIST_COMMANDS; ROLE_DENIALS begins at :118 and the 'artist' row at :123-125 denies only server_config / manage_users / reload_config. Enforcement at python/synapse/server/websocket.py:675-679 (`if is_rbac_enabled() and self._session_manager` ... `check_permission`).
- **CONFIRMED** — Tool reachability is not the gap — all 137 registered tools have live handlers; the advertised surface is 147.
  - *Evidence:* `len(TOOL_DEFS) == 137`, `len(PENDING_TOOL_DEFS) == 0`. Cross-checked all 137 command_types against `register("…")` across handlers.py plus handlers_render/node/memory/farm/usd: 132 matched statically, 7 unmatched, and those 7 are exactly ['farm_cancel','farm_capabilities','farm_inspect','farm_job','farm_jobs','farm_prepare','farm_submit'], registered dynamically at python/synapse/server/handlers.py:795-796 via `for command in sorted(FARM_READ_COMMANDS | FARM_CONTROL_COMMANDS): reg.register(command, getattr(self, "_handle_" + command))`. Extras confirmed at mcp_server.py:547 (_INSPECTOR_TOOL_NAME), :717 (_SCOUT_TOOL_NAME), :811-812 (the two BLOCKS names), and the 6-entry group dict at :1127-1134. Live reads are cheap and fast: get_scene_info 188 bytes, get_stage_info 213 bytes, synapse_health returns healthy with houdini_available true and write_plane ok, synapse_doctor 13 ok / 1 fail / 0 skipped with main_thread not stalled (0 consecutive timeouts, max dispatch wait 1295 ms, max hold 625 ms on memory_loop:owner).

### What the surveyor did not look at inside its own dimension

- The install path — their own brief item (e). They never opened the installer, scripts/, or anything under docs/studio/ beyond DEPLOYMENT.md's mode table. The entire install finding rests on one commit message and one doctor check. Nobody, including them, has traced what a fresh user actually executes.
- That `synapse_run_recipe` is not registered at all. `'synapse_run_recipe' in [t[0] for t in TOOL_DEFS]` is False; only `synapse_list_recipes` exists. Their recipe finding blames trigger regexes, but through MCP an artist can enumerate 62 recipes and execute zero of them — a structurally larger hole than the phrasing problem they describe.
- That `synapse_emergency_halt` is on the panel worker's standard-mode denial list. They built a whole finding on 'nothing can cancel a render' without noticing that the halt control is also unreachable from chat. It is in the 40 they counted but not in the 12 they named.
- That six Solaris composite builders (component_builder, scene_template, create_variants, set_purpose, import_megascans, shotsetup_karma_xpu) are denied in standard mode, which directly contradicts their own ~80-usable-tools estimate crediting 'Solaris build tools'.
- A dual-write divergence sitting in plain sight in the health payload they quoted: MonetaBackedStore reports count 1161 while MonetaBackedStore._jsonl_net reports records 1165 — a persistent 4-record gap between the serving store and its JSONL shadow. They quoted this object repeatedly and never asked why the two numbers differ.
- That MonetaBackedStore answers `answered: false` on its own write-health contract — 'exposes no health() ... so its write state is unknown, not healthy'. The store they call 'the bright spot' cannot self-report whether it is accepting writes; only its JSONL shadow can.
- The 154 branches and 11 worktrees given to them as grounding facts. They never checked whether an artist-facing fix for any of these five paths is already written and stranded unmerged — which would change every remediation estimate in the report.
- The panel's Ollama path. They invoke it in finding 7 ('the README advertises Ollama as a first-class option') to argue the 91KB payload would overflow a local model, but never verified that the Ollama provider is wired or what its context window actually is.
- Whether the panel has its own in-Houdini scout call site. They flag this themselves as UNKNOWN, which is honest, but it is the difference between 'scout is down on one path' and 'scout is down everywhere' — and it is the single cheapest open question on their list.
- The `_off_loop` / persistent-event-loop machinery at mcp_server.py:200-245, which documents a probed Houdini-specific executor-shutdown defect ('run #2: FAIL RuntimeError: Executor shutdown has been called'). They read past it while citing the very function it defines, and it is the kind of process-global fragility that decides whether a production day survives.

### Open questions

- What percentage survives a production day: my estimate is roughly 60% of the 137-tool surface. Scene inspection, USD/stage reads, node creation, material and Solaris build tools, memory writes and TOPs inspection are genuinely usable — call it 80 tools. Render (12 tools) is chat-unreachable and UI-only via a 3-day-old dialog. Knowledge/scout (2 tools) are down or unusable. Farm (7) needs local mode plus a saved hip. COPs (22) are honestly labelled in their own group preamble as scaffolds that configure without cooking. This is a reasoned estimate from measured denial counts and live probes, not a measured pass rate — I did not run the suite, per instructions.
- Does the panel's in-Houdini scout path (if one exists separate from mcp_server's) hit the same SQLite cross-thread bug? I verified the /mcp path fails and that the in-Houdini symbol table resolves correctly to H22, but I did not trace a panel-side scout call site. UNKNOWN.
- Does the Render workspace (#85, landed 2026-09-15) actually complete a render on this build? I did not exercise it — it mutates. Its dependency chain is farm_submit → _require_local_deployment + a saved hip, and the scene is currently unsaved, so an artist in the state this machine is in right now could not use it.
- What does a genuinely clean machine hit? install_stamp.json on this box points at a OneDrive demo-capture tree, so the local doctor 'version' fail may be a dev artifact rather than the fresh-user experience. With no installer-verification.json for 5.75.1, nobody knows either way.
- Is the 2-rows-per-read-only-call memory growth intentional telemetry or a leak? I measured the rate but did not trace which writer emits 'AI session started' on a plain read. If a session note is written per MCP connection, the rate scales with client count, not artist activity.

## docs-truth — MIXED

> README.md itself is the most honest document in the repo and every number in it verifies against a producer — but the gates stop at the front door, so the first page past it still ships the pre-fix v5.74.0 installer and the "Current status" page is eight releases stale.

**Trajectory.** Strongly improving, and improving fast. 65 commits touched README.md since 2026-08-07 (`git log --since=2026-08-07 --oneline -- README.md | wc -l` → 65). There is a visible doc-truth guard lineage: 957a17be "release truth - VERSION canonical, six-surface sync, refuse-dirty-tag gate" → 2d2bad15 "reach P1-R2 public-face agreement - README vs release tags gate" → b1ec9a8d "the loop reads the tag, not the branch - release_ci_gate.py" → 53e4f9cb "release: v5.75.1 -- the tag that was checked". The v5.75.1 release notes (docs/releases/v5.75.1.md:9-23) are a model of the convention: they name their own prior release's four defects, including "a published figure that no CI leg produced". The regression is not in the README — it is that each fix was applied by hand to the one file that was wrong, and the gate was never widened, so drift has migrated one hop downstream into docs/getting-started/ and docs/status.md where nothing checks it.

**The refuter on where this report overstates itself.** Yes, in three specific ways — though the report is unusually good and most of its evidence survived contact.

1. The headline is the main offender: "the gates stop at the front door" is a clean story that the evidence does not support. The real shape is a patchwork, not a perimeter. Bound by conformance tests: docs/studio/EGRESS.md (tests/test_m3_egress_docs.py — a new remote-egress call site fails CI until the doc is updated), docs/studio/UPGRADE.md (test_m3_upgrade_surface.py), DEPLOYMENT.md's env-var table (test_m3_env_conformance.py:38,:151), README's tool count and version banner. Bound by nothing at all: SECURITY.md, docs/getting-started/installation.md, docs/status.md (all three verified by grep across tests/, harness/verify/ and scripts/). The front door is not uniformly gated and the rooms beyond it are not uniformly ungated. Their own top-ranked fix — add installation.md to sync_version SURFACES — only makes sense once you see the patchwork, so the headline actively obscures their best recommendation.

2. "every number in it verifies against a producer" overstates what verification bought. Exactly one README number has a test that fails on drift (137 tools). The rest verify because I could reproduce them today. The download button was also reproducible-today right up until v5.75.0 shipped it stale. Treating reproducible-now as equivalent to guarded is the precise error the v5.75.1 notes were written to name.

3. Finding 4's significance paragraph is more confident than its evidence and points at the wrong target. "The only check that compares the README claim against git tags" is false — tag_not_ahead_of_banner (version_agreement.py:199-218) does exactly that, live and working. Meanwhile the thing they should have been confident about went unmentioned: the script exits 0 while printing FAIL, and grep finds zero references to it under .github/. They found a cosmetic abstention and missed the structural one in the same file.

Status "mixed" is defensible and I would keep it. Severity assignment is where I'd push back hardest: F9 (LOPCAT) is filed "watch" with an artistImpact that is backwards — the loader ignores the build stamp entirely (lop_knowledge.py validates only schema at :105 and blake2b at :112), so a stale Solaris answer ships silently rather than being refused. With its test passing 9/9 on synthetic fixtures and the verifier exiting 0, that is blocker-shaped, and it is the single finding in the report whose severity is wrong in the dangerous direction.

### Findings

#### [BLOCKER · load-bearing] The 'Full setup guide' linked from README:32 and from docs/index.md:10 sends artists to download v5.74.0 — the release BEFORE the memory-store fix README:113 headlines. The link resolves; it does not 404. It silently delivers the pre-fix installer.

**Evidence.** docs/getting-started/installation.md:10 `**[Download SYNAPSE-5.74.0-Setup.exe](https://github.com/JosephOIbrahim/Synapse/releases/download/v5.74.0/SYNAPSE-5.74.0-Setup.exe)**`; also :12 (release-notes link, tag v5.74.0) and :13 (SHA256SUMS at v5.74.0). README.md:32 links to this file; docs/index.md:10 calls it 'Get SYNAPSE into Houdini'. `gh release view v5.74.0 --json assets` → SYNAPSE-5.74.0-Setup.exe state=uploaded, i.e. the stale link works. README.md:13 correctly points at v5.75.1, and `gh release view v5.75.1` confirms that asset exists.

**For the artist.** An artist who clicks 'Full setup guide' rather than the README button installs the build whose memory store 'refused every write for two days while every health surface reported OK' (README.md:113). The checksum link on that page also verifies the wrong file.

#### [BLOCKER · load-bearing] docs/studio/DEPLOYMENT.md documents the full multi-user studio path — studio-lan/studio-vpn binding 0.0.0.0, firewall rules, TLS, role matrix — with zero mention that `execute_python`/`execute_vex` run ungated on the live path. README:188 and SECURITY.md:19 both state a handler-layer gate is a prerequisite that is 'tracked, not shipped'. The three documents disagree about whether studio deployment is a supported posture.

**Evidence.** `grep -n -i 'execute_python|ungated|consent|not shipped|prerequisite|single-user' docs/studio/DEPLOYMENT.md` returns no caveat (only line 117 `SYNAPSE_COOK_SANDWICH`, line 382 about a monetary budget). DEPLOYMENT.md:6-12 presents studio-lan/studio-vpn as modes; :281-291 gives firewall-open commands; :272-279 gives the role matrix. RBAC does gate `viewer` (python/synapse/server/rbac.py:119-122 ROLE_DENIALS) but the ordinary `artist` role is explicitly permitted execute_python (rbac.py:61-63, :103-110) — so any authenticated artist key on the LAN gets arbitrary Python in another seat's Houdini with no consent prompt. Contrast SECURITY.md:19 and README.md:188.

**For the artist.** A studio TD following DEPLOYMENT.md opens port 9999 on 0.0.0.0 believing RBAC is the boundary. It is not the boundary for the artist role, which is the default working role.

#### [BLOCKER · load-bearing] docs/status.md — titled 'Current status and limits' and linked from README:143 as '[Current status]' — was last touched at v5.67.4 and describes v5.67.3 as 'the latest changes'. That is eight releases and nine days behind VERSION 5.75.1. No test binds it.

**Evidence.** `git log -1 --format='%ad %h %s' --date=short -- docs/status.md` → `2026-09-09 d7c90a15 docs: refresh artist guide and diagrams for v5.67.4`. docs/status.md:8-12 'What the latest changes cover … The v5.67.3 reliability release … v5.67.4 updates documentation'. `grep -rn 'status.md' tests/ harness/verify/` → no matches. VERSION=5.75.1.

**For the artist.** The page an artist is pointed to for 'what is shipped and what needs checking' predates the farm/TOPs work (v5.71.0), the tool-name rename (v5.70.0) and the entire memory-store incident. It also links a 'repair branch' (:39) as excluded work that has since moved.

#### [WATCH · load-bearing] The REACH public-face gate that would catch a stale 'Latest' claim is dead on the current README text. `_README_TAG_CLAIM_RE` is case-sensitive for `is latest`; README writes `is Latest`. The check reports `ok` with reason 'nothing to pin' — an abstention printed as a PASS.

**Evidence.** harness/verify/version_agreement.py:75 `_README_TAG_CLAIM_RE = re.compile(r'tags:\s*v([0-9]+\.[0-9]+\.[0-9]+)\s+is\s+latest')` (no re.I). README.md:7 `tags: v5.75.1 is Latest`. Running `python harness/verify/version_agreement.py` prints: `PUBLIC  ok  tag_claim_current  (no 'tags: vX is latest' claim in README - nothing to pin)` and `tag_claim=None`. Proved directly: case-sensitive match → None, case-insensitive → matched span (75,98).

**For the artist.** none — but this is the only check that compares the README claim against git tags rather than against the VERSION file, so the outside-world half of release truth is currently unguarded while reporting green.

#### [WATCH · load-bearing] Nothing guards the README download/checksum URL version. The v5.75.1 notes name exactly this as a v5.75.0 defect ('the README download button still pointed at v5.74.0's asset'); it was fixed by hand and no guard was added, so it can recur silently.

**Evidence.** `grep -rn 'Setup.exe|SHA256SUMS|releases/download' tests/*.py scripts/*.py harness/verify/*.py` → no matches; `grep -rln 'Setup.exe' tests/ scripts/ harness/verify/` → nothing. scripts/sync_version.py:41-51 SURFACES covers six locations and matches README only via `<sub>v(X.Y.Z) · Houdini` — the banner, not the links. docs/releases/v5.75.1.md:18-19 records the defect.

**For the artist.** The download button is correct today only because someone remembered. The next release can ship a wrong button with a full green gate.

#### [WATCH · load-bearing] SECURITY.md's vendored-dependency list — the scope of a published 'ours to patch and release' commitment — omits 6 of the 16 packages actually vendored.

**Evidence.** SECURITY.md:25 lists anthropic · pydantic · pydantic_core · httpx · httpcore · h11 · anyio · certifi · idna · jiter (10). `ls python/synapse/_vendor/` plus a set-diff script gives OMITTED: annotated_types, distro, docstring_parser, sniffio, typing_extensions, typing_inspection. Nothing listed is absent.

**For the artist.** none directly — but a studio security review reading SECURITY.md:27 ('A vulnerability in any of these, as shipped here, is ours to patch') gets an under-scoped inventory of what SYNAPSE actually ships.

#### [WATCH] CLAUDE.md's 'Current Status' table gives line counts that are wrong for every row, the largest by 4.3x — and the table contradicts CLAUDE.md's own §1.7, which cites a line number the table says cannot exist.

**Evidence.** CLAUDE.md Current Status claims `shared/bridge.py` ~700, `shared/evolution.py` ~600, `shared/router.py` 271, `shared/types.py` 250, `python/synapse/memory/agent_state.py` ~688. `wc -l` → 3012, 778, 320, 348, 880. CLAUDE.md §1.7 cites `shared/bridge.py:1718` for the `dirtyAllTasks` call and `:1723` for the catch; `grep -n 'dirtyAllTasks' shared/bridge.py` puts them at :2377 and :2382, and line 1718 is an unrelated undo-anchor warning.

**For the artist.** none — internal. But CLAUDE.md is the agent-facing contract, and an agent told to inspect bridge.py:1718 for the PDG rollback bug reads the wrong code.

#### [WATCH · load-bearing] No public document cites the test figure the repo's own baseline declares is the only citable one. harness/verify/suite_baseline.json says the shipping (hython) leg is 'the ONLY number a release claim may cite' and records it as 119 failed / 5 errors; docs/status.md publishes only the green stock-Python figure.

**Evidence.** harness/verify/suite_baseline.json `shipping/_what`: 'What artists actually run … The ONLY number a release claim may cite … Honestly BAD and recorded as measured'; `shipping/passed=6193, failed=119, skipped=191, errors=5`; `shipping/interpreter` = hython 3.13.10 on Houdini 22.0.368. docs/status.md:14-16 publishes '8,614 stock tests passed with 415 explicit skips' plus '433 focused native checks'. Caveat, stated honestly: that baseline is `measured=2026-08-15` at commit b2412b6b on 22.0.368, not a current measurement, and status.md's figures come from the v5.67.3 release record — they are different measurements, not the same number disagreeing.

**For the artist.** The artist-facing status page reports only the interpreter artists do not run. The interpreter they do run has a recorded non-green figure that appears nowhere public.

#### [WATCH · load-bearing] The repo's own verifier reports a live FAIL on build-stamp agreement, and no public doc mentions it — while docs/status.md:59-63 publishes 'Houdini symbol/node references are build-aware' as a capability.

**Evidence.** `python harness/verify/version_agreement.py` prints `LOPCAT  FAIL  (catalog lop_solaris_knowledge_22.json is stamped 22.0.368 but the introspected runtime authority h22_symbol_table.json is stamped 22.0.400)`. The same run prints GRAPH PASS at build=22.0.417. docs/studio/UPGRADE.md:1-11 documents that a build-stamp mismatch disarms the phantom gate (default policy `refuse`, fail-closed).

**For the artist.** Solaris/LOP node-catalogue lookups are stamped one build behind the runtime authority. Per UPGRADE.md the fail-closed default means refusal rather than a wrong answer — so the artist-visible symptom is a scout that says no, not a phantom that ships.

### What the surveyor did not look at inside its own dimension

- The abstention-as-pass they were hunting for was one line away and they walked past it. `python harness/verify/version_agreement.py >/dev/null 2>&1; echo $?` → exit_code=0 while the same run prints `LOPCAT FAIL`. And `grep -rn 'version_agreement' .github/` → zero hits: the script is never invoked in CI at all. They wrote a whole finding about a case-sensitive regex inside a script that cannot fail a build under any circumstances.
- The check-that-cannot-fail, in the same area. `python -m pytest tests/test_lop_catalog_stamp_agreement.py -q` → 9 passed in 0.40s, while the real repo is in the LOPCAT FAIL state. Every test constructs a synthetic tmp_path tree and calls verifier.lop_catalog_agreement(repo_root=root); nothing asserts against the actual repo artifacts. Textbook isolated-green hiding a composed red — a failure class this project's own memory index names explicitly.
- The doc-binding map, which is the real deliverable their dimension was asking for and which would have restructured their ranked-edit list. Which public docs have a conformance test: EGRESS.md, UPGRADE.md, DEPLOYMENT.md (env table only), README (tool count + banner). Which have none: SECURITY.md, installation.md, status.md. EGRESS.md is the working model — tests/test_m3_egress_docs.py:40 'A new remote-egress site fails here until EGRESS.md is updated' — and it is the pattern every one of their eight ranked fixes is reaching for without naming.
- docs/studio/EGRESS.md itself — a security/capability doc naming three remote endpoints (api.anthropic.com, generativelanguage.googleapis.com, integrate.api.nvidia.com) and answering 'what leaves the workstation.' Squarely inside 'top-level claims in docs/, especially docs/studio/ and anything describing install, security, or capability.' Never assessed. Same for DIAGNOSTICS.md, DROP_DAY.md and STUDIO_SPRINT.md; they only opened DEPLOYMENT.md and UPGRADE.md out of six files in that directory.
- Reachability, which would have re-ranked their fixes. `grep -rn 'studio/' README.md docs/index.md SECURITY.md docs/getting-started/*.md docs/status.md` returns exactly two hits, both pointing at UPGRADE.md (docs/index.md:21, installation.md:129). Nothing in the artist- or TD-facing path links DEPLOYMENT.md, EGRESS.md or STUDIO_SPRINT.md. Their fix #2 (caveat DEPLOYMENT.md) addresses an orphaned page; fix #1 (installation.md) addresses the page README hands every artist. The gap between those two is much wider than their ranking suggests.
- They never tested the 'fixed' claims in README:113-117 against anything live — the exact failure class the task names ('a "fixed" claim where only the doc changed'). I did: the live Moneta snapshot holds 1153 rows with 1153 unique entity_ids and 0 duplicate ids. The duplicate-deposit degradation is not present in the running store. That is a positive the report should have carried, and it required one filtered script over the snapshot.
- status.md's 8,614 matches neither leg of the repo's own baseline (gate: 6389 passed / 0 failed on python 3.14.2; shipping: 6193 passed / 119 failed on hython). They framed this as 'different measurements, not the same number disagreeing' and stopped there. The sharper finding is that the repo's headline artist-facing test figure has no reproducible producer anywhere I could locate — a direct violation of the Law-2 convention they were auditing against. Resolving which collection scope yields 8,614 is UNKNOWN; it needs a suite run the task forbids.
- suite_baseline.json's shipping.context_note, which they omitted: the 5 errors are collection failures from test-only deps absent in the vendored hython env (websockets — test_passthrough_hygiene, test_port_wave_scene1, test_websocket_cancel_*). Their recommendation #8 is to publish the hython figure; publishing '119 failed / 5 errors' without that note would itself be a misleading public claim.
- README's 'New in 5.75.1' block (:113-119) is five prose capability assertions with no producer path — 'the second write no longer happens, and a collision guard sits behind it', 'installing this release does not repair a store that is already degraded'. They graded README's numbers and left its prose claims entirely ungraded, even though those are the claims an artist actually acts on. Related: README:111's 'shipped since 5.43.0' sources to CHANGELOG.md:7, which sits under '## Unreleased' — a shipped claim citing an unreleased changelog section. (The producer command itself does reproduce: `git tag --contains 87927076 -l 'v*' | sort -V | head -1` → v5.43.0.)
- The report never states what the ~40k CLAUDE.md size limit actually costs. The dimension brief asked whether trimming left it accurate and internally consistent; they found two internal contradictions (line counts, §9) but never asked what was trimmed away or whether a pointer now dangles — e.g. whether the §15/§13/§14 'this section was removed, see git log' stubs still resolve to anything a reader can follow.

### Open questions

- Ranked edits to make the surface honest — 1) Point docs/getting-started/installation.md:10,:12,:13 at v5.75.1 and add installation.md to scripts/sync_version.py SURFACES so it cannot drift again. 2) Add the ungated-execute_python caveat to docs/studio/DEPLOYMENT.md at the Deployment Modes table (:6) and again above Firewall Rules (:281), in SECURITY.md:19's words. 3) Either refresh docs/status.md to 5.75.1 or retitle it with the build it describes and bind it to a test. 4) Make harness/verify/version_agreement.py:75 case-insensitive (or accept Latest/Preview/RC like tests/test_phase0c_doc1_toolcount.py:172 already does) so tag_claim_current stops abstaining green. 5) Pin the README download and SHA256SUMS URLs to VERSION in sync_version.py. 6) Complete SECURITY.md:25's vendored list to all 16 packages. 7) Regenerate CLAUDE.md's Current Status line counts and §1.7's bridge.py line citations, and reconcile §9 against the Phase 5/Phase 6 rows. 8) Publish the hython-leg figure alongside the stock figure wherever a test count appears, per the baseline file's own rule.
- UNKNOWN: whether docs/studio/DEPLOYMENT.md's studio-lan/studio-vpn path has ever been exercised end-to-end, or is aspirational text from the STUDIO_SPRINT.md instruction doc that was never revalidated. I verified the code exists (rbac.py, auth.py, websocket.py origin validation) but found no receipt, test or ledger entry attesting a real two-seat deployment. That changes how urgently finding 2 needs fixing versus how urgently the whole doc needs a 'not qualified' banner.
- UNKNOWN: whether harness/verify/suite_baseline.json's shipping leg (119 failed, measured 2026-08-15 on 22.0.368) still reflects HEAD. I did not re-run it — the task forbids the full suite and Houdini is live. Until someone re-measures on 22.0.400, nobody can say whether the artist-facing interpreter is better or worse than 119 failures, which means no public doc can honestly cite a shipping number at all right now.
- UNKNOWN: whether the LOPCAT FAIL (catalog 22.0.368 vs symbol table 22.0.400) is a known-and-accepted state or an unnoticed regression. `git log` on the catalog would answer it; I ran out of breadth budget before going deep there.
- Worth a decision, not a finding: 318 tracked .md files under docs/, most of them dated harness blueprints, battleplans and sprint instructions, are published on a public remote. They are historical and mostly say so, but STUDIO_SPRINT.md in particular reads as a capability plan in a directory an artist is told to read for deployment.
